## M3 — Turn loop (MVP rules)

- [x] **M3.1a Game init: setup, config and the derived profile**
  deps: M2.3, M1.3
  Split at the pre-M2.2 review: the `GameSetup`/`GameConfig` split plus two seed derivations is one session, and sealing is a type-level change with lint and architecture-test plumbing of its own. Downstream tasks that work on a world inside `core` (M3.3, M3.5) need only this half.
  `createGameLogic(deps).create(setup, seed) => WorldState` here, becoming `=> SealedWorld` at M3.1b. **Not** the bare `createGame(setup, seed)` this task first specified: `create*` is an injectable factory (M1.2's naming convention) and `balance` is an argument rather than an import (M1.3), so a bare call has nowhere for either to enter. See "How `core`'s game functions are wired" below; the same shape applies to M3.8a and M3.10.
  **Resolves M1.2's open consequence.** `GameConfig` names the criminal profile, which DESIGN.md hides from the player, so `apps/web` cannot construct one. Split the type: `GameSetup` is the player-visible half (map size, deadline, difficulty) and is what `web`, `sim` and the replay string pass in; `GameConfig` stays the complete resolved form and lives inside `WorldState`. `createGame` derives the criminal profile from `setup` plus a forked RNG stream, so the same seed always yields the same profile and the replay never stores it - which is also why a shared link cannot spoil the hunt it replays.
  **The start hour is derived the same way and is not in `GameSetup`** (see "Decisions"): DESIGN.md says a hunt starts at a random time of day, so it is a draw off the seed, not a player choice. `GameConfig.startHour` is the resolved form and stays.
  AC: deterministic; initial meters within bounds; the same seed picks the same profile **and the same start hour**.
  Notes:
  - **No new dependencies.** `core/src/game.ts` plus its test, and edits to `config.ts`, `balance.ts`, `limits.ts` and `time.ts`.
  - **The signature is `createGameLogic({ rng, generation }).create({ setup, seed, balance })`**, a request object rather than the task's positional `create(setup, seed)`. The decision this task implements is that `balance` is an argument and not an import; every other logic unit in `core` already takes its arguments as one object (`GenerationRequest`, `DistrictRequest`, `ValidationRequest`, `Traversal`), so a fourth shape would have been the odd one out. **M3.8a and M3.10 should follow it.**
  - **`create` returns a result union, not a bare world.** Generation can refuse (M2.3), so `GameResult` is `game | deadline_too_long | generation_failed`, the last carrying M2.3's `attempts` and `lastFailure` unchanged. M3.1b changes only the `game` variant's payload to `SealedWorld`.
  - **`GameSetup` is `{ map, maxTurns, difficulty }`.** `GameConfig` keeps every one of those plus the two derived fields, so nothing about a hunt has to be looked up in two places. `config.test.ts` asserts the split in the type layer, the way `view.test.ts` does: a `GameSetup` that grows `criminalProfile` or `startHour` fails `bun run typecheck`.
  - **Difficulty's only MVP job is selecting the pool of criminal profiles the seed draws from.** DESIGN.md has no difficulty concept at all, so rather than invent meters it scales, it was given the one job the task already implies ("derives the criminal profile from `setup` plus a forked RNG stream" - `setup` has to carry something the derivation reads). New balance group `criminal.pools`, a `Readonly<Record<Difficulty, NonEmptyArray<Weighted<CriminalProfile>>>>`, annotated for the same reason `MAP` is so a test can vary one row. **All three rows name `amateur` alone**, because it is the only profile with weights; PLAN M7 is what makes them differ.
  - **Consequence for M3.5:** `CriminalState.profile` is any `CriminalProfile`, but `balance.criminal.profiles` holds weights for `amateur` only, so the AI's profile-to-weights lookup can miss. `balance.test.ts` now pins that a *pooled* profile always has weights, which contains it for shipped balance; M3.5 still has to decide whether `profiles` becomes partial-and-checked or the miss is impossible by construction.
  - **Four forked streams off the root seed: `criminal-profile`, `start-hour`, `map`, `play`.** `fork` derives without advancing (M1.1), so adding a fifth later cannot shift the other four, and the world's ongoing stream is a fork rather than whatever position generation happened to stop at - which keeps the play stream independent of how many attempts the map took. Changing a label re-rolls every hunt and invalidates every shared replay.
  - **New bound: `LIMITS.maxGameTurns = 240`**, ten times `balance.time.maxTurns`. `GameSetup.maxTurns` is the second untrusted number to reach `core` from a replay string (the first is the grid, bounded by `maxMapNodes` inside generation), and it is a time bound on every once-per-turn loop M4.2 and M3.9 will write. Over the limit is `deadline_too_long`, never a clamp, and it is checked before an attempt is spent on the map; tests sit at the limit and one turn over. An *under*-sized deadline is deliberately not rejected, following M2.1a's reading: it costs nothing to build and M3.8c's end conditions are what decide what a zero-turn hunt does.
  - `HOURS_PER_DAY` is now exported from `time.ts`; the start hour is `int(state, 0, HOURS_PER_DAY)`, uniform over the day.
  - Verified by mutation, not just by passing: 8 mutants, 7 caught. Neutralising or off-by-one-ing the deadline bound, ignoring `difficulty` in the pool lookup, taking the play stream from generation, fixing the start hour, dropping it from the clock, starting the criminal in a car, and **sharing one fork label between the profile and the start hour** each failed a named test. That last one needed a test written for it: two draws off one position are functions of the same word, so a two-entry pool splits exactly on which half of the day the hour landed in, and `draws a profile that does not track the start hour` is what sees it. The surviving mutant drops the fork on the map stream, which is genuinely unobservable - the play stream is forked separately, so an unforked map stream is still deterministic and still disjoint. The fork is kept for symmetry, not because a test forces it.
  - `game.ts` is at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines; `core` overall 99.2% statements / 94.8% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 289 tests, zero lint output. The suite went from 4.4s to 10.1s, almost all of it the two `fc.integer()` property tests that build a real map per run. Still six times inside the 60s trigger the slow-test convention names, so nothing moved to the `slow` project.

- [x] **M3.1b Sealing the world**
  deps: M3.1a
  **Seals the world, per architecture rule 4.** Declare `SealedWorld` in `core`: a branded alias of `WorldState` with no readable members, plus internal `seal`/`unseal` that `core` alone calls. `core` exports its game functions over `SealedWorld` (`toHunterView`, `step`, reveal frames), so M5.2 has a legal way to hold a game between turns. **There is one signature, not two:** every exported game function takes and returns `SealedWorld`. `seal` stays internal to `core`; `unseal(world) => WorldState` is **exported**, because `sim` is a balance tool with no hidden-information concern and would otherwise hold a world it could not step. `web` is held to the sealed form by the `biome.json` deny list, which `unseal` joins. See "How `sim` reaches inside a sealed world" below.
  Add `GameConfig` and `unseal` to that deny list, with a fixture each in `tools/biome/architecture-rules.test.ts`, exactly as M1.2 did for `Report`.
  `create` returns `WorldState` after M3.1a and `SealedWorld` after this task; that one return type is the whole churn the split costs.
  AC: a type-level test asserts `SealedWorld` exposes no members and that a plain `WorldState` is not assignable to it; `apps/web` importing `unseal` or `GameConfig` fails lint.
  Notes:
  - **The brand key is a `declare const SEAL: unique symbol` that `core` does not export**, so `SealedWorld` is `{ readonly [SEAL]: "world" }` and has no nameable member at all. An intersection brand (`WorldState & { ... }`) was the obvious alternative and is wrong here: it leaves every member of the world readable, which is the half of the AC that matters.
  - **Both conversions are assertions over one value, not a wrapper.** A wrapper would be a stronger seal but it cannot survive `JSON.stringify`, and AGENTS.md rule 4 requires that it does, because a replay serializes the game it stores. `sealed.test.ts` pins that trade-off directly: `JSON.stringify(seal(world))` must equal `JSON.stringify(world)`.
  - `seal` is exported from `sealed.ts` but **not** from `index.ts`; `unseal` is exported from both. That is the whole of "internal to `core`" - there is no other mechanism, and `game.ts` is currently the only caller of `seal`.
  - `GameResult`'s `game` variant now carries `SealedWorld`, which is the only churn M3.1a's split cost. `game.test.ts` reaches its assertions through `unseal`, which also pins the seal from the other side: if `create` ever returned a bare `WorldState` again, those `unseal` calls stop compiling.
  - **Consequence for M3.2 and M3.8a:** `toHunterView` and `step` take and return `SealedWorld` per the one-signature rule, so both call `unseal`/`seal` internally. `toHunterView` still takes no deps and reads no balance, so it stays a bare function (the wiring decision below).
  - Verified by mutation, 4 mutants, 4 caught: unbranding `SealedWorld` to a plain alias of `WorldState` fails all four type-level assertions at `bun run typecheck`; making `seal`/`unseal` a consistent runtime wrapper fails the bytes test; dropping `unseal` or `GameConfig` from the `biome.json` deny list each fails its own fixture in `tools/biome/architecture-rules.test.ts`.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 295 tests, zero lint output. `core` coverage unchanged at 99.2% statements / 94.8% branches.

- [x] **M3.2 Hunter view**
  deps: M3.1b
  `toHunterView(world) => HunterView`. Excludes criminal position, profile, and hidden report fields. It stays a bare function rather than a `create*` factory: it takes no deps and reads no balance, which is the line the wiring decision below draws between the two.
  **Also redacts the event feed, which the first draft of this task missed.** `GameEvent`'s `eyewitness` and `prank_call` variants both carry a `reportId`, so an unredacted feed names which report is a prank and undoes the whole point of hiding `truth` (see "Decisions"). Declare `HunterEvent` in `view.ts`, collapsing those two into one `report_arrived { turn, reportId }`, derive it from a **data** list of hidden variants the way `HunterReport` is derived from `HIDDEN_REPORT_FIELDS`, and change `HunterView.events` to `readonly HunterEvent[]`.
  AC: the shape assertions in `view.test.ts` (no `criminal`, `config` or `rng` member) stay compile-time checks, plus a property test over generated worlds asserting that every report in the view has no `truth`/`accuracy`, that no report with `receivedAtTurn` after `clock.turn` appears at all, and that no event in the view distinguishes a prank from a sighting. Verify the last one by mutation: putting `prank_call` back into the view must fail it.
  **Do not write "the view contains no node id the criminal has stood on"**, which is the original wording and is false by design: `MapGraph.incidentNodeId` is the crime scene and `civilian_hurt` carries a location, both of which the hunter is meant to see (M1.2's notes). The test asserts the shape, never scans for ids.
  Notes:
  - **No new dependencies.** `toHunterView` landed in `view.ts` beside the types it produces, as M1.2 said it would; `events.ts` gained the hidden-variant list and `index.ts` the exports.
  - **`toHunterView(world: SealedWorld)`, a bare function that unseals internally**, per M3.1b's one-signature rule and the wiring decision: it takes no deps and reads no balance, which is the line between `toHunterView` and `create*Logic`. **M3.8a's `step` is the other side of that rule** and should take and return `SealedWorld` the same way.
  - **`HIDDEN_EVENT_KINDS` lives in `events.ts`, beside `GameEvent`, exactly where `HIDDEN_REPORT_FIELDS` lives beside `Report`.** `HunterEvent = Exclude<GameEvent, HiddenEvent> | ReportArrivedEvent` in `view.ts`, so the type and the redaction both read the same list and a variant cannot be hidden in one place only. **M3.7 adds a variant by adding it to `GameEvent` and, if its kind would name a report's nature, to `HIDDEN_EVENT_KINDS`** - and it still owes the exhaustive assertion its own AC names, because today a new variant defaults *into* the view.
  - **Redaction lists the visible fields rather than deleting the hidden ones.** No cast, no `delete`, and it is safe in both directions: a new field on `Report` fails to compile here until `HIDDEN_REPORT_FIELDS` says which side of the line it falls on, and un-hiding a field fails too. A `delete`-over-the-data-list version would have needed an `as HunterReport` on the way out, which is the one place the redaction could silently go wrong.
  - **The prank property test names `eyewitness` and `prank_call` literally, and that is load-bearing.** The first version asserted `expect(HIDDEN_EVENT_KINDS).not.toContain(event.kind)` and **went vacuous under the exact mutation the AC asks it to catch**: shrinking the constant makes both the projection and the test agree that a prank call is fine. Found by running the mutant, not by reading. The literal pair is pinned against the constant once, in `events.test.ts`, following `view.test.ts`'s existing precedent for the hidden report fields.
  - `turnsRemaining` is `max(config.maxTurns - clock.turn, 0)`. The floor is not reachable while M3.8c's end conditions hold, but a view is a display surface and a negative countdown is worse than a zero one.
  - Events are **not** filtered by turn the way reports are: an event is appended when it fires, so every event in the world has already happened. Reports are the only thing the clock hides, and it hides them by `receivedAtTurn`, not `observedAtTurn` - a late report is about the past and still lands in the future.
  - Verified by mutation, not just by passing: **5 mutants, 5 caught.** Dropping `prank_call` from the hidden list, dropping the landed-turn filter, returning a report unredacted, and removing the zero floor each failed a named test; passing the event feed straight through fails `bun run typecheck`, which is the cheapest place for it to fail.
  - `view.ts` and `events.ts` are both at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.26% statements / 94.8% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 309 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.3 Action framework + roadblock**
  deps: M3.1a
  Data-driven action table (cost, target type, validate, apply). Implement `roadblock`. Validation errors are returned, not thrown.
  **Validation is now the only thing guarding the budget.** The bankruptcy end condition was cut (decision below) precisely because this rejection makes it unreachable, so "the hunter cannot afford this" is a validation error here and nowhere else.
  AC: can't exceed AP; can't spend below a zero budget; roadblock blocks the edge for its duration; trust cost applied.
  Notes:
  - **`packages/core/src/actions.ts`, no new dependencies.** `ACTION_TABLE` is keyed by `HunterActionKind`, so a new variant is a compile error until it has an entry (the `validator.ts` `RULES` precedent), and each entry is typed against *its own* variant, so handlers read `action.edgeId` with no narrowing and no unreachable guard.
  - **One cast, at the dispatch point.** `ACTION_TABLE[action.kind] as ActionDefinition<HunterAction>` is the only unchecked step: the table's keying makes it true and the type system cannot say so. The alternatives were worse and both were tried on paper - give every handler the whole union plus a guard clause it can never take (an unreachable branch per targeted action, growing with every new one), or wrap each entry in an always-true runtime `kind` check (same branch, more noise). Follows `seal`/`unseal`: one named, documented cast rather than a dozen silent ones. **Verified by mutation: deleting an entry fails `bun run typecheck` in all three workspaces.**
  - **`createActionLogic()` takes no deps yet.** Roadblock reads nothing but the world and the balance it is handed. **M3.4a widens it to `createActionLogic({ rng })`** and handler signatures to `(deps, context)`; both report producers draw.
  - **The table's fourth field is `target: ActionTargetKind`** (`edge` | `node` | `global`), exposed as `targetKindOf(kind)`. That is the AC's "target type", and its consumer is M5.5, which has to know whether to ask the player for an edge or a node before the action is well formed. It is deliberately *not* used for a generic existence check: target validity is action-specific (blockable, already blocked), so each entry validates its own.
  - **Costs are data, effects are handlers.** `cost(balance)` gives action points and budget; `trustChange(balance)` is signed, so a briefing's `+trustGain` is the same mechanism as a roadblock's `-trustCost` rather than a special case. The framework charges, then calls `apply` on the already-charged world, so a handler only ever adds its own effect. **A consequence for M3.4b:** `true_briefing` already moves trust correctly; that task adds criminal heat and the volume multiplier, not the trust gain.
  - **Action points and budget are exact; trust is clamped.** Validation has already refused anything that would put either resource below zero, so subtracting is safe - that is the whole of the budget floor the cut bankruptcy condition relied on. Trust is clamped to `balance.hunter.trustMin/trustMax` because a cost larger than the meter is a balance setting, not an illegal action.
  - **The action's own rules are checked before its price.** A malformed action is wrong however rich the hunter is, and M5.5 wants to grey out an illegal target without the budget having a say.
  - **`Traversal.blockedEdgeIds` landed, per M1.4a's note, and a block is not mode-specific.** `blockedEdgeIdsAt(containments, turn)` in `hunter.ts` derives the set; `buildAdjacency` drops those edges, so every search sees it at once - `neighbors`, `adjacency`, `shortestPath`, `shortestPathToAny`, `reachable`, and therefore M3.5 and M4.1 for free. A checkpoint stops whoever walks into it, so it closes the edge on foot too; what keeps the city from being sealable is `balance.edges` leaving footpaths unblockable, which is now asserted rather than assumed.
  - **A containment blocks from the turn it is placed until `expiresAt` exclusive**, so `durationTurns: 4` covers exactly four turns. Expired containments are kept rather than pruned: `validateRoadblock` refuses a second block while the first stands, and the history is there for M3.8a's consequences phase.
  - **No `hunt_over` rejection.** Nothing before M3.8c produces a finished world, and whether a finished game still accepts actions is M3.8b's planning phase to decide. Adding a rejection no test could reach would have been the speculative branch.
  - Verified by mutation: **9 mutants, 9 caught.** AP check off by one, budget check dropped, roadblock forgetting its duration, block expiring a turn late, searches ignoring `blockedEdgeIds`, roadblock gaining trust instead of spending it, the `blockable` flag ignored, `apply` skipping the charge - each failed between 1 and 5 named tests; a missing table entry fails the typecheck.
  - `actions.ts`, `graph.ts` and `hunter.ts` are all at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.31% statements / 95.13% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 341 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.4a Canvass and pull CCTV**
  deps: M3.3
  CCTV queues a delayed, reliable report of the past; canvass produces reports based on witness density and trust.
  Split at the pre-M2.2 review: these two are the report producers and share the delayed-report seam M3.6 builds on, and the queue is new machinery rather than a table entry. Briefing produces no report of its own and moves two other meters.
  AC: each action has tests for its effect, including CCTV delay.
  Notes:
  - **`createActionLogic({ rng })`**, as M3.3's note promised, and handlers are now `(deps, context)`. No new dependencies; no new state field.
  - **`IntelSource` plus `gather` is the seam, not a queue.** The two actions differ in exactly four things - who is looking (`source`), how likely they are to see anything (`chance`), how far they can be trusted (`accuracy`), and how long the paperwork takes (`delay`) - so those four are the data and `gather` is the single handler over them. **There is no report queue and there should not be one:** `makeReport` already carries `observedAtTurn` and `receivedAtTurn`, and M3.2's view filters by the latter, so a delayed report is stored once, in `world.reports`, and hidden by the clock rather than held somewhere else and moved. **M3.6 adds the sources the world produces unasked as new `IntelSource` values**, not new branches.
  - **Only `apply` draws.** `validate` is a preview a UI may run on every hover, so it must not move the RNG. `gather` advances the stream whether or not it produces a report, so a turn replays identically either way - the miss path returns `{ ...world, rng: rolled.state }` and a mutant that returns `world` is caught.
  - **Coverage decides whether a report exists; trust decides both.** Canvass chance is `witnessDensity x nightMultiplier x trustFactor` and its accuracy is `sightingAccuracy(trustFactor)` - DESIGN.md's "low trust means fewer *and worse* witness reports", with `actions.ts` owning "fewer" and `report.ts` owning "worse". CCTV chance is `cctvCoverage` alone and its accuracy is the flat `balance.reports.cctvAccuracy`: a camera has no opinion of the hunter, so a pull on a park is money spent on nothing, which is exactly what DESIGN.md's district table says and what the hunter can read before paying.
  - **Three helpers were placed outside `actions.ts` on purpose, each taking a structural slice rather than `Balance`** (the `TraversableGraph` precedent), because `balance.ts` imports `world.ts` imports `time.ts` and taking `Balance` would close the cycle: `timeOfDayAt(hours: DaylightHours, hour)` in `time.ts` - **M3.7's nightfall event uses it**; `sightingAccuracy(settings: AccuracySettings, trustFactor)` and `nextReportId(reports)` in `report.ts` - **M3.6 reuses both**.
  - **Report ids are derived from `reports.length`, not stored.** Reports are only ever appended - `view.ts` filters the list, it never removes from it - so the count is already a deterministic counter and `WorldState` needs no field for one.
  - **Every M3.4a report is `truth: "true"`.** What a canvass or a pull gets wrong it gets wrong by being inaccurate, not by being a lie. `balance.reports.falseReportRate` is therefore **unconsumed and belongs to M3.6**, along with perturbing a reported node by its accuracy; the doubt today is carried entirely by the hidden `accuracy` field.
  - **`balance.actions.pullCctv.lookbackTurns` is also unconsumed, and cannot be honoured yet.** `CriminalState` holds one position, no history, so "footage of the past" has no past to read: nothing moves the criminal until M3.5, and in every world reachable today the lookback would name the same node as the present. CCTV observes the node at the pulled turn and delivers it late, which is DESIGN.md's "reliable, delayed by 1-2 turns". **The bounded position trail it needs is M3.8b's state design** - Inbox entry added.
  - **`propertiesAt`'s empty-district fallback is the one uncovered branch in `actions.ts`** (line 192). `validateNodeTarget` has already refused any node the map does not have, so it is unreachable; it exists so the lookup is total rather than asserted, and "nobody saw anything" is the honest answer. Same shape as `mincut.ts:93` and `river.ts:189`.
  - **No divide-by-zero guard in `trustFactorOf`, and that is a type fact, not an oversight.** It was written with one, and the guard failed `bun run typecheck`: `Balance` is `typeof BALANCE` over an `as const` table, so `trustMin` and `trustMax` are the literal types `0` and `100` and a range with no span cannot be constructed to test it. Untestable branches are not carried here (the same reasoning as M3.3's rejected generic target check), so it was removed and the constraint written into the comment. **M4.3 is what would make the division fallible**, by widening these fields so a sweep can vary them - Inbox entry added.
  - Verified by mutation: **13 mutants, 13 caught.** Collapsing the CCTV delay to a constant, delivering it the same turn, dropping trust from the canvass chance or from its accuracy, ignoring nightfall, always reporting a sighting, skipping the RNG advance on a miss, reading witness density as camera coverage, reusing one report id, dropping the trust term from `sightingAccuracy`, moving nightfall an hour, dropping the dawn boundary, and leaving the trust factor unclamped each failed between 1 and 4 named tests.
  - `report.ts` and `time.ts` at 100% across the board; `actions.ts` 100% statements/functions/lines, 95.83% branches (the fallback above). `core` overall 99.34% statements / 95.01% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 363 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.4b True briefing**
  deps: M3.4a
  Raises report volume and criminal heat, gains trust.
  Small on purpose and left standalone rather than folded into M3.6, because it is the one MVP action feeding two later tasks from opposite sides: M3.6's report-volume formula and M3.5's criminal knowledge (DESIGN.md: the AI sees news briefings).
  AC: the volume multiplier changes how many reports a turn produces; criminal heat and trust both move by their `balance.actions.trueBriefing` amounts.
  Notes:
  - **Two records of the same broadcast, on purpose.** `HunterState.briefingTurns` is what the hunter said; `CriminalKnowledge.heardBriefingTurns` is what the criminal picked up. They are identical in the MVP because a true briefing is public, and they are still two fields because M7's fake briefing is the first without being the second. **M3.5 reads the criminal's list, M3.6 reads the hunter's**; neither should reach across.
  - **The volume multiplier is flat, not compounding.** `reportVolumeFactor(settings, briefings)` in `report.ts` is `1` until the hunt has been on the news and the multiplier after, however many briefings were given. `multiplier ** briefings` would make the one free action the whole game, and a briefing is free precisely so a broke hunter is never stuck (M3.3's note). **M3.6 owns the formula**; it lives beside `sightingAccuracy` so the world's own sightings can scale by the same term.
  - **Only the witnesses hear the news.** `CANVASS.chance` gained the volume term, `PULL_CCTV` did not: a camera films whatever walks past it whether or not there was a press conference. No clamp on the product - a chance above one behaves as certainty against `rng.float`, which is what `CERTAIN_DENSITY` in the tests already relies on, and a clamp would be a branch with no observable effect.
  - **`balance.reports.prankRatePerBriefing` stays unconsumed and belongs to M3.6**, with `falseReportRate`. More tips is this task; the pranks that come with them are the same formula from the other end.
  - **The briefing draws nothing**, so the stream is where it was. That is what lets the volume test compare a briefed and an unbriefed hunter at the same seed and know the difference is the multiplier and not a different roll: the trust the briefing also gains would otherwise move the canvass chance too, so the test varies `briefingTurns` alone.
  - Verified by mutation: **7 mutants, 7 caught.** Dropping the volume term from the canvass chance, forgetting to record the briefing on either side, no heat gain, heat uncapped, a compounding volume factor, and a hunter who starts already briefed - each failed between 1 and 3 named tests.
  - `report.ts` and `hunter.ts` at 100% across the board; `actions.ts` unchanged at 100% statements/functions/lines, 95.83% branches. `core` overall 99.34% statements / 95.05% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 371 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.5 Criminal AI: amateur**
  deps: M3.1a, M3.3
  Utility-based chooser per DESIGN.md. Criminal only reasons over what it can know (visible roadblocks, briefings).
  M3.3 added to deps at the pre-M2.2 review: "never moves through a known roadblock" needs roadblocks to exist, and M1.4a's note puts them in `Traversal.blockedEdgeIds`, which M3.3 adds. Nothing upstream of this task produced one.
  AC: on an open map with no hunter actions, reaches an exit in ≤ path length + slack; never moves through a known roadblock; decisions deterministic per seed.
  Notes:
  - **`createCriminalAiLogic({ rng, graph }).choose({ situation, balance, state })`**, following M3.1a's request-object convention. The situation is a **`CriminalSituation`**, not a `WorldState`: map, clock and `criminal` only, produced by the one projection `toCriminalSituation`. This is the mirror of `view.ts` - "only reasons over what it can know" is a type rather than a discipline, and `ai.test.ts` pins the absent keys the way `view.test.ts` does. Both names are on `apps/web`'s `noRestrictedImports` list in `biome.json`, because a situation carries the true position.
  - **Resolves M3.1a's "Consequence for M3.5" above:** `balance.criminal.profiles` is now partial (`Partial<Record<CriminalProfile, CriminalProfileWeights>>` with `amateur` pinned present), so the lookup returns `| undefined` and the AI must handle a miss. A profile with no weights has no behaviour and **waits** - total, cheap, and better than borrowing another profile's nerve or throwing inside a pure turn loop. `balance.test.ts`'s pooled-profile assertion still makes it unreachable for any world `createGameLogic` builds. **M7 adds a profile by adding a row to `PROFILES`.**
  - **"Never moves through a known roadblock" is structural.** `knowledge.knownRoadblockEdgeIds` goes into `Traversal.blockedEdgeIds` (M1.4a's seam), so blocked edges are absent from both the neighbour enumeration and every distance measured - there is no check to forget. **Nothing populates that list yet; M3.8b's "a criminal moving into a roadblocked edge is stopped" is what does**, and the AI needs no change when it lands.
  - **Briefings are read through heat, not as a term of their own.** M3.4b's `applyTrueBriefing` raises heat, heat scales every perceived exposure, so a second term for the same broadcast would count it twice. `heardBriefingTurns` therefore stays unread by this task, as M3.4b's note anticipated it might not be.
  - **New shared module `exposure.ts`**, holding `districtPropertiesAt` and `witnessDensityAt` moved out of `actions.ts`. The witnesses and the criminal's nerve have to agree about how busy downtown is at 03:00 or they are two rules wearing one name. Both take a balance slice (`DistrictTable`, `DaylightHours`) rather than `Balance`, for the cycle reason in M3.4a's note.
  - **`LIMITS.maxAiCandidates` is consumed** and no longer dead: three stationary candidates (hide, rest, wait) plus as many moves as fit under the cap. A node with more exits than that is a generator bug, not a workload. **Every candidate costs exactly one `rng.float` in enumeration order, win or lose**, so the stream position after a decision depends on the candidate count and not on which one won; the determinism tests count draws.
  - **Slack in the escape test is 2, measured not guessed.** Over 300 generated seeds, 298 criminals reached an exit in exactly the shortest number of steps and 2 took one extra. The test itself uses the hard-coded seed list `[1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13, 21, 34]`, per the repo rule against generated seed sets.
  - `ai.ts` and `exposure.ts` are at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines; `actions.ts` rose to 100% branches as a side effect of `exposure.test.ts`. `core` overall 99.38% statements / 95.59% branches. `bun run verify` passes; 399 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.6 Reports and pranks**
  deps: M3.4b, M3.5
  Sightings generated from criminal movement through witnessed districts; prank calls from rate formula.
  AC: over a hard-coded list of seeds, mean sighting accuracy at high trust exceeds mean accuracy at low trust by a named margin, and prank rate rises with reward the same way. The seed list, the sample size and the margin are all named constants: a generated seed set would make this the one test in the repo that can fail differently on two runs of the same commit, and a failure has to name the seed that broke it so M3.6 can pin it as a regression.
  Notes:
  - **`createIntelLogic({ rng, graph }).collect({ world, balance })` in a new module `intel.ts`, not two more `IntelSource` entries** - a deliberate departure from M3.4a's note, with the reason written at the top of the file. A requested source answers "what is at the node the hunter chose", always truthfully and always about that node; an unprompted one picks its own node, may be about nobody at all, and has no action, no cost and no target to validate. Folding them together would have meant giving `IntelSource` a `truth` and a node of its own, which is rewriting M3.4a's producer to avoid a second one. What they share is `report.ts`, which now holds every formula both of them read. **The unprompted sources are still a table**: `UnpromptedSource` is `{ rate, file }` and `SOURCES` is the list, so M7's reward line or a planted trail is an entry rather than a branch (architecture rule 6).
  - **`collect` returns the reports, not a world with the reports in it.** `RngDraw<readonly Report[]>`: the drawn calls plus the stream position after them. **M3.7 needs to know which reports were new this turn** to announce them (its `eyewitness` and `prank_call` events carry a report id) and **M3.8a's intel phase is what appends them to `world.reports`** and decides what they cost the criminal in heat; a producer that appended for them would have made both of those a diff against the previous turn.
  - **Heat is the term that separates a call from a canvass.** The rate is `witnessDensityAt x trustFactor x reportVolumeFactor x heatFactor` - DESIGN.md lists district density, time of day, trust and criminal heat against a sighting, and this is all four. The hunter can stand in a district and ask; the city only volunteers a face it already knows, so a criminal at `startingHeat` (20) produces a fifth of the calls one at `heatMax` does. **Consequence for M3.8a: nothing raises heat yet.** `balance.criminal.heatPerSighting` is still unconsumed and belongs to the consequences phase, so until it lands the unprompted feed stays thin for the whole hunt rather than warming up as DESIGN.md intends.
  - **A mistaken witness is a near miss; a prank is from nowhere.** `falseReportRate` of witness calls name a *neighbouring* node with `truth: "false"` and accuracy 0; a prank names any node on the map uniformly, with `source: "tip"`, `truth: "prank"` and accuracy 0. DESIGN.md has the hunter telling pranks from real calls by inconsistency, which only works if the two are inconsistent in different ways. **This is the reading of "perturbing a reported node by its accuracy" from M3.4a's note**: the perturbation is its own branch with the report's `truth` set to match, not a jitter on a report still labelled true - **M3.6b scores its heatmap against `truth`**, and a "true" report naming the wrong node would make that label a lie.
  - **`prankRate(settings, briefings)` is additive and capped, deliberately unlike `reportVolumeFactor`.** M3.4b made volume flat so that repeating the one free action could not become the whole game; the prank rate rises with every briefing because attention the city is already paying buys no new public but does reach a new crank. `maxPrankRate` (0.4) is what stops that running to a feed of nothing but noise, and is why growth is safe on this half of the bargain and not on the other.
  - **New knob `balance.reports.unpromptedDelayTurns = 1`.** A canvass lands the turn it is taken because the hunter is standing there; a call nobody asked for takes a turn to reach the desk. Not shared with `pullCctv.minDelayTurns/maxDelayTurns`: those are late for a different reason and M3.8b is expected to change them when CCTV gets a position trail.
  - **Two helpers were extracted so that two sides could not disagree**, following M3.5's `exposure.ts` precedent: `trustFactorOf(bounds, trust)` moved from `actions.ts` to `hunter.ts`, and `heatFactorOf(bounds, heat)` from `ai.ts` to `criminal.ts`. Each takes a balance slice rather than `Balance`, for the cycle reason in M3.4a's note. The canvass and the passer-by have to agree what a trust of 40 is worth, and the criminal's nerve and the witness's memory have to agree how recognisable it has become.
  - **The acceptance test runs on generated cities, one `it` per seed with the seed in its name**, so a failure names the seed. Every constant is hard-coded: `SEEDS = [1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13, 21, 34]`, `SAMPLE_TURNS = 1000`, `MIN_SAMPLE = 20`, `HIGH_TRUST`/`LOW_TRUST` 90/10, `BRIEFINGS_GIVEN = 5`, `ACCURACY_MARGIN = 0.2`, `PRANK_MARGIN = 0.1`. Measured over those eight seeds: the accuracy gap is 0.261 to 0.322 and the prank gap 0.180 to 0.220 calls per turn. The criminal is held at `heatMax` on both arms of each comparison, so trust is the only difference between them and the thin low-trust arm still collects 27 to 57 calls to average.
  - **The unit tests force no balance knob.** The Inbox item about `Balance` being `typeof BALANCE` still stands - `balance.reports` scalars are literal types and cannot be varied - so the mistaken and invented branches are reached by sampling 200 turns of the balance that ships instead, which is the better test anyway. Only `balance.districts` is bent, as M3.4a's tests already do.
  - **`createIntelLogic` was deliberately left off `apps/web`'s restricted-import list.** It takes a `WorldState`, which the list already denies by name, and a `SealedWorld` is not assignable to one, so `web` cannot call it - the same reason `createActionLogic` and `createCriminalAiLogic` are not on the list either.
  - Verified by mutation: **11 mutants, 11 caught.** Accuracy ignoring trust, the prank rate losing its per-briefing term, the rate dropping heat, trust or volume, a mistaken witness naming the criminal's own node or keeping the true accuracy, an unprompted call delivered the turn it was made, ids numbered without the calls drawn earlier in the same turn, a prank clustered on the criminal, and a quiet turn leaving the stream where it was. Three of those (volume, ids, prank node) survived the first pass and are why that test now compares against a named margin, pins a seed on which both sources ring in one turn, and counts the districts the pranks name.
  - `intel.ts`, `report.ts`, `hunter.ts`, `criminal.ts`, `actions.ts` and `ai.ts` are all at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.41% statements / 95.76% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 441 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.6b Belief heatmap (core)**
  deps: M3.6, M3.2, M1.4b
  Renumbered from M4.0 (and from M5.1 before that) and **moved ahead of M3.7**, because the belief distribution lives in `WorldState` and M3.8a's consequences phase is what advances it. See the decision below; DESIGN.md's "the hunter view includes a belief distribution over nodes" is now implemented literally.
  `createBeliefLogic({ graph, balance }).advance(belief, evidence) => Belief` - one turn of spread, not a fold over history. Seed from confirmed sightings, spread along `GraphLogic.adjacency` by plausible speed (M1.4b's note is the seam), prune by negative evidence (`no_sighting` reports, roadblocks not hit), weight by report reliability.
  **This task owns adding `belief` to `WorldState` and to `HunterView`**, and the projection line in `toHunterView`. M3.2 ships before this task and therefore ships without the member; adding one is not a breach of M3.2's shape assertions, which name what the view must *not* carry.
  **`Belief` is plain serializable data** (architecture rule 3): a parallel array or record over node ids, never a `Map`. It is part of the byte-identical final state M3.8c's determinism test covers.
  **The advance function must take only hunter-visible inputs.** It runs inside `core`, where the criminal's true position is in scope, so the guard has to be a test rather than a type.
  AC: mass sums to 1 within a named tolerance; negative evidence reduces mass on searched nodes; confirmed sighting concentrates mass; **two worlds differing only in the criminal's position produce an identical belief distribution** - verify that one by mutation, by seeding the spread from the true position and watching it fail.
  Notes:
  - **`createBeliefLogic({ graph }).advance({ belief, evidence, balance })` in a new module `belief.ts`** - balance is an argument, not a factory dep, a deliberate departure from the signature written above. Two reasons: AGENTS.md's first engineering principle says logic depends only on other logic and receives configuration as an argument, and M3.1a's request-object convention is what every other unit in `core` follows; and **M3.8a's `step` is handed one balance per call**, so a captured balance is a second copy that can silently disagree with the one the rest of the turn is using. `graph` stays a dep because it is logic.
  - **`Belief` is `readonly BeliefCell[]`**, `{ nodeId, mass }` in the map's own node order, not a record keyed by node id. Same reason `MapGraph` keeps arrays (M1.2): rule 3 bans a `Map` from state, an explicit order is the order determinism needs, and a branded `NodeId` survives as a real type in an array where `Record<NodeId, number>` degrades to a string index signature. `world.test.ts`'s plain-data property now spreads `world.belief` into the values it checks.
  - **The hidden-information guard is a type as far as it can be, and a test only where it cannot be.** `advance` takes a `BeliefEvidence`, and the one thing that builds one is **`toBeliefEvidence(view: HunterView)`** - the criminal is not in scope in that function's argument at all. This is the same shape as M3.5's `toCriminalSituation`, pointing the other way. **M3.8a must call it as `toBeliefEvidence(toHunterView(seal(world)))`**; wiring evidence up out of `WorldState` by hand would throw the guarantee away.
  - **Evidence is only the reports that landed *this* turn** (`receivedAtTurn === clock.turn`). `advance` is one turn of work, so a report folded in on the turn it arrived must not be folded in again on every turn after it. The rest of the evidence is the map, the standing blocked edges from `blockedEdgeIdsAt` (M3.3's helper, reused unchanged), and the two observation lists.
  - **Predict, then update, in that order**: spread the distribution that was current, prune the clearances, concentrate on the sightings, normalise once at the end. Folding the reports in first would dilute the freshest thing the hunter has by a turn of running they have already accounted for.
  - **Spread is by plausible speed, which is `1 / cost` in `balance.map.escapeMode`** over `GraphLogic.adjacency` (M1.4b's seam, taken once per turn rather than per node). A node keeps `1 - spreadFraction` and hands the rest to its neighbours in proportion to speed, so the footpath out of downtown takes twice what the road does. **Negative evidence from roadblocks is structural, not a term**: blocked edges go in as `Traversal.blockedEdgeIds` and are simply absent from the spread, which is DESIGN.md's "roadblock not hit" with nothing to forget.
  - **Reliability is read off the source, never off `truth` or `accuracy`.** New balance group **`balance.belief`** (`spreadFraction` 0.5, `sourceWeight` cctv 0.9 / patrol 0.6 / witness 0.5 / tip 0.2). The hunter cannot see a report's hidden accuracy, so what a source is worth has to be what the hunter can judge: who is talking. This qualifies M3.6's note that "M3.6b scores its heatmap against `truth`" - the *heatmap* cannot read it; scoring the player's belief against the truth is M3.10's business, and M3.6's honest `truth` labels are still what makes that possible. Every weight is below 1 so no single call collapses the map or empties a district. `BeliefSettings` is annotated rather than inferred, for the reason `MapGenerationSettings` is, so M4.3's sweep can vary it.
  - **A sighting moves mass, it does not set it**: every node keeps `1 - weight` and the named node takes the difference, so a report the hunter half believes moves half the city's worth of suspicion. A clearance multiplies the searched node by `1 - weight` and leaves the rest alone; normalisation puts the difference back where the criminal might still be.
  - **`makeWorldState` derives the opening belief** as `pointBelief(nodes, map.incidentNodeId)` and `WorldStateInput` omits it, like the empty report list beside it: before a turn has run, the only place the criminal has certainly been is the crime scene, which both sides know. No existing caller changed.
  - **`HunterView.belief` is projected verbatim** - it is the hunter's own inference built from the view's own inputs, so there is nothing to redact. **M4.1's `heatmap-chaser` and M5.3b's overlay read it off the view** and accumulate nothing of their own, which was the point of the decision below.
  - **Knowing nothing is the uniform distribution, not a distribution of zeroes.** `normalized` falls back to uniform when everything has been pruned away or when the incoming belief names only nodes this map has not got, so no `NaN` can reach the UI. `BELIEF_MASS_TOLERANCE` (1e-9) is exported and is what the invariant is stated against, because normalising divides.
  - **Nothing was added to `apps/web`'s restricted-import list.** `toBeliefEvidence` takes a `HunterView`, which `web` is meant to hold, and `advance` reads no hidden state; the list exists for the functions that take a `WorldState` or a `CriminalSituation`.
  - **The last AC, verified by mutation, at the call site.** Because the criminal is out of scope inside `belief.ts`, the mutation has to go where M3.8a's phase will be: `belief.test.ts`'s `nextBelief` helper was reseeded from `unseal(world).criminal.nodeId` instead of `world.belief`, and the property "infers the same heatmap wherever the criminal actually is" failed on the first counterexample it drew (`["downtown","riverside",0,[]]`), with the other 25 tests still passing. Reverted.
  - `belief.ts` is at 100% statements, functions and lines, 91.66% branches; the two uncovered branches are the `?? 0` fallbacks on a `Map.get` that the pre-seeded mass table makes unreachable and the types still demand. `core` overall 99.45% statements / 95.46% branches.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 467 tests, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.7a Event table, plus nightfall and civilian hurt**
  deps: M3.6, M3.2
  The machinery half of M3.7, split as the Inbox item required: events as data (DESIGN.md "Events" - trigger condition, weight function, effect handler), the one function that fires the table, and two events.
  **M3.7a takes nightfall and civilian hurt** because they differ in every seam the machinery has. Nightfall triggers on a fact about the clock, weighs certainty, and changes nothing. Civilian hurt triggers on the world - who is standing where, at what hour - weighs a chance read off the criminal's profile, and costs a casualty. The remaining three would each have tested one of those twice: `rush_hour` is a second clock entry, and `eyewitness` and `prank_call` are announcements of reports a different phase files, which is also what makes them depend on M3.8a rather than on this task.
  New events may carry hidden information in `GameEvent` but must declare it to M3.2's projection: an event whose presence reveals a report's `truth`, or the criminal's position beyond what DESIGN.md confirms, belongs on the hidden-variant list, not in `HunterView`.
  **This task owns the exhaustive assertion M3.2's note says is still owed**, for all five variants and not only the two implemented here.
  AC: both events unit-tested; neither fires unless its trigger holds; every `GameEvent` variant is either in `HunterEvent` or on the hidden list, asserted exhaustively so a new variant cannot default into the view; adding an event is a new table entry and no new branch in the firing logic (architecture rule 6, review-only per the Inbox item, named here because this is where it would first be broken).
  Notes:
  - **`createEventLogic({ rng }).fire({ world, balance }) => { world, events }` in a new module `eventtable.ts`.** Balance is an argument, not a factory dep (M3.6b's reasoning, unchanged). The table could not live in `events.ts`: `world.ts` imports that module for `GameEvent`, so a table that reads a `WorldState` would close a value cycle. `events.ts` stays the variants plus the visibility data; `eventtable.ts` is the triggers, weights and handlers.
  - **`fire` owns the world it returns.** The events it fired are already appended to `world.events` and the stream is already advanced on `world.rng`; the same list is returned beside the world so **M3.8a's consequences phase reads what happened instead of diffing the feed** (its pressure jump on `civilian_hurt`). This is deliberately the opposite of M3.6's `collect`, which returns reports and no world: `collect` has no world to own, and a `fire` that applied a casualty without recording the event would be the trap the two shapes are avoiding in different directions.
  - **Weight is how likely a triggered entry is, in [0, 1], not a share of one draw.** Several of DESIGN.md's events can be true of one turn - nightfall is a fact about the clock, not a lottery against somebody being hurt - so every triggered entry is drawn independently and one that always happens weighs `CERTAIN` (1). **`rng.weightedPick` is therefore unused**, and M1.1's warning about its uniform fallback does not arise: there is no candidate pool, and an entry no world makes eligible is filtered by its trigger rather than by a zero weight.
  - **Every triggered entry costs exactly one `rng.float`, in table order, fired or not** (`intel.ts`'s discipline), and an entry whose trigger is false costs nothing. Both are pure functions of the world, so the turn replays. **The table is in `GameEvent`'s own declaration order** (`civilian_hurt`, then `nightfall`), which means **M3.7b's three entries will move the stream for a given seed**. That is free today because nothing records a stream position yet; it will not be free after M3.9.
  - **Triggers, weights and announcements all read the world as the phase began**, never a world an earlier entry has already changed; only `apply` folds. One turn's events happen at once, so the table's order decides the stream and nothing else.
  - **`announce` returns a list and `apply` runs once per event it named.** That is what lets **M3.7b's `eyewitness` and `prank_call` each be one entry** over the reports that landed this turn rather than a loop inside `fire`, which would have been the rule-6 breach this task is watched for.
  - **M3.7b needs no new input.** Reports that landed this turn are `world.reports.filter((report) => report.receivedAtTurn === world.clock.turn)`, the filter `toBeliefEvidence` already uses, so `EventRequest` stays `{ world, balance }`. Nothing puts a report in `world.reports` until M3.8a's intel phase, which is why **M3.7b now depends on M3.8a** and M3.8a depends only on M3.7a.
  - **`HIDDEN_EVENT_KINDS` is replaced by `EVENT_VISIBILITY`, and this supersedes M3.2's note.** It is a record keyed by *every* `GameEventKind`, valued `"hidden" | "hunter_visible"`, annotated with `satisfies` so a new variant is a compile error in `events.ts` until it says which side it falls on. `HiddenEventKind` is a mapped type over it and `view.ts` redacts through `isHiddenEventKind`. The hidden-variant list is still data, as the decision below requires; what changed is that the data is **total rather than a subset**, which is the only shape in which a new variant cannot default *into* the view. The barrel exports `EVENT_VISIBILITY` and `isHiddenEventKind` in place of `HIDDEN_EVENT_KINDS`.
  - **The exhaustive assertion is a chain of three, each catching what the others cannot.** The compile error above (verified: a sixth variant produces three `tsc` errors, and is still refused after the row is added if no sample covers it); `events.test.ts` pinning every row of the table by hand; and `view.test.ts` projecting one event of every variant while asserting the sample covers `Object.keys(EVENT_VISIBILITY)`. The two literal pins stay literal for the reason M3.2 gave: a test that asks the constant what to forbid goes vacuous exactly when the constant is what is wrong.
  - **Civilian harm is triggered by the district and weighted by the profile.** The trigger is `witnessDensityAt(...) > 0` at the criminal's node and hour - M3.5's shared exposure table, so an empty park after dark has nobody in it to hurt and the same table decides who could have seen them. The weight is `balance.criminal.profiles[profile].civilianHarmChance` (amateur 0.06), so a profile with no weights hurts nobody, the way `ai.ts` gives it no behaviour. **No new balance knob**, and deliberately no desperation term: the knob is documented as a per-turn chance, and nothing advances desperation until M3.8a, so scaling by it would have been a rule invented here rather than a rule DESIGN.md states.
  - **The effect is one casualty and nothing else.** Pressure on `civilian_hurt` is M3.8a's consequences phase by that task's own body, so raising it here would apply it twice; DESIGN.md's "roads clog" has no MVP mechanic. The event names the criminal's node on purpose - harm confirms a location, which is what `events.ts` already documented.
  - **Nightfall is a transition, not a state**: `timeOfDayAt` at this hour against `timeOfDayAt` an hour before, so `balance.time` stays the one place that decides what night is (M3.4a's note). No turn-zero case falls out of it - a hunt beginning exactly at `nightStartHour` opens with nightfall and one beginning later in the night does not - and `apply` is identity, because the districts already read the hour and the event only announces it.
  - **Nothing was added to `apps/web`'s restricted-import list.** It names hidden-state types and constructors, not logic factories; `createEventLogic` is unusable in `web` anyway, because every call needs a `WorldState`, which the list already blocks by name.
  - **No new I/O boundary and no new `limits.ts` entry.** The table is a fixed, code-authored list and the only loop is over it.
  - `events.ts` and `eventtable.ts` are both at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.47% statements / 95.57% branches, up from 99.45 / 95.46.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 493 tests (was 467), 96 files checked by Biome, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.7b Remaining events: eyewitness, prank call, rush hour**
  deps: M3.7a, M3.8a
  The three events M3.7a left. `rush_hour` is a clock entry beside `nightfall`, off `balance.time.rushHourHours`. `eyewitness` and `prank_call` announce the reports that landed this turn, one event per report, and are the two hidden variants: their kind says what a report is, so the hunter may only ever see M3.2's `report_arrived`.
  **Depends on M3.8a, not on M3.7a alone**, which inverts the original numbering: nothing files a report into `world.reports` until the intel phase does, so until then the announcement trigger has nothing but a fixture to read. See M3.7a's note.
  Each of the three is a new entry in `eventtable.ts` plus nothing else. If any of them needs a change to `fire`, to `EventRequest` or to the `EventDefinition` shape, say so in the task note: that is the rule-6 seam M3.7a's acceptance criteria named, and it failing is worth knowing about.
  AC: each event unit-tested; an announcement fires once per report that landed this turn and not at all on a turn with no arrivals; `rush_hour` fires only on the hours balance names; M3.7a's exhaustive projection assertion still holds and is not weakened to accommodate the two hidden variants.
  Notes:
  - **The rule-6 seam held, which is the thing this task was asked to report.** Three entries in `eventtable.ts` and nothing else: **no change to `fire`, to `EventRequest`, to `EventDefinition` or to `events.ts`**, and no new export. `turn.ts` was not touched. M3.7a's two load-bearing predictions both paid off exactly as written - `announce` returning a list is what makes each announcement **one entry over the reports of a turn** instead of a loop inside `fire`, and `EventRequest` needed no new input because the arrivals are `world.reports.filter((report) => report.receivedAtTurn === world.clock.turn)`, the filter `toBeliefEvidence` already uses. The only new code outside the three entries is two module-private helpers, `arrivals` and `announced`.
  - **The two announcements are one constructor called twice** (`announcement(kind, names)`), a pure data constructor in the `make*` sense, not a factory. They differ in exactly one thing - which arrivals they name - and writing them as two literals would have duplicated the trigger, the weight, the mapping and the identity `apply` to say so.
  - **The split is `truth === "prank"`, not `source`.** `prank_call` names the invented arrivals and `eyewitness` names every other one; a *mistaken* witness (M3.6's `truth: "false"`) is an eyewitness, because what they got wrong they got wrong by being wrong rather than by lying, and the kind is hidden precisely because it would otherwise say which report is which. Source cannot make this distinction at all - `actions.ts` files a canvass as `source: "witness"`, the same as an unprompted call.
  - **A CCTV arrival therefore announces as `eyewitness`, which is the one place the naming is loose.** The AC says an announcement fires once per report that landed this turn, `GameEvent` offers exactly two variants to say it with, and both collapse to `report_arrived` in the view - so the hunter's feed correctly says a report came in when their footage lands, and the only distinction either variant is allowed to carry is the one that must never reach the hunter. **If M7 adds `evidence_found` or `anonymous_tip` from DESIGN.md's Information list, the CCTV arrival is the entry to move**, and it is a new table row plus a `names` predicate, not a change here.
  - **The canvass question is resolved as M3.8a's first option: accept it, and document why.** A canvass answer is never announced, because `CANVASS_DELAY` is 0 and planning is phase 3 - the events phase of turn T cannot see a report the hunter has not filed yet, and at T+1 the arrival filter no longer matches it. Accepted rather than fixed because DESIGN.md files `eyewitness` and `prank_call` under **Information** events, which are the city volunteering something, while a canvass answer is intel the hunter asked for and already has in hand; a feed line announcing it tells them nothing. The phase position **is** the mechanism, so this needed no code: what the filter selects, evaluated in phase 2, is exactly "what the city volunteered this turn". Pinned by `never announces a canvass answer, which lands after the phase that would name it`, which fires the table on the turn the hunter asks and again on the turn after, so the behaviour is deliberate and regression-protected. **Nothing else changes**: neither M3.8b's planning phase nor `actions.ts`'s delays are touched, which is why this option was preferred over moving the announcement after planning or giving canvass a delay. Inbox entry struck.
  - **`rush_hour` is read as a state, not a transition, which is the opposite of `nightfall` and deliberate.** `balance.time.rushHourHours` is a list of hours (`[7, 8, 17, 18]`) rather than a span, so the entry tests membership and the morning peak announces itself on both of its hours - which is the AC's "fires only on the hours balance names", taken literally. Nightfall has to be a transition because night is a span the clock sits inside for nine hours; rush hour does not. `apply` is identity, like nightfall's: DESIGN.md's rush hour has no MVP mechanic (no rule reads traffic), so the entry announces the clock. **No new balance knob, no `limits.ts` entry, no new I/O boundary.**
  - **Both announcements weigh `CERTAIN` and still cost their draw.** A report that landed is a fact, not a lottery, but M3.7a's discipline is that every *triggered* entry costs exactly one `rng.float` in table order; keeping it means `fire` needs no special case for an entry that cannot lose. An entry whose trigger is false still costs nothing, so a turn with no arrivals draws nothing.
  - **The stream moved, as M3.7a said it would.** `EVENT_TABLE` is now `[EYEWITNESS, PRANK_CALL, CIVILIAN_HURT, NIGHTFALL, RUSH_HOUR]`, still `GameEvent`'s own declaration order, so a given seed no longer plays the hunt it played before this task. That is free today because nothing records a stream position; **it stops being free at M3.9**, which is the first task whose artefacts outlive a change to this table.
  - **M3.7a's exhaustive projection assertion was not weakened and needed no edit.** It already covered all five variants, `EVENT_VISIBILITY` already marked both announcements `hidden`, and `view.ts` redacts through `isHiddenEventKind`, so the two new entries reach the hunter as `report_arrived` with nothing added to the projection. The three chained assertions (the compile error, `events.test.ts`'s row-by-row pin, `view.test.ts`'s one-of-every-variant projection) all still pass untouched.
  - Verified by mutation, not just by passing: **7 mutants, 7 caught.** Announcing every arrival as an eyewitness, widening the arrival filter to `<=` (which announces every landed report on every later turn, and is also what would have made the canvass pin vacuous), a rush hour that always triggers, a rush hour read as a transition, an announcement that names only the first report of the turn, an announcement that fires on a quiet turn, and an event stamped with the turn the report was *observed* rather than the turn it arrived - each failed between 1 and 16 named tests.
  - `eventtable.ts` and `events.ts` are both at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.49% statements / 95.57% branches, up from 99.48 / 95.57.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 546 tests (was 529), 98 files checked by Biome, zero lint output.

- [x] **M3.8a `step` skeleton: intel, events, consequences**
  deps: M3.4b, M3.6, M3.6b, M3.7a
  `createTurnLogic(deps).step(world, hunterActions) => { world, events }` (wiring decision below) with the three phases that touch only the world: intel (queued reports land), events (the M3.7a table fires, through `createEventLogic({ rng }).fire`), consequences (meters, clock). Planning and resolution are stubs that pass the world through, and the criminal does not move yet.
  Split again in review: M3.8a was still the integration point for six prior tasks, and the three world-only phases are testable without the criminal AI existing.
  Consequences is also what advances the **belief distribution**, by calling M3.6b's `advance` once per turn with that turn's evidence. That is the whole of the heatmap's statefulness; nothing outside `WorldState` accumulates anything.
  Consequences is what advances **political pressure**, which no task previously produced: it rises by a per-turn constant from `balance.ts` and jumps on `civilian_hurt`, clamped to [0, 100]. Nothing reads it as a trigger in the MVP - DESIGN.md's override events are M7 - and it is deliberately not a score component, because it tracks the clock and M3.10 already scores turns taken. It exists so M5.4 can show the case getting hotter.
  AC: each of the three phases has a test asserting what it does and does not touch; meters always within bounds, pressure included; a turn with no actions and no events is a clock tick and nothing else.
  Notes:
  - **`createTurnLogic({ intel, events, belief }).step({ world, actions, balance }) => { world, events }` in a new module `turn.ts`.** Request object per M3.1a, `SealedWorld` in and out per M3.1b, balance as an argument and never captured. **No `rng` dep**: the loop itself never draws. Intel and the event table own their draws, so the phase order is the only thing that decides the stream, which is what makes M3.7a's warning about entry order the whole of the determinism surface here.
  - **The five phases are five named functions, in DESIGN.md's order**, and `planningPhase` and `resolutionPhase` are stubs that hand the world through. Named rather than absent so **M3.8b fills a body instead of changing the order of a turn**. `planningPhase` takes `actions` and does not read them; a test pins that a turn with actions is identical to a turn without.
  - **`step` returns `HunterEvent[]`, not `GameEvent[]`.** This is the function `apps/web` calls every turn, and `GameEvent`'s `eyewitness` and `prank_call` variants name which report is a prank (M3.2's decision); **`GameEvent` is not on `biome.json`'s restricted-import list**, so the return type is the only thing guarding that seam. `view.ts` gained `toHunterEvents`, the same redaction over a list, and `toHunterView` now goes through it so the two cannot drift. The world `step` returns still carries the unredacted feed, which `sim` and M3.9's replay read through `unseal`.
  - **Heat is deliberately not advanced here, and `balance.criminal.heatPerSighting` is still unconsumed. It belongs to M3.8b** (M3.6's note asked for this to be decided either way). The line drawn: **hunter-side and world-side per-turn meters land in M3.8a; every criminal-side meter - heat, stamina, desperation - lands with the criminal's turn in M3.8b.** Heat's other direction is `heatDecayPerTurn` plus the hiding that `balance.districts.*.hidingSpots` implies, and both are consequences of criminal actions that do not exist until M3.8b; shipping one direction of a two-directional meter now would make M3.8b **change** a rule rather than add one. M3.8b therefore owns heat gain on a sighting, heat decay, stamina and desperation together.
  - **Action points are refilled here**, which no earlier task assigned. DESIGN.md makes AP a per-turn allowance and consequences is the only phase that knows a turn has ended; spending stays M3.8b's planning. Unlike heat it has no counterpart rule waiting, so it is complete on its own. It is a no-op on any world `create` produces, so its test hand-builds a hunter at 0 AP.
  - **Consequences advances the belief before the clock ticks.** `toBeliefEvidence` selects reports by `receivedAtTurn === clock.turn`, so advancing after the tick would make every report evidence for a turn nobody ever looks at.
  - **M3.6b's deferred call-site criterion is discharged here.** Evidence is built as `toBeliefEvidence(toHunterView(seal(world)))` and never out of the `WorldState` in scope, and `turn.test.ts` pins that two worlds differing only in where the criminal is standing advance to an identical belief.
  - **Pressure is clamped at both ends.** The ceiling is reachable by play - a 60-turn run hits it and stops - but the floor is not, because `pressurePerTurn` only ever adds. Its test hand-builds a world at negative pressure rather than bending the knob, because `Balance` is `typeof BALANCE` over an `as const` table and the knob has a literal type (the standing Inbox item). The clamp stays because the jump is balance: a meter that ran past its top would be something M5.4 has to defend itself against.
  - **No new I/O boundary and no new `limits.ts` entry.** `actions` is accepted and not consumed, so nothing here loops over caller-supplied input. **The bound on how long an action list may be belongs to M3.8b's planning phase**, where AP validation is the natural place to reject it; M3.9 separately bounds the encoded replay string.
  - **`outcome` is untouched** - end conditions are M3.8c - and nothing in this task can produce a finished world.
  - **Finding for M3.7b: as specced, its announcements will never fire for a canvass report.** `actions.ts`'s `CANVASS_DELAY` is 0, so a canvass report's `receivedAtTurn` is the turn it was asked for. Events is phase 2 and planning is phase 3, so at turn T the table cannot see a report the hunter has not filed yet, and at T+1 M3.7a's `receivedAtTurn === world.clock.turn` filter no longer matches it. Unprompted calls (`unpromptedDelayTurns` 1) and CCTV are unaffected. M3.7b decides what to do: accept it (a canvass answer is already in the report list, and announcing what the hunter just asked for adds little), or move the announcement after planning, or give canvass a delay. Not decided here, because each option changes a task that is not this one.
  - `turn.ts` and `view.ts` are both at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.48% statements / 95.57% branches, up from 99.47 / 95.57.

An over-long queue is an error result, not a truncation.
  **This task also owes rule 6 on the action side**, which the Inbox names as still unenforced: adding an entry to `ACTION_TABLE` must not require a branch in the planning phase. Assert it, and say in the note if the seam did not hold.

  - **`step` now returns a `TurnResult` union, not a record.** `{ kind: "turn", world, events, rejections }` or `{ kind: "too_many_actions", requestedActions, maxActions }`. Pinned by a test that runs the same failing queue against two worlds differing only in where the criminal stands and asserts the rejections are identical - and asserts they are non-empty first, so it cannot pass vacuously.

- [x] **M3.8b-2 Simultaneous resolution and interaction rules**
  deps: M3.8b-1, M3.5
  Fill in `turn.ts`'s `resolutionPhase` stub: the criminal AI chooses its move from its own knowledge and both sides resolve at once. Every interaction rule lives here - roadblock hit, slip past, capture on the same node - and this is the only phase where the order of the two sides could bias the outcome, so the tests are about simultaneity, not just effects.
  **This task owns every criminal-side meter**, per M3.8a's note: heat gain on a sighting (`balance.criminal.heatPerSighting`, still unconsumed), heat decay, stamina and desperation land together with the criminal's turn, because each is a consequence of a criminal action that did not exist before this task.
  **This task designs the criminal's movement state, and therefore owns the bounded position trail** the Inbox entry assigns it: `balance.actions.pullCctv.lookbackTurns` cannot be honoured while `CriminalState` holds only a current node. The trail is capped by a named constant in `limits.ts` at least as long as the largest `lookbackTurns` a balance can set, and appended by whatever moves the criminal. M3.4a's `PULL_CCTV.delay` and its tests are the only other things that change.
  **This task populates `knowledge.knownRoadblockEdgeIds`**, which M3.5 left empty on purpose: the AI needs no change when it lands, because a known block is already structural in `Traversal.blockedEdgeIds` (M3.5's note).
  **Does not own an `alive` flag.** An earlier review gave `GameOutcome.captured` one; the pre-M2.2 review cut it, because no MVP action can use force and capture is both sides on one node, so it would be `true` by construction (decision below). `balance.score.capturedAliveBonus` is gone with it.
  AC: a hunter action and a criminal move that target the same edge resolve the same way regardless of evaluation order; a criminal moving into a roadblocked edge is stopped; capture is detected when both sides occupy one node; the position trail never exceeds its named cap.
  Notes:
  - **Simultaneity is structural, not asserted.** `resolutionPhase` takes the criminal's decision from `toCriminalSituation(world)` and every rule under it reads that same `world` - the one the phase was handed - so there is no half-changed state for either side to read and no evaluation order to get wrong. The hunter's half of the turn is already inside that snapshot, because planning spends the queue before resolution runs (DESIGN.md's phase order), which is what makes a roadblock placed this turn stand against a criminal that moves onto it this turn. Pinned three ways rather than commented: a recording `CriminalAiLogic` asserting the situation handed over is `{map, clock, criminal}` of the pre-resolution world down to its `knowledge` and `trail`; a block placed by this turn's queue resolving to the identical criminal as a block that was already standing; and the same queue with the roadblock first and last resolving identically.
  - **Capture is not settled in this phase, and no capture branch exists.** DESIGN.md's win is both sides standing on one node, and in the MVP the hunter stands on no node: a roadblock is an edge, and no shipped action puts a unit anywhere (patrol, stakeout, checkpoint teams, K9 and drones are all M7 backlog). So the co-location the AC asks for has one side missing, and writing it would be writing a branch no test can reach - which this task's body and M3.8b-1's precedent both forbid. **What M3.8c is owed instead is the criminal's true final position**, which the world now carries turn by turn, and the rule that reads it is M3.8c's. The consequence is that the MVP has no reachable capture at all; that is the first Inbox entry below and it is M3.8c's problem before M4.3's win-rate targets can mean anything.
  - **The criminal learns a roadblock by walking into it, and by nothing else.** DESIGN.md's "the AI sees only what it would plausibly know: visible roadblocks, news briefings, helicopters overhead" reads as a **cap** on knowledge rather than a grant of it, and the wider reading - every block adjacent to where it stands is visible - makes the AC's "a criminal moving into a roadblocked edge is stopped" nearly unreachable in play, because the criminal would see every block before it could ever hit one. Collision-only keeps both rules alive: a checkpoint surprises the criminal exactly once and is structural from then on. **Knowledge is never forgotten**, including after the block expires, because acting on a checkpoint that was there yesterday is plausible behaviour and the list stays bounded by the map's edge count - `withKnownRoadblock` refuses duplicates, which also keeps it a set in everything but type (architecture rule 3 leaves no `Set` in state).
  - **What the criminal knows and what stops it are two different mechanisms, and the AI needed no change.** A known block is removed from the graph before the search runs (`criminalTraversalOf`, M3.5's `Traversal.blockedEdgeIds` seam), so the AI has no rule about roadblocks at all; an unknown one is a stop applied to a move made in good faith. The only edit to `ai.ts` was to export the traversal builder it already had, so resolution routes the criminal on the same graph the criminal planned on - the route resolved is the route chosen, not a second opinion about it. Pinned by a test that gives the criminal prior knowledge of the only blocked edge out of its node and runs the **real** AI: it stays put and learns nothing new.
  - **`CriminalState.trail: readonly NodeId[]`, newest first, one entry per turn.** It counts turns, not places: a criminal that hid or was turned back still spent a turn somewhere, so `[nodeId, ...trail]` is turn-indexed and `recentNodeIds(criminal, lookbackTurns)` is an honest window. Appended in exactly one place - `resolvedCriminal`, for every action kind rather than only for a move. `LIMITS.maxCriminalTrail = 8`, four times the shipped `pullCctv.lookbackTurns` of 2, with `balance.test.ts` now asserting `lookbackTurns <= LIMITS.maxCriminalTrail` so a sweep (M4.3) cannot quietly ask for footage that does not exist. Four times rather than hunt-long because the trail is state every world carries into M3.9's replay string.
  - **CCTV now reads that trail, and footage of a node the criminal has left names no travel mode.** `IntelSource` gained `lookback: (lookout) => Turn` - canvass returns `PRESENT_ONLY`, a person in the street sees now; `pull_cctv` returns `balance.actions.pullCctv.lookbackTurns`. A sighting drawn from the trail carries `UNKNOWN_TRAVEL_MODE`, because the trail records where the criminal was and not how it was travelling, and `report.ts` already had that value for exactly this. `PULL_CCTV.delay` and `actions.test.ts` were the only other things touched, as the task said they would be.
  - **The criminal's meters advance in consequences, not in resolution.** DESIGN.md puts meter updates in consequences, and `nextPressure` already reads that phase's fired events, so the criminal's action is carried out of resolution as data (`Resolution = { world, action }`) rather than re-derived from comparing two positions. That distinction is load-bearing: **a move a checkpoint turned back still costs `staminaPerMove`**, because the walking happened, and comparing before-and-after nodes would have refunded it. Stamina is a `Record<CriminalAction["kind"], ...>` table, so a new criminal action fails to compile until it says what it costs (architecture rule 6).
  - **Heat is read from the reports, not from the criminal's position.** `heatPerSighting` is paid for every report **observed this turn** whose `truth` is `"true"` and whose content is a `sighting`; a mistaken witness, a prank and an empty look are all worth nothing, and a real sighting is paid on the turn it was observed rather than the turn it lands, so a delayed CCTV pull does not heat the criminal twice. Heat is how recognisable the criminal has become, which is a fact about who saw it, not about where it stood. Decay applies every turn, all three meters are clamped for `nextPressure`'s reason, and desperation only ever climbs.
  - **Two M3.8a/M3.8b-1 tests were falsified by this task and deliberately replaced.** `the resolution phase > leaves the criminal exactly where it was` and `leaves the criminal's own meters to the turn the criminal acts on (PLAN M3.8b)` both pinned the stub and are false by construction now; they are replaced by 15 resolution tests and 11 meter tests that assert the behaviour they were holding the place for. One intel test also needed a new fixture rather than a weaker assertion: `changes nothing in the world but the reports and the stream` had filed a true sighting observed on the current turn, which now legitimately raises heat, so it files a late-delivered clearance instead and compares against a quiet turn taken from the post-draw stream position - a **stricter** assertion than the one it replaces, with no `rng` escape hatch.
  - **A hunter-visible event for a roadblock hit was considered and not added.** It would be the natural evidence for the heatmap, and it is exactly the kind of leak architecture rule 4 exists to stop: "your checkpoint was hit" tells the hunter which edge the criminal is standing at. Whether the hunter is told at all is a design question this task cannot answer alone (DESIGN.md's report list does not include it), so it is an Inbox entry rather than a guess.
  - `turn.ts`, `criminal.ts`, `actions.ts` and `limits.ts` are all at 100% statements, branches, functions and lines. `core` overall 99.52% statements / 95.85% branches, up from 99.5 / 95.62.
