Re-derive both seeds from a fresh sweep if you retune; do not widen the assertions.
  - **Decision: `outcomeBase.timed_out` stays at the provisional 150, because the measurement says no legal value of it moves a single score.** At slip 0.7 over the 400-hunt sweep all 46 `greedy_roadblock` stalemates score exactly `minimumScore` at base 0, 150 and 289 alike. A stalemate always pays the full `time.maxTurns * score.turnPenalty` = 360 in clock penalty, and `balance.test.ts`'s "the worst capture beats the best ending short of one" invariant caps this row at 289, so every admissible value floors. The tests pin it to the open interval (0, 289) from both directions and nothing else can. 150 is retained rather than tuned: it is the middle of the interval the invariants leave, and there is no behaviour to tune it against until the score floor changes (see Inbox).
  - **Mutation check: 11 mutants, 10 killed, 1 survivor.** Killed - slip 0.58 (slow, worked-capture band), 0.6 (core, `holds a criminal it cannot take until the deadline runs out`), 0.68 (core, same test), 0.78 (slow, worked band), 0.8 (core, `ends a hunt the hunter works the checkpoints in, with the criminal taken`), 0.2 (core `holds a criminal...` plus slow's worked band and hunt-length test), 1 (three core tests including `leaves a checkpoint both a way through and a way to take somebody`, plus 5 of 8 slow tests), `timed_out` base 0 (core, `scores only a capture and a stalemate on the outcome alone` and `ranks a hunt that ran out of clock below one that was won`), base 300 (core, that same ranking test and `makes the worst capture beat the best ending short of one`), and the `turn.ts` blocked-edge guard inverted (31 core tests including all three changed here). Survivor - slip 0.75 passes both core (120/120 in the file) and slow (8/8); justified because 0.75 still produces the behaviour the targets state (51.7% worked capture, inside the 50-70% band), and a band on behaviour is not meant to kill a setting that satisfies it.
  - **`packages/core/src/balance.ts` changed in its comment only; no balance value moved in this task.** The `ROADBLOCK` doc comment now records the measured window edges (0.58 and 0.78) and the passing window [0.60, 0.75] in place of the earlier unsupported "[0.62, 0.72]" claim, discloses that the aimless band is a guard rail rather than a constraint that chose the number, and adds the finding that the knob moves how long a containment lasts rather than whether it holds.
  - **`docs/PLAN.md`'s M3.11 note is stale from this commit on.** It reads "All seven `ESCAPE_SEEDS` end `captured` under it"; the correct statement is that all seven end with the escape stopped, and six of seven end `captured`, the seventh (`SEED = 7`) `timed_out` with the criminal contained. The note was not edited here - PLAN.md belongs to the maintainer.
  - **Two of the three Inbox entries that landed on this task are closed by the measurements; the maintainer can drop them.** *Every hunt ends in about five turns*: a worked hunt now runs a mean 8.56 turns against an unworked 4.65, and 46 of 400 run the full 24-turn deadline, regression-tested by `runs a worked hunt well past the five turns an unworked one takes`. *A hunter who does nothing outscores every bot that plays*: `greedy_roadblock` now averages 695.2 against doing nothing's 51.8 (13.4x) and `random` 87.3, pinned by `pays a hunter who works the case a multiple of one who sits on it`. Both have a residual that belongs to `heatmap_chaser` rather than to the balance - it still ends at 4.64 turns and still scores below doing nothing - which is the Inbox entry below. The third entry, losses all flooring at `minimumScore`, stays open with sharper evidence and is restated below rather than fixed here.
  - **`bun run verify` passes with zero warnings: typecheck clean across three workspaces, Biome clean over 124 files, 51 test files and 919 tests in a 23s test step.** Baseline on the last clean tree was 50 files / 909 tests; the predecessor's work took it to 51 / 918 with 2 failing; this task's net is +1 test (one seven-seed test replaced by two) and 0 failing. Coverage on both touched files is 100% statements, branches, functions and lines (`balance.ts` 11/11 statements, `turn.ts` 84/84 statements and 23/23 branches); repository total 99.6% statements, 96.69% branches. No `sim` source changed, so M4.2c's `sim` figures stand and no throwaway coverage config was needed. Nothing committed, nothing pushed, `packages/sim/src/bots/**` untouched.

## M5 — Web UI (dispatch style)

There is no M5.1 and no M4.0: the belief heatmap is **M3.6b**, because it is state `WorldState` carries and M3.8a advances, and M4.3 tunes against it.

Playwright covers **M5.5's turn flow and M5.6a's finish, and nothing else** ("Decisions"): AGENTS.md limits e2e to critical flows, so every other assertion here runs under the jsdom environment M5.2 installs.

- [x] **M5.2 Game store**
  deps: M3.8c, M3.1b, M3.2
  M3.2 added to deps at the pre-M2.2 review: the store exposes `HunterView`, and no chain of its other deps reaches `toHunterView`. M5.4 gets it transitively through here.
  Zustand store holding the sealed world privately and exposing only `HunterView` + dispatch functions.
  **The original wording, "holding `WorldState` privately", cannot be implemented**: architecture rule 4 forbids `apps/web` from importing `WorldState` and `biome.json` already blocks it by name, so the task as first written would not lint. The store holds `SealedWorld` from M3.1b - opaque data it can keep and hand back to `core`, never read - alongside the action log M5.6b's share link needs. Dispatch calls `step` on the `GameLogic` wired at the composition root and handed to the store, rather than importing it (wiring decision below); the store holds data and calls logic, it does not become logic (engineering principle 1).
  Wire it in `apps/web/src/main.tsx`, the composition root (M0.4's note), not inside a component.
  This task also owns the **DOM test environment**, which the Inbox flagged and nothing had claimed: switch the `web` Vitest project to `environment: "jsdom"` and add `jsdom`, the one new dependency. Assert through `react-dom/client` and DOM queries. Do not add a component-testing library unless a test genuinely needs user-event semantics, and justify it in the note if you do; Playwright still owns the end-to-end flows.
  AC: components cannot access `WorldState` via exported types; a type-level test asserts `SealedWorld` exposes no members; store tests run under jsdom.
  Notes:
  - **The store holds a `Hunt | null`, and the world and the view move together inside it.** `Hunt` is `{ world: SealedWorld, view: HunterView, seed, setup, recordedActions }`: `seed` and `setup` sit beside the log because M5.6b's share link needs all three at once (`makeReplay({ seed, setup, actions })`, M3.9's note), and one object rather than five loose members makes a view that predates the world it was taken from unrepresentable. **The view is held, not derived in a selector.** Deriving would call `toHunterView` on every render and return a fresh object each time, which is exactly what Zustand's reference equality over `useSyncExternalStore` cannot see through - M5.4's feed would re-render on every unrelated set - and it would make reading the store a computation rather than a read. Projecting once per transition, in the one place the world changes, is the same trade `core` already makes.
  - **`createGameStore({ game, turn }, balance)`: deps are logic and only logic, and the balance is initial state rather than a closure capture.** The store never calls `create*`; `main.tsx` wires all fifteen factories and hands it a `GameLogic` and a `TurnLogic` (the wiring decision below). Every transition is a module-level pure function `(deps, state, input) => Pick<GameStoreState, "hunt" | "refusal" | "rejections">` and the dispatch is `set(started(deps, get(), request))`, so the store holds data and calls logic and is not itself logic (engineering principle 1). Seeding the balance into state rather than capturing it is M3.10's rule applied one layer out: every turn of a hunt is played under the numbers the hunt was created with, and there is no second copy to drift. `toHunterView` is imported directly, which is the line the wiring decision draws between `create*Logic` and the bare `make*`/projection functions.
  - **Every refusal is held, never thrown and never swallowed, and planning rejections are a separate member from them.** `StoreRefusal` reuses `core`'s own variants - `Exclude<GameResult, { kind: "game" }> | Exclude<TurnResult, TurnTaken>` - so a new refusal on either side arrives here without an edit, plus two the store raises before it calls anything (`no_hunt`, `too_many_recorded_turns`). `state.rejections` carries M3.8b's `PlanningRejection[]` and is cleared at the start of every turn that plays. The split matters to M5.5: a refusal means the turn did not happen and the queue was not recorded, a rejection means the turn happened and one row of the queue did not, and "validation errors shown inline" is the second. Four mutants confirm it - swallowing a refused `step`, recording its queue anyway, dropping `no_hunt`, and accumulating rejections across turns each fail a named test.
  - **The action log does need a count bound: `apps/web/src/limits.ts` gains `maxRecordedTurns = 240`, equal to `core`'s `LIMITS.maxGameTurns`.** A hunt is only stopped by an end condition, and the deadline is not one (the standing Inbox entry), so `in_progress` is a fixed point the player can keep pressing End Turn against and the log is the thing that grows - AGENTS.md section 5's "max actions in a replay" applied to the only place that accumulates. 240 is not a new number: it is what `replayRefusalIn` bounds a recorded turn list by, so a longer log is one no replay could carry. The turn over the cap is **refused, not truncated**, and the log is left intact. Testing it needed a hand-written `TurnLogic` that echoes the world it is handed, because the shipped engine ends every MVP hunt in about five turns and `web` cannot construct a `SealedWorld` of its own; that is a fake wired through the same factory seam, not `vi.mock`.
  - **All three acceptance criteria are checks that fail when broken, and each was proved by mutation.** "Components cannot access `WorldState`" is `biome.json`'s existing `apps/web` deny list plus a **new `.tsx` fixture** in `tools/biome/architecture-rules.test.ts` (`web_component_world_state`): the override always covered `apps/web/**/*.tsx`, but every fixture was a `.ts` module, so nothing proved the rule reached a component. `Fixture` gains an optional `extension` for it. Narrowing the override's includes to `.ts` fails that case with "biome processed no such file". The type-level half is `AssertTrue<IsNever<Extract<keyof T, string>>>` over `SealedWorld`, over `Hunt["world"]` as the store actually declares it, and over what `useGameStore` hands a component; `GameStoreState` is separately asserted to expose no `world`/`criminal`/`config` of its own. Aliasing `SealedWorld` to `WorldState`, intersecting the stored world with a readable field, and adding a bare `world` to the state each fail `bun run typecheck` at a named line. "Store tests run under jsdom" is the `web` Vitest project's `environment: "jsdom"`; setting it back to `node` fails four tests. Four more mutants over the store's behaviour were run and all were caught.
  - **`zustand@5.0.15` and `jsdom@30.1.0`, both exact and both in `apps/web/package.json`** (zustand a dependency, jsdom a devDependency), checked against the registry rather than recalled. **No component-testing library was added**: `react-dom/client` plus `act` and `container.querySelector` is enough for a store nothing types into, and Playwright still owns the two end-to-end flows. Vite and Vitest did not move - `node_modules/.bun` holds exactly one `vite@`.
  - **The React seam is `storecontext.tsx`, not the store file**: `GameStoreProvider store={store}` (React 19's context-as-provider, no `.Provider`) and `useGameStore(selector)`, which throws if it is read outside a provider rather than falling back to a module-level store. That is what keeps the composition root the only place a store is made, and it is what lets a test render the same components against a store wired with fakes.
  - **What M5.3a, M5.4, M5.5 and M5.6b must know.** Read through `useGameStore((state) => ...)` with the narrowest selector that will do; `state.hunt` is `null` before `start` and every component has to render that case. M5.3a and M5.4 want `state.hunt.view` and nothing else - the sealed world is on the same object and has no member to reach for. M5.5 owns both error surfaces: `state.rejections` for the row the player got wrong, `state.refusal` for the dispatch that did not happen, and it should narrow on `kind` exhaustively because the union grows from `core`. M5.6b has `seed`, `setup` and `recordedActions` sitting together on the hunt, which is `makeReplay`'s whole argument. Nobody should add a member to `GameStoreState` that reads the world; the type-level assertions exist to stop it.
  - `bun run verify` passes in 12.4s; 787 tests (was 770) in 45 files (was 43), 113 files checked by Biome (was 109), zero lint output. `core` coverage is unchanged at 99.59% statements / 96.68% branches, since nothing in `core` changed. `bun run build` still emits a static `dist/index.html` plus one 258.41 kB chunk (82.06 kB gzipped; zustand is about 1 kB of it), and `bun run test:e2e` still passes - `App.tsx` was deliberately left untouched, so the shell spec asserts the same title against a tree that now has a provider around it.


- [x] **M5.3a Map renderer (SVG)**
  deps: M5.2
  `MapRenderer` component: nodes, typed edges, exits, roadblocks, selection. Everything the map is made of, drawn from `HunterView`, with no overlay.
  Split out of M5.3 in review: the base map and the belief overlay have different inputs, different dependencies and different failure modes, and together they were the largest UI task in the plan. The `MapRenderer` seam AGENTS.md names (PixiJS may replace it later) is defined here.
  Draws `MapGraph.river` too: M2.1c put it on the graph specifically so the renderer could have it, and an AC that does not name it is how it gets silently dropped a second time.
  AC: under jsdom, clicking a node selects it, node count and edge count match the view, and a view with a river renders it. Not Playwright ("Decisions"): this is a rendering assertion, not a critical flow.
  Notes:
  - **`MapRenderer` is presentational and controlled; `MapPanel` is the only store-aware half.** Props are `{ view, selection, onSelect, overlay?, theme? }` - all data plus the two seams a renderer cannot avoid - so a PixiJS renderer satisfies the same contract. **Selection is UI state owned above the map**, not in the store and not in the component: `MapSelection` is `{ kind: "node" | "edge" }` and M5.5's action panel has to read the same value to build an action, so the screen that holds both owns it. Edge selection is implemented as well as node, because roadblock is the MVP action that ends hunts and it targets an edge.
  - **The overlay seam is ready for M5.3b**: `overlay` renders into a `<g>` after the river and before the edges and nodes, with `pointer-events: none` already set and tested, which settles M5.3b's "does not intercept clicks" AC in advance. Query hooks are `data-testid` (`map`, `map-node`, `map-edge`, `map-river`, `map-selection`, `map-overlay`) plus `data-nodeid`/`data-edgeid`/`data-edgekind`/`data-exit`/`data-incident`/`data-blocked`/`data-selected`.
  - **Two deviations, both deliberate.** Edge kinds and district types are keyed records, not `switch`es with a `never` check: the compile-time guarantee is the same and architecture rule 6 asks for a data table (the `SvgTheme` precedent in M2.4). Markers are ARIA `option`s in one `listbox` rather than `role="button"`, because SVG has no native button and Biome rejects both `role="button"` on a `<g>` (`useSemanticElements`) and no role at all (`noStaticElementInteractions`); `option` is also the role that carries `aria-selected`.
  - `bun run verify` passes in 23.0s; 938 tests (was 923) in 54 files (was 52), 129 files checked by Biome, zero lint output. 13 mutants run, 13 killed. `bun run build` emits a bundle with the same hash as before the task - the renderer is not in the production graph until M5.5 wires it - and `bun run test:e2e` still passes.

- [x] **M5.3b Heatmap overlay**
  deps: M5.3a
  The belief distribution drawn over the base map, behind the nodes and edges.
  It reads `HunterView.belief` (M3.6b), so the overlay is a pure render of a field the store already has; there is no separate distribution to fetch or accumulate. Do not snapshot-test the SVG (AGENTS.md "Testing expectations"); assert on the mapping from probability to the rendered attribute instead, under jsdom.
  AC: a node with zero belief renders no heat; the highest-belief node renders the strongest; the overlay does not intercept clicks meant for nodes.
  Notes:
  - **The ramp is normalised against the busiest cell, not against probability 1**, and that is the decision M5.7's visual pass tunes against. A hunt's mass is spread over tens of nodes, so the strongest cell on a 48-node map is rarely above a few per cent and an absolute ramp would draw a city of near-invisible dots. It costs the reading "how sure is the hunter overall", which no pixel could carry legibly, and buys the one the player acts on: which node is the best guess now. Nothing live is invisible - the faintest cell still draws at `minOpacity` - and **zero is absent rather than transparent**: `heatOf` returns `null` below `BELIEF_MASS_TOLERANCE` (core's own constant, not a float compared to 0) and `BeliefOverlay` renders no layer when no cell has heat, so a cold node has no element over it at all.
  - **`heatOf(ramp, mass, peak)` is the whole of the mapping and is asserted directly**, never through a snapshot of the SVG: zero is no heat, the peak is the top of the ramp, and a fast-check property pins monotonicity. The rendering tests only check that the mapping reaches `fill-opacity`, `r` and `cx`/`cy`. A cell naming a node the map does not have is dropped **before** the peak is taken, so a stale cell cannot dim every node that is on the map.
  - **Composed through M5.3a's existing seam; neither `maprenderer.tsx` nor `mappanel.tsx` was edited.** `BeliefOverlay({ belief, nodes, ramp? })` draws into the `map-overlay` layer that already takes no pointer events, and a test clicks a node through real heat to pin the AC end to end. `BeliefOverlayPanel` is the connected half (the `meters.tsx`/`meterspanel.tsx` split), so **M5.6b composes by passing both nodes into the one overlay slot** - `overlay={<><BeliefOverlayPanel /><CriminalPath ... /></>}` - and the map needs no second overlay prop. Test ids are `belief-overlay` and `belief-heat`, with `data-nodeid`/`data-intensity`.
  - **`DEFAULT_HEAT_RAMP` is the `DEFAULT_MAP_THEME` pattern**: one exported `HeatRamp` table (fill, radius ends, opacity ends), passed as an optional prop rather than captured, so M5.7 lifts it into `theme.ts` by moving one literal. Its red is `DEFAULT_MAP_THEME.incidentStroke`, so there is one red to tune, and interpolation is linear between the two ends.
  - `bun run verify` passes in 23.5s; 986 tests (was 964) in 60 files (was 58), 141 files checked by Biome, zero lint output. 7 mutants run, 7 killed. `bun run build` still leaves the overlay out of the bundle (nothing imports it until M5.5) and `bun run test:e2e` still passes.

  deps: M5.2

  AC: renders from view; new reports highlighted.
  Notes:
  - **Presentational plus connected, per M5.3a.** `ReportFeed({ reports, currentTurn, maxEntries? })` and `Meters({ view, bounds })` hold no state and never touch the store; `ReportFeedPanel` and `MetersPanel` take no props, read the view off the store and render `null` before `start`. `MetersPanel` uses two selectors rather than one composite, so neither subscription allocates.
  - **Meter maximums arrive as data.** `MeterBounds` is a structural subset of `balance.hunter` that the panel reads off the store, so `Meters` holds no number of its own; the clock is the exception and takes its range from the view (`clock.turn` of `clock.turn + turnsRemaining`). `METER_KINDS` is exported data and `MeterKind` derives from it, so a test asserts every kind reaches the DOM.
  - **The feed enforces `LIMITS.maxReportsInFeed` itself** (a default prop, so every caller is bounded, M5.6b included) by keeping the newest N and rendering a `data-withheld` count. Truncate-and-declare rather than refuse: AGENTS.md's "oversized input is an error" bounds input crossing a trust boundary, whereas this is in-memory data `core` produced, already bounded by the hunt's turn count, and refusing would cost the player the intel the screen exists for. Ordering is a stable sort on `receivedAtTurn` alone, and `HunterView.events` is deliberately unrendered - nothing in the feed reads or could read a report's nature.
  - `bun run verify` passes in 23.0s; 964 tests (was 938) in 58 files (was 54), zero lint output. 9 mutants run, 9 killed. `bun run build` is unchanged (the components are not in the production graph until M5.5) and `bun run test:e2e` still passes.

- [x] **M5.5a Action panel and target selection**
  deps: M5.3b, M5.4
  The dispatch screen itself, and the half of the turn that happens before the player commits: choose an action, pick its target on the map, see what it would cost.
  **This is the first half of M5.5's "split before starting"** (see Inbox). It owns the screen assembly - `App.tsx` and whatever layout component it needs - because selection is UI state owned above the map (M5.3a's note) and the action panel and `MapPanel` have to read the same value. M5.3a implemented edge selection as well as node selection for exactly this: roadblock targets an edge.
  The MVP action set is DESIGN.md's subset: roadblock, canvass, pull CCTV, true briefing. Read `targetKindOf` and `actionCostOf` from `core/src/actions.ts` rather than restating either; an action the player cannot afford is rejected at planning time (DESIGN.md "End conditions": budget is not an end condition).
  AC: under jsdom, selecting an action narrows the map to the targets that action accepts, and an action plus a target of the wrong kind cannot be built. No Playwright here - the turn flow is M5.5b's.
  Notes:
  - **Selection lives in `DispatchScreen` as two `useState` values**, `armed` and `selection`; everything else on screen is derived per render. They are component state, not store state, because they die with the turn and nothing outside the screen reads them - if M5.5b needs the queue to survive a remount, the *queue* goes in the store and these two stay put.
  - **The map is narrowed by the owner, not the renderer.** `MapRenderer` was not changed and has no prop for restricting targets: it reports every click, `DispatchScreen.select` drops one the armed action does not accept, and `arm` drops a held selection that no longer fits. The invariant is that `selection` is always a target the armed action accepts, which is why `draft` has no half-built state. If M5.7 wants dimmed rather than merely inert nodes, that is an additive `selectableKind` prop on the renderer; the narrowing logic stays here.
  - **`draft: HunterAction | null` is M5.5b's seam** - the queue takes exactly that value and `store.endTurn(queue)` takes the array. `state.rejections` and `state.refusal` are deliberately unread here; they are M5.5b's inline error surfaces.
  - **The wrong-kind-target guard is type-level with a test backstop.** `HunterAction`'s union makes the malformed value unconstructible, `ActionBuildTable` is a mapped type so each builder returns only its own action and a fifth kind in `core` is a compile error here, and `buildAction` returns `HunterAction | null` so no caller can skip the check. Costs and target kinds come from `actionCostOf`/`targetKindOf` and are restated nowhere in `web`; an unaffordable action renders disabled, not hidden, because a player who cannot see the price cannot plan around it.
  - **`App.tsx` now renders `DispatchScreen`**, which is `NewHuntForm` until a hunt exists. The new-hunt surface is a difficulty and a seed only, with grid and deadline from `balance` (the same call `sim`'s `setupFrom` makes) and the start hour and profile left to the seed, which is what keeps a shared replay link from spoiling the hunt it replays. `App.tsx` stays the only `<h1>`, so `e2e/app-shell.spec.ts` still resolves one heading and was not touched.
  - **The typed seed is a new I/O boundary**: `LIMITS.maxSeedInputLength = 10`, checked before parsing and refused rather than truncated, with the field carrying no HTML `maxLength` so the refusal stays reachable. The composition block the four panel tests each copied now lives in `apps/web/src/wiring.testfixture.ts`, outside the Vitest include glob; it exports wiring only, because setup literals belong beside the assertions.
  - `bun run verify` passes; 1027 tests (was 986) in 64 files (was 60), 150 files checked by Biome, zero lint output. `bun run test:e2e` passes unweakened. **This is the task that pulls M5.3a, M5.3b and M5.4 into the production graph**: the bundle goes from 258.41 kB / 82.06 kB gzipped to 272.24 kB / 87.12 kB, the first move since M5.2.

- [x] **M5.5b Queue and end turn**
  deps: M5.5a
  Queue the chosen actions, end the turn, show validation errors inline.
  **This is the second half of M5.5's split.** It owns both error surfaces the M5.2 note names: `state.rejections` for the row the player got wrong and `state.refusal` for the dispatch that did not happen, narrowed exhaustively on `kind` because the union grows from `core`.
  AC: Playwright: start game -> roadblock -> end turn -> turn counter advances. This is one of the two flows Playwright keeps ("Decisions"); the other is M5.6a's finish.
  Notes:
  - **The queue is component state in `TurnQueuePanel`, not store state.** It dies with the turn and nothing outside the dispatch screen reads it, which is the test M5.5a's note set; `armed` and `selection` stay in `DispatchScreen` beside it. The screen assembly grew a third connected panel rather than more handlers because `DispatchScreen` was already close to Biome's `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` ceiling - `TurnQueuePanel` and `DispatchErrorsPanel` are the same presentational/connected split `mappanel.tsx` makes.
  - **A refused turn keeps the staged queue; only a played turn clears it.** A `StoreRefusal` means nothing was recorded (M5.2's note), so the rows are still the player's. The handler cannot learn that from a selector - the subscribed value is from the render already over - so `storecontext.tsx` now also exports **`useGameStoreApi()`**, returning the store itself, and `endTurn` reads `store.getState().refusal` back immediately after dispatching. M5.6a's finish button will want the same seam.
  - **Inline rejections are drawn against `hunt.recordedActions.at(-1)`, not against the live queue.** `PlanningRejection.index` names a position in the *submitted* queue, and the screen empties its queue the moment the turn plays, so rendering against the store's own record of what was sent is what stops the two drifting. `DispatchErrorsPanel` uses three non-allocating selectors and a module-level `lastDispatch`, per M5.4's note on composite selectors.
  - **Both error surfaces are exhaustive `switch`es with a `never` check**: `refusalMessage` over all six `StoreRefusal` variants (`deadline_too_long`, `generation_failed`, `too_many_actions`, `hunt_over`, `no_hunt`, `too_many_recorded_turns`) and `rejectionMessage` over all six `ActionRejection` variants. `dispatcherrors.test.tsx` writes one of every variant out longhand, so a seventh in `core` is a compile error in the test as well as in the source. `DispatchErrorsPanel` renders in the pre-hunt branch too, because `deadline_too_long` and `generation_failed` leave `hunt` null and would otherwise be a start button that silently does nothing.
  - **The queue is a new I/O boundary: `LIMITS.maxQueuedActions = 32` in `apps/web/src/limits.ts`**, checked in `enqueue` before the row is taken and refused with `{ kind: "too_many_queued", queued, maxQueued }` rather than truncated. Kept equal to `core`'s `LIMITS.maxQueuedActions` on purpose: a queue this side accepted and `step` refused would cost the player the whole turn instead of one row. Nothing enforces the agreement - see Inbox.
  - **`actionqueue.ts` is pure data plus pure functions** (`enqueue`, `removeAt`, `actionSummary`, `queueRows`), the way `actiondraft.ts` is; `turnqueue.tsx` is words and markup only. `queueRows` exists because an ordered queue has no identity but position, and `key={row.index}` from a row object is what satisfies `noArrayIndexKey` honestly rather than by suppression. `actionSummary` reads its label from `ACTION_PRESENTATION`, and `GLOBAL_TARGET_LABEL` ("the whole city") is now exported from `actionpanel.tsx` so the board and the queue cannot name the same action two things.
  - **Ending a turn with an empty queue is a move, not a mistake** - waiting out the hour for reports already in flight - so `end-turn` is never disabled. Test ids added for M5.6a and M5.7 to reuse: `turn-queue`, `queue-row`, `queue-add`, `queue-remove`, `queue-refusal`, `end-turn`, `dispatch-errors`, `dispatch-refusal`, `dispatch-rejection`.
  - **`e2e/turn.spec.ts` is the second and last Playwright spec before M5.6a's.** It repeats its test ids as local constants rather than importing them: the owning modules are `.tsx` and `tsconfig.tools.json`, which type-checks `e2e/`, has no JSX setting. It clicks the edge with `dispatchEvent("click")` because an edge marker is an SVG `<g>` around a `<line>` whose box can be a pixel tall, which Playwright's actionability check reads as hidden. It asserts the picked road reads `data-blocked="true"` afterwards, so the spec fails if the queue never reaches `core` rather than merely emptying.
  - `bun run verify` passes with zero warnings; 1082 tests (was 1027) in 67 files (was 64), 159 files checked by Biome (was 150). 14 mutants run, 14 killed (12 under Vitest, 2 under Playwright). `bun run test:e2e` passes, 2 specs. `bun run build` goes from 272.24 kB / 87.12 kB gzipped to 276.51 kB / 88.52 kB.

- [x] **M5.6a End screen**
  deps: M3.10, M5.5b
  The screen a finished hunt lands on: outcome and the M3.10 score breakdown, component by component rather than one number.
  Split out of M5.6 in review: the end screen needs scoring, the replay viewer needs the replay format and the overlay, and the share link is the only part of the three that crosses an I/O boundary.
  AC: Playwright: finish a seeded game and see the breakdown - the second of the two flows Playwright keeps. That every score component the breakdown carries is rendered, so adding one to `balance.ts` cannot silently go missing, is asserted under jsdom, where it is exhaustive and cheap.
  Notes:
  - **`Hunt` gained a fourth projected field, `score: ScoreBreakdown`, computed every transition exactly like `view` is.** `GameStoreDeps` gained `scoring: ScoringLogic`, wired at `main.tsx` beside `game` and `turn`. `started` and `ended` both call `deps.scoring.score({ world: result.world, balance: state.balance })`; scoring an in-progress world is not a special case, so `EndScreenPanel` reads a value that is always there.
  - **The end screen is `endscreen.tsx` (presentational) + `endscreenpanel.tsx` (connected), the `meters.tsx`/`meterspanel.tsx` split.** `EndScreen({ breakdown: ScoreBreakdown })` returns `null` if `breakdown.outcome.kind === "in_progress"`, so `EndScreenPanel` is one selector. `FinishedOutcome = Exclude<GameOutcome, { kind: "in_progress" }>`.
  - **Every score component is rendered by iterating `breakdown.components`, keyed against `SCORE_COMPONENT_LABELS: Readonly<Record<ScoreComponentKind, string>>`** - a lookup table, not a `switch`. A fifth component landing in `core/src/score.ts` is a compile error here until labelled, which is the AC's "cannot silently go missing" at compile time; `endscreen.test.tsx` proves the same thing at test time with a `Readonly<Record<ScoreComponentKind, number>>` fixture. `OUTCOME_LABELS` is the same table over `FinishedOutcome["kind"]`.
  - **`DispatchScreen` now swaps to `EndScreenPanel` once `state.hunt.view.outcome.kind !== "in_progress"`, closing the M5.5b Inbox entry** ("nothing tells the dispatch screen the hunt is over except a refusal"). Once swapped the board - map, action panel, queue, End Turn - is gone; `hunt_over` stays a defensive case in `StoreRefusal`/`dispatcherrors.tsx` for anything that calls `endTurn` after the fact some other way.
  - **The Playwright seed is `"1"`, the new-hunt form's own default, chosen by measurement**: it escapes on turn 4 with no hunter actions at all (seeds 1-40 all escaped inside 3-6 turns). `e2e/end.spec.ts` loops End Turn up to 8 times - headroom over the measured 4 - checking for `end-screen` before each click, asserting `data-outcome="escaped"` and at least one `score-component`. The real exhaustiveness check is `endscreen.test.tsx` under jsdom, per AGENTS.md's e2e-for-critical-flows-only rule.
  - **No new `limits.ts` entry.** The end screen renders data already in memory and crosses no boundary.
  - **Test ids for M5.6b to reuse**: `end-screen`, `end-screen-outcome` (`data-outcome`, `data-turn`), `score-breakdown`, `score-base`, `score-component` (`data-kind`, `data-measured`, `data-points`), `score-total`.
  - `bun run verify` passes with zero warnings: 1090 tests (was 1082) in 69 files (was 67), 164 files checked by Biome (was 159). 5 mutants run, 5 killed. `bun run test:e2e` passes, 3 specs (was 2). `bun run build` goes from 276.51 kB / 88.52 kB gzipped to 278.96 kB / 89.24 kB.

- [x] **M5.6b-1 Replay scrubber**
  deps: M5.6a, M3.9, M5.3b
  Replay scrubber showing the criminal's true path over the heatmap, from `RevealFrame`s.
  **First half of M5.6b's split** (see Inbox: "Four tasks are marked 'split before starting'"). The scrubber is one session; the share link and its URL boundary are M5.6b-2.
  The scrubber renders `RevealFrame`s and never `WorldState` (architecture rule 4). `M5.3b`'s overlay is the dep that makes "true path over the heatmap" mean anything.
  AC: under jsdom, scrubbing to a turn shows the criminal's position for it. Not Playwright - the two e2e flows are M5.5's turn and M5.6a's finish.
  Notes:
  - **`Hunt` gained a fifth field, `frames: readonly RevealFrame[] | null`, but it is *not* projected every transition the way `view` and `score` are.** `playback.ts`'s `frameOf` unseals whatever world the replay reaches, including the live world if the replay is shorter than the turns actually played, so calling `playback.play` while the hunt is still `in_progress` would hand the UI the criminal's current position mid-hunt - a correctness violation of architecture rule 4, not just a performance one. `framesFor` (`gamestore.ts`) is called from both `started` and `ended` but early-returns `null` unless the *new* view's outcome has already settled, so the replay plays back exactly once per hunt, right when it ends.
  - **`GameStoreDeps` gained `playback: PlaybackLogic`, wired at `main.tsx` beside `game`/`turn`/`scoring` via `createPlaybackLogic({ game, turn })`.**
  - **`frames`, `seed`, `setup` and `recordedActions` all still live on `Hunt` exactly where M5.2 and M5.6a's notes said they would.** M5.6b-2 reads `hunt.seed`/`hunt.setup`/`hunt.recordedActions` for `makeReplay`, the same way `framesFor` does; it does not need `hunt.frames` itself, which is single-purpose for the scrubber.
  - **The overlay composition M5.3b's note reserved (`overlay={<><BeliefOverlayPanel /><CriminalPath ... /></>}`) is now real**, with `CriminalPathPanel` handed a `turn` prop rather than reading it off the store, the same shape `MapPanel`'s `selection` prop uses. `CriminalPath` (`criminalpath.tsx`) is a pure function of `{frames, nodes, turn}`; it draws a trail (`<polyline>`, turns 0..scrubbed) and a marker (`<circle>`, the scrubbed turn alone). `trailPositionsOf`/`markerOf` are exported and asserted directly, the `heatOf` precedent.
  - **`positionIndexOf` moved out of `maprenderer.tsx` and `beliefoverlay.tsx` into a new shared `apps/web/src/mapnodes.ts`**, closing the M5.3b Inbox entry that named this task as the trigger.
  - **A new screen-assembly component, `replayscreen.tsx` (`ReplayScreen`, the `DispatchScreen` shape, not `x`/`xpanel`)**, owns the currently-scrubbed `turn` (plain `useState`, defaults to 0), because both `ReplayScrubber` and `CriminalPathPanel` need the same value. It reuses `MapPanel` unedited with a dummy `selection`/`onSelect` and renders the *hunt's final* `BeliefOverlayPanel` heat, not a per-turn heatmap - only the criminal's marker moves as the turn is scrubbed (see Inbox). `ReplayScreen` renders `null` until `hunt.frames !== null`.
  - **`DispatchScreen` renders `<><EndScreenPanel /><ReplayScreen /></>` once the hunt is over**, a one-line addition; `endscreen.tsx`/`endscreenpanel.tsx` themselves are untouched.
  - **`ReplayScrubber` is a plain controlled `<input type="range">` plus a turn indicator, holding no state of its own.** No new `limits.ts` entry: the slider's `min`/`max` come from `frames.length`, already bounded by `core`, and a native range input cannot itself produce an out-of-range value.
  - **Test ids added**: `criminal-path`, `criminal-path-trail`, `criminal-path-marker` (`data-nodeid`, `data-turn`); `replay-scrubber`, `replay-scrubber-input`, `replay-scrubber-turn` (`data-turn`); `replay-screen`.
  - `bun run verify` passes with zero warnings: 1122 tests (was 1090) in 74 files (was 69), 174 files checked by Biome (was 164). 12 mutants run, 10 killed outright, 1 killed after strengthening a test, 1 confirmed equivalent. `bun run test:e2e` passes unchanged, 3 specs. `bun run build` goes from 278.96 kB / 89.24 kB gzipped to 282.19 kB / 90.22 kB.

- [x] **M5.6b-2 Share link**
  deps: M5.6b-1
  Shareable replay link that round-trips through the URL.
  **Second half of M5.6b's split.** This task owns the **URL boundary** in `web`, and `apps/web/src/limits.ts` already holds its bound: reject anything longer than `LIMITS.maxReplayStringLength` before decoding, not after. M3.9 bounds the same string on the `core` side; both need the over-the-limit test, because they are two different boundaries (a hostile URL versus a malformed replay).
  AC: under jsdom, a URL one character over the limit is rejected with an error, not truncated or parsed. Not Playwright - the two e2e flows are M5.5's turn and M5.6a's finish.
  Notes:
  - **The `LIMITS` duplication is settled by exporting `core`'s `LIMITS` and having `web` read it, per M3.9's own lean.** `packages/core/src/index.ts` now exports `LIMITS`; `apps/web/src/limits.ts` reads `CORE_LIMITS.maxReplayStringLength`, `CORE_LIMITS.maxGameTurns` (for `maxRecordedTurns`) and `CORE_LIMITS.maxQueuedActions` directly instead of carrying its own copies, so the three cannot drift apart again. This makes a `core` limit part of `web`'s public dependency surface, which is the cost M3.9's note named; it was taken because it is strictly smaller than a second test asserting agreement, and `LIMITS` is not a hidden-state type (architecture rule 4's restricted-import list is untouched).
  - **Only three of the five duplication-flavored Inbox entries actually had a `core` constant to import.** `maxReportsInFeed` (M5.4) and `maxSeedInputLength` (M5.5a) were grouped with the three above by analogy, but neither pairs with a real `core.LIMITS` field - the feed bound is `core`-agnostic and the seed bound is the RNG's implicit 32-bit range, never a named constant. Both stay local literals; see Inbox below.
  - **The share link lives at a query parameter, `?replay=<value>`, not a hash.** `apps/web/src/sharelinkurl.ts` owns `SHARE_LINK_PARAM`, `replayParamOf` (read), and `shareLinkFor`/`decodeShareLink` (the two directions). Chosen over a hash because `URLSearchParams` gives exact get/set/clear semantics for free.
  - **`decodeShareLink` is `web`'s own boundary check, ahead of `core`'s `decodeReplay`, with its own refusal kind (`share_link_too_long`, never `core`'s `too_long`)** - the task's central point: a hostile URL and a malformed replay are two different boundaries even though, after the `LIMITS` fix above, they now read the same constant.
  - **`sharelinkurl.ts` can't be named `sharelink.ts`**: TypeScript module resolution can't tell a sibling `.ts` and `.tsx` apart by specifier, so the boundary logic (`sharelinkurl.ts`) and the presentational widgets (`sharelink.tsx`) needed different basenames despite being one concept.
  - **`loadShared` reuses the exact `Hunt` shape a played-out hunt gets, rather than a lighter read-only one.** `gamestore.ts`'s new `shared` reducer calls `deps.playback.play({ replay, balance })` directly (the same call `framesFor` makes for the scrubber) and builds `{ world, view, score, seed, setup, recordedActions, frames }` from its result, so `EndScreenPanel` and `ReplayScreen` needed **zero edits**. Consequence worth flagging: a share link for a hunt that had **not** yet ended resumes as a normal playable `in_progress` hunt (`frames` stays `null`, the dispatch board renders, `End Turn` works) rather than jumping to a read-only replay view (see Inbox).
  - **New store surface: `GameStoreState.shareLinkRefusal: ShareLinkLoadRefusal | null` and dispatch `loadShared(value: string): void`**, deliberately a field of its own rather than folded into `StoreRefusal` - it means "the page's own URL produced no hunt," not "the last dispatch against a running hunt did nothing." `ShareLinkLoadRefusal = ShareLinkRefusal | Exclude<PlaybackResult, {kind:"playback"}>`, exhaustively switched in `gamestore.ts`.
  - **`main.tsx` reads `window.location.search` once, at the same point it reads `MOUNT_ELEMENT_ID`**, and dispatches `loadShared` before the first render if a `replay` param is present. Every other new function (`decodeShareLink`, `shareLinkFor`) takes plain strings so it stays unit-testable with no DOM assumptions.
  - **`DispatchScreen` gained two panels, no branch logic**: `<ShareLinkPanel />` beside `<EndScreenPanel /><ReplayScreen />` in the finished-hunt branch, `<ShareLinkErrorPanel />` beside `<DispatchErrorsPanel /><NewHuntForm />` in the no-hunt branch.
  - **Test ids added**: `share-link`, `share-link-url`, `share-link-encode-error`, `share-link-load-error`.
  - `bun run verify` passes with zero warnings: 1148 tests (was 1122) in 77 files (was 74), 180 files checked by Biome (was 174). 11 mutants run, 10 killed outright, 1 initially survived and was killed after strengthening a test. `bun run test:e2e` passes unchanged, 3 specs. `bun run build` goes from 282.19 kB / 90.22 kB gzipped to 289.37 kB / 92.20 kB.

- [x] **M5.7 Visual pass**
  deps: M5.6b-2
  Dispatch theme per DESIGN.md "Visual direction": dark background, thin glowing roads, red heatmap, monospace report feed. Add `CREDITS.md`.
  **Scoped at the pre-M2.2 review**, because "visual pass" named no bounded deliverable and would otherwise absorb every UI nit in M5. What it delivers, and nothing else: one token file (`apps/web/src/theme.ts`) holding the palette, the type scale and the heat ramp; every component reading tokens instead of literals; the glow treatment on road edges; `CREDITS.md` with a source and licence line per asset (there are none today, and AGENTS.md forbids adding one without an entry).
  Out of scope: layout changes, new components, animation, cross-browser work. Anything found that needs one goes in the Inbox.
  AC: no layout overflow at 1280×800 and 1920×1080; a test asserts no colour literal appears under `apps/web/src` outside the token file; `CREDITS.md` exists.
  Notes:
  - **`apps/web/src/theme.ts` is the one token file**: `PALETTE` (raw colour tokens), `TYPE_SCALE` (the monospace font stack plus two sizes), and the three theme tables the renderer, the heatmap and the criminal path already declared types for - `MAP_THEME: MapTheme`, `HEAT_RAMP: HeatRamp`, `CRIMINAL_PATH_THEME: CriminalPathTheme`. Each type stays declared in the component that owns the contract; only the literal values moved, so each component's `DEFAULT_*` export is now one line.
  - **`DEFAULT_SVG_THEME` in `packages/sim/src/svg.ts` was deliberately left alone.** `apps/web` may depend on `packages/core` only (architecture rule 5), and `core` holds no presentation strings (architecture rule 1), so there is no shared location either side could move the palette to. The drift the Inbox flagged is now between exactly `apps/web/src/theme.ts` and `packages/sim/src/svg.ts`, not between three files and one - resolved as far as this task's constraints allow.
  - **"Thin glowing roads" is an SVG `<filter>` in `maprenderer.tsx`**: one `feGaussianBlur` + `feMerge`, referenced by every edge `<line>`'s `filter` attribute, blurring the line's own colour rather than introducing a second glow-colour token. `MapTheme` gained `glowBlur: number` (1.5 SVG user units).
  - **The dark background and monospace font are applied once in `main.tsx`, not a CSS file**, since `apps/web` had no `index.css` before this task and adding one now would be a second, unenforceable copy of `theme.ts`'s palette (the colour-literal test only scans `.ts`/`.tsx`). `document.documentElement.style.colorScheme = "dark"` also renders native controls (`<button>`, `<input>`, `<meter>`) in their dark variant. `reportfeed.tsx` additionally sets the monospace font and small size directly, so DESIGN.md's "monospace report feed" callout holds even wherever the document default doesn't reach.
  - **The native `<meter>` (M5.4's Inbox item) was tried both ways, and the native element won on a Biome constraint, not a preference.** `accentColor: PALETTE.accent` computes correctly but Chromium still paints its own green/yellow/red heuristic over the value bar regardless - a genuine Chromium limitation, verified by a real render. The Inbox's own fallback, a `role="meter"` styled `<div>`, is rejected outright by Biome's `useSemanticElements` ("Replace with `<meter>`"), and no `biome-ignore` precedent exists in this repo to introduce for a cosmetic case. Landed on the native `<meter>` with `accentColor` set; both options the Inbox named were exhausted, so this is closed, not deferred.
  - **The new-hunt form's keyboard-submit path (M5.5a's Inbox item) is done**: real `<form onSubmit>`, `type="submit"` on the Start button, `submit()` moved into the `onSubmit` handler with no new logic and no layout change. Judged in scope as a near-zero-risk one-line change per the task's own carve-out.
  - **The map `<svg>` pan/zoom question (M5.3a's Inbox item) is decided: no zoom for MVP.** The computed `viewBox` already sizes the container correctly; the overflow AC passed against it unmodified at both target viewports, so nothing here forced revisiting the decision.
  - **The colour-literal test lives at `tools/theme/colorliterals.test.ts`, not under `apps/web/src`**, because `apps/web`'s own Biome override turns on `correctness/noNodejsModules`, so a `node:fs`-reading test cannot live inside that tree at all - `tools/` is the precedent (`architecture-rules.test.ts`). It is two hand-written regexes (hex and `rgb()`/`hsl()`/their `a` variants), not a Biome rule; named CSS colour keywords are deliberately unmatched since nothing in the tree uses one and a keyword list would false-positive on prose in comments. Test files are excluded from the scan.
  - **AC verification for "no layout overflow at 1280x800 and 1920x1080" used a throwaway, uncommitted Playwright script** checking `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` across the new-hunt form, the mid-hunt dispatch screen and the end screen at both viewports (6/6 passed), deleted afterward - matching the task's own "no new Playwright spec" scope.
  - **The two restart/new-hunt-affordance Inbox entries (M5.6a, M5.6b-1) were left alone, as scoped.** They are a new component (a button) plus a store reset dispatch, both explicitly out of this task's scope. See Inbox: recommended as a new backlog line, since there is no M5.8 yet.
  - **No focus ring on a keyboard-focused map node or edge (M5.3a's Inbox item) is still open**, left there rather than guessed at - it needs a `:focus-visible` selector, which has no one-time DOM-property equivalent the way the dark background did, and both real fixes (an injected `<style>` element, or interaction-state logic in `maprenderer.tsx`) are past the one-line bar this task set for itself.
  - `bun run verify` passes with zero warnings: 1158 tests (was 1148) in 78 files (was 77), 182 files checked by Biome (was 180). 4 mutants run, 4 killed. `bun run test:e2e` passes unchanged, 3 specs. `bun run build` goes from 289.37 kB / 92.20 kB gzipped to 290.84 kB / 92.84 kB. This is the last task in the planned M0-M5 MVP milestone.

---

## M6 — Visual overhaul

The MVP is playable and looks like an unstyled document, which is what M0-M5 asked for: `apps/web` has no CSS file, no class names and five inline style objects in total, `DispatchScreen` renders six panels as bare siblings in source order, and the map `<svg>` carries a `viewBox` with no `width`/`height` inside containers that never size it, so it paints at the browser's 300x150 intrinsic default with the panels stacked underneath. M5.7's visual pass was deliberately scoped to tokenising the colours that already existed and excluded layout, new components and animation.

This milestone is the one that spends that budget, and it is placed before the content backlog on the maintainer's instruction: the game should look slick before it grows. **Target: "Payday dispatch board x Door Kickers tactical plan"** - a real plan of a city with district blocks, a banded river and marked gates, wrapped in heavy hard-edged chrome with procedural avatars.

Four decisions were taken with the maintainer before the milestone was written, and each is a constraint on every task below:

- **Renderer: stay React SVG**, behind the `MapRenderer` seam M5.3a built. No PixiJS; that stays in M7.
- **Art source: code-drawn only.** Procedural seeded SVG avatars, hand-authored path icons, `feTurbulence` grit. No asset files, therefore no new `CREDITS.md` entries and no licence question.
- **Map metaphor: tactical city plan**, not a prettier node-link diagram. Blocks with footprints, a river channel with bridge decks, layered roads, exit gates.
- **Placement: a new M6**, with the post-MVP content backlog renumbered to M7. Done as the milestone's first act, across 38 references in 12 files.

Standing constraints, collected here so no task rediscovers them:

- **`noMagicNumbers` is an error** outside `limits.ts`, `balance.ts` and tests. Object-literal property values are exempt, which is why `MAP_THEME` is legal, but a bare `r={4}` in JSX is not. Every geometry number arrives as a token or a prop.
- **`noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` is capped at 10** and the repo's current worst is 9. Each new map layer is its own small component, extending the `MapEdgeLine`/`MapNodeMarker`/`MapRiver` split.
- **Test ids and `data-*` attributes are a contract**: 54 ids and 33 data attributes are pinned across the jsdom suites and the three Playwright specs. Add attributes, never rename or drop one. DOM *shape* is almost never asserted, so wrapper elements and restructuring are free.
- **Five specific assertions are the real tripwires**, each owned by a named task below:
  - `maprenderer.test.tsx` and `beliefoverlay.test.tsx` assert the literal string `pointer-events: none` in `map-overlay`'s **inline** `style` attribute. It stays inline; a CSS class fails both. (M6.5, M6.6)
  - `maprenderer.test.tsx` pins document order river -> overlay -> edges -> nodes via `compareDocumentPosition`. New layers go before the river or after the nodes, never between. (M6.4, M6.5)
  - `meters.test.tsx` asserts `accentColor === "rgb(95, 212, 255)"`, which **pins `PALETTE.accent` to `#5fd4ff` exactly**. Retuning the accent is a deliberate test edit, not a surprise failure. (M6.1)
  - `newhuntform.test.tsx` asserts exactly one `<input>` and one `<select>` in the form, and separately asserts the seed input carries no `maxLength`. Difficulty-as-cards breaks the first. (M6.9)
  - `app-shell.spec.ts` needs `App.tsx`'s `<h1>` to stay an `h1` carrying `GAME_TITLE`. (M6.2)
- **Architecture rule 4 still binds.** Everything drawn comes from `HunterView` or `RevealFrame`, and the criminal's appearance must not correlate with the hidden profile. (M6.7)
- **No new dependencies.** No CSS framework, icon package, animation library or geometry library. The one non-trivial algorithm is written in-repo.
- **`packages/core` and `packages/sim` are untouched by this milestone.** Everything below is derivable from what `HunterView`, `RevealFrame` and `BALANCE` already export.

  Rewrite DESIGN.md's "Visual direction (initial)" into the direction above, and amend its "Out of scope for MVP" list so portraits read as M6 work rather than as excluded.
  Grow `apps/web/src/theme.ts` from a palette into a design system: a surface/elevation ramp, district tones plus hatch definitions, the three-hue discipline (red is the criminal, gold is objectives, cyan is the player's own instruments, nothing else saturated), two type stacks with a real size and weight scale, a spacing scale, stroke-weight tiers, glow and shadow tiers, motion durations and easings.
  **This task takes the milestone's one architectural decision: `apps/web` gets a stylesheet.** M5.7 declined one because it "would be a second copy of `theme.ts`'s palette the colour-literal test could not see", which was sound then and is not now: inline style objects cannot express `:hover`, `:focus-visible`, `@keyframes`, `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` or the vendor `::-webkit-meter-bar` pseudo-elements, and this milestone needs all five. Resolution: `theme.ts` stays the single source and gains an exported `CSS_VARIABLES` table; `main.tsx` writes them onto `document.documentElement` with `setProperty`, beside the one-time page chrome it already applies; a new `apps/web/src/index.css` is authored exclusively in `var(--mh-*)` references; and `tools/theme/colorliterals.test.ts` is extended to scan `.css` with **no** token-file exemption, with its regexes widened to catch `oklch()`, `lab()`, `color()` and `color-mix()`. That is strictly more enforcement than exists today, not less.
  Out of scope: component changes beyond wiring `main.tsx` and importing the stylesheet.

  Also: `PALETTE.textMuted` is declared and read by nothing today - use it or drop it. If the accent is retuned, update `meters.test.tsx` in the same commit with the reason in the task note.
  Notes:
  - **The tokens are derived, not tabulated.** `theme.ts` stays data only - `DESIGN_TOKENS` groups `PALETTE`/`TYPE_SCALE`/`SPACE`/`RADIUS`/`SHADOW`/`MOTION` - and the new `cssvariables.ts` holds the logic that turns a group and a token into `--mh-<group>-<token>`. The AC's hand-written `CSS_VARIABLES` table would have been a second list to keep in step; `cssvariables.test.ts` asserts the published count equals the token count instead, so a new token cannot be forgotten.
  - **`TYPE_SCALE`'s keys lost their `font` prefix** (`fontSizeBase` -> `sizeBase`): the group already supplies it, and `--mh-font-font-size-base` was the test failure that found it.
  - **`PALETTE.accent` was not retuned**, so `meters.test.tsx` is untouched. `PALETTE.textMuted` is now published as `--mh-color-text-muted`; its first visual consumer is the feed's staleness treatment in M6.8.
  - **The stylesheet is `<link>`ed from `index.html`, not imported from `main.tsx`**: `apps/web/tsconfig.json` sets `"types": []`, so a CSS import would need `vite/client`'s ambient declarations or a shim, and page chrome should exist before React mounts. `main.tsx` writes the custom properties onto `document.documentElement` in the same place M5.7 set `document.body.style.*`.
  - **Motion is opt-in**: every animated rule goes inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`. Biome's `noImportantStyles` rejects the usual blanket `* { animation-duration: 1ms !important }` reset, and that reset only ever worked because of the `!important` - a universal selector has no specificity. Opting in fails safe: a forgotten block means no animation, not an unstoppable one. M6.10 asserts the guarantee.
  - **`vitest.config.ts`'s `web` project now sets `css: true`.** Vitest replaces a CSS import with an empty string otherwise, `?raw` query included, which made the stylesheet check silently vacuous until the `toBeGreaterThan(0)` guard caught it.
  - **The `noDefaultExport` override grew `**/*.d.ts`** in `biome.json`, for `assets.d.ts`'s `*.css?raw` shim - AGENTS.md's own "except where a tool requires it" carve-out. `apps/web` cannot read the stylesheet with `node:fs`: its Biome override bans Node modules, and `tools/` cannot import `theme.ts` because `theme.ts` type-imports from `.tsx` and `tsconfig.tools.json` sets no `jsx` option.
  - **Mutation-checked**: a planted hex and a planted `oklch()` in `index.css`, a planted hex in a `.tsx`, and a typo'd `var(--mh-*)` name each fail, and each passes again on restore.
  - `bun run build` emits `dist/assets/index-*.css` (1.02 kB, 0.44 kB gzipped); bundle 292.04 kB, 93.51 kB gzipped (M5.7: 290.84 / 92.84). 1167 tests (was 1158) in 79 files (was 78), 186 files checked by Biome (was 182). The built page computes the same seven chrome rules M5.7 applied inline, and all three Playwright specs pass.

- [x] **M6.3 District cell geometry**
  deps: M6.1
  Pure logic, no rendering. A new `apps/web/src/districtcells.ts` derives a footprint polygon per node from node positions alone, by clipping the padded map bounds against the perpendicular bisector between each node and every other - Sutherland-Hodgman half-plane clipping, small enough to write in-repo rather than add a geometry package.
  **Derived in `web` rather than added to `core` on purpose.** Node ids encode their grid cell (`n-<column>-<row>`), but `core` deliberately keeps `GridCell` off `MapGraph` and calls parsing the id back out "guesswork dressed as arithmetic", so a derivation from positions is the only generator-agnostic option: it works on today's jittered 100-unit grid and on M7's Voronoi generator identically, with no `core` change.
  `districts.ts` grows one contiguous region per `DistrictType` with exits punched out as scattered single nodes, so **merging cells by `districtType` yields a real district blob**, not a patchwork - that is what M6.4 draws.
  Bounded per AGENTS.md section 5 by a named cap on sites considered, against `LIMITS.maxMapNodes`.
  AC: every cell contains its own site; cells are disjoint and their areas sum to the bounds area within tolerance; output is deterministic for a given node list; a fast-check property holds both invariants over random point sets; degenerate input (one node, coincident nodes) returns something drawable rather than throwing.
  Notes:
  - **API** (`apps/web/src/districtcells.ts`): `districtCellsOf(nodes, bounds): DistrictCellResult`, either `{ kind: "cells", cells }` or `{ kind: "too_many_sites", sites, maxSites }`. Each `DistrictCell` is `{ nodeId, districtType, polygon }`, one per node **in node order**; `polygon` is a convex `readonly Position[]`, implicitly closed, winding as the clip leaves it (drawable as an SVG `points` list either way). `paddedBoundsOf(points, padding): CellBounds` (`{ minX, minY, maxX, maxY }`) builds the rectangle.
  - **Coordinate space is map units**, the same as `MapNode.position` and the renderer's `viewBox`; no rounding is applied, so M6.4 formats with `maprenderer.tsx`'s `coordinate()`. Padding is the caller's: M6.4 should pass `paddedBoundsOf(drawn, theme.padding)` over the same node-plus-river point list the `viewBox` uses, so the cells tile exactly the visible plate. The ground-coloured street gaps are a render-time inset or stroke, not something the geometry carries.
  - **Degenerate cases**: no nodes gives no cells; one node gets the whole rectangle; coincident nodes skip each other (they have no bisector), so each gets the same cell, which contains its site and is drawable but overlaps its twin - the disjointness invariant holds only over distinct positions, and the property test generates distinct integer points for that reason. A node outside the given bounds can come back with an empty polygon; `paddedBoundsOf` over the same nodes never produces that.
  - **Bound**: `LIMITS.maxDistrictCellSites` reads `core`'s `LIMITS.maxMapNodes` (256); clipping is quadratic, and one site over is refused before any clipping runs.
  - Disjointness is tested by sampling: no random point lies strictly inside two cells. 1182 tests (was 1167) in 80 files (was 79).

- [x] **M6.4 Map surface: blocks, river, roads, bridges, exits, time of day**
  deps: M6.3
  The Door Kickers pass on everything that is not an actor. District blocks filled and hatched per type with ground-coloured street gaps, merged per district region. Roads as casing plus fill with M5.7's glow as a third layer beneath both, edge kinds differing by dash and width rather than hue alone. Exit gates carrying an `ExitKind` glyph - `districts.ts` documents the kind as flavour no MVP rule reads, so it is free iconography. A day/night wash over the whole plate driven by `clock.hour` through `timeOfDayAt`, because `balance.districts.park.nightWitnessMultiplier` is 0 and night should look like it costs something.
  **The river needs building, not thickening.** `MapGraph.river` is an *open* staircase polyline of `2 * cuts` points running half a cell past each end, so a channel comes from offsetting it into a band; stroking it wider is not the same shape. Bridge decks are drawn where a `bridge` edge crosses it.
  Files: `maprenderer.tsx` gains `MapBlocks`, `MapRiverChannel` and `MapExitGate`; `MapTheme` grows the new tables and `theme.ts` supplies them. `map-river` keeps its test id on the new channel group, and new layers respect the pinned document order.
  AC: a view with a river renders the channel and every bridge deck on it; each `DistrictType` renders a block with its own fill and a new variant is a compile error; each `EdgeKind` renders with its own treatment; `maprenderer.test.tsx`'s node and edge counts, layering order and glow-filter assertions all still pass.
  - **Layer order** (document order, bottom to top): glow filter defs, `map-plate`, `map-blocks`, `map-wash`, `map-river` (channel, current, decks), `map-overlay`, the listbox (edges, then nodes), `map-exit-gates`. M6.5's pips go inside the node markers or after `map-exit-gates`, and nowhere between the river and the nodes.
  - **The wash sits under the river**, not over the whole SVG. The pinned order leaves only "before the river" or "after the nodes", and a wash above the nodes would dim the heatmap, the selection and the actors, which are the player's instruments. So night darkens the ground and blocks, and everything the player reads stays at full contrast. `data-timeofday` is on both `map-wash` and the root `map` svg, so M6.5 can key actor styles off the svg without adding a prop. `MapRenderer` takes an optional `daylight: DaylightHours` (defaults to `BALANCE.time`), and `MapPanel` passes the store's `balance.time`.
  - **Blocks** (`districtblocks.ts`): `districtBlocksOf(nodes, bounds, streetGap)` returns one inset polygon per node, in node order, or passes M6.3's `too_many_sites` refusal through unchanged. Only edges facing a *different* district are pulled in, by `streetGap / 2`, so districts merge visually without any polygon union. A cell narrower than the gap comes back empty and is not drawn. Test ids: `map-blocks` > `map-block[data-district]` (one `<g>` per type, filled with `url(#map-block-<type>)`) > `map-block-cell[data-nodeid]`. Hatches are `userSpaceOnUse` patterns, so a hatch runs unbroken across a district. `districtcells.ts` now exports `clipToHalfPlane` and `HalfPlane` for this. There is no new colour for the fills: each tone and hatch is a `PALETTE` token.
  - **River** (`riverchannel.ts`): `riverChannelOf(points, width)` offsets the de-duplicated course into a closed band with mitred joins, capped at 4 half-widths. `bridgeDecksOf(spans, course, measure)` lays one deck per bridge at its first crossing, lengthened by `1/sin` of the crossing angle so a slanted bridge still reaches both banks. The new bound `LIMITS.maxRiverPoints` (2 x `maxMapNodes`) has an over-limit test. `map-river` is now the channel `<g>`; inside it are `map-river-channel` (polygon), `map-river-current` (the dashed polyline on `core`'s original points) and `map-bridge-deck[data-edgeid]`.
  - **The existing river test was changed deliberately.** "draws the river a view carries" asserted `points` on `map-river`, which the task itself moved onto a group. It now asserts that `map-river` exists and that `map-river-current` carries the same `"0,35 50,35"`. No other existing assertion changed.
  - **Edges** are three strokes per edge, all tagged with `data-layer` (`EDGE_LAYERS`): the glow `<line>` (still the only `<line>` per edge and still the only element with the filter, so M5.7's glow test holds as written), then the casing `<path>`, then the fill `<path>`. Kinds differ by `width`/`dash`/`casingWidth`/`casingDash` in `theme.ts`'s `EDGE_STYLES`. The blocked-edge dot is unchanged, left for M6.5 to replace.
  - **Exit gates**: `map-exit-gate[data-exit][data-nodeid]` is a plate plus an `EXIT_GLYPHS[kind]` stroke path (16-unit box), offset up and to the right of the node. The whole gates layer is `pointer-events: none`, so clicks reach the node.
  - **The viewBox and the plate both come from `paddedBoundsOf`** (closing M6.3's Inbox item). The private `boundsOf`/`leastOf`/`greatestOf` are gone.
  - `DISTRICT_BLOCKS`, `DAYLIGHT_WASH` and `EXIT_GLYPHS` are typed `Record<DistrictType | TimeOfDay | ExitKind, ...>`, so a new variant is a compile error.

  - **`MapTheme` changed shape.** `nodeRadius`, `incidentRadius`, `nodeStroke`, `exitStroke`, `incidentStroke`, `markerWidth`, `selectionWidth`, `selectionGap`, `blockedStroke` and `blockedRadius` are gone. `pip` (size 13), `incidentRing` (r 12), `focus` (gap 3), `reticle` (gap 4, tick 4), `checkpoint` and `harm` (r 15, badge 11) are new. M6.6 should know two things. `data-timeofday` on the root svg is still unused by the actors. The pips sit over the heatmap, so a field drawn in the overlay stays underneath them.

- [x] **M6.6 Belief field and report pins**
  deps: M6.5
  The heatmap becomes a field rather than stacked translucent circles: radial-gradient blobs blended additively, a threshold contour at the top tier, and a legend. `heatOf(ramp, mass, peak)` and its monotonicity property are kept exactly - only what the mapping drives changes. Normalising against the peak stays right: belief is one cell per node summing to 1, so a 48-node city's peak is a few per cent.
  Every `HunterReport`'s `content` carries a `nodeId`, for `sighting` and `no_sighting` alike, so recent reports can be pinned on the map with an age treatment. That is the largest readability win available from data already in the view.
  AC: M5.3b's three ACs hold unchanged - zero belief renders no heat, the peak renders strongest, and the overlay intercepts no clicks, including the literal inline `pointer-events: none`; `heatOf`'s unit and property tests are untouched; `belief-heat` keeps `data-nodeid` and `data-intensity`.
  Notes:
  - **Files.** `beliefcontour.ts` (the contour geometry), `reportpins.ts` (which pins there are), `mapreportpins.tsx` (the pin layer and `ReportPinGlyph`), and `belieflegend.tsx` (the key), each with a co-located test. `beliefoverlay.tsx` changed; `heatOf`, `peakBeliefMass` and `beliefHeatOf` did not, and no existing assertion in `beliefoverlay.test.tsx` or `beliefoverlaypanel.test.tsx` was changed.
  - **The field.** Every `belief-heat` circle keeps `data-nodeid`, `data-intensity`, `r` and `fill-opacity` from `heatOf`. It now fills from one shared `radialGradient` (`belief-field-gradient`, stops from `BELIEF_FIELD.stops` in `ramp.fill`) and gains `data-top-tier`. Additive blending is `mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter` on `.mh-belief-field__blob`, inside a `.mh-belief-field` group with `isolation: isolate` so the blend never reaches the plate. It is static, so it sits outside the motion block. `HEAT_RAMP` was retuned for the gradient: radius 22-56 (was 5-18) and opacity 0.2-0.85 (was 0.06-0.55).
  - **The contour.** The top tier is every node whose intensity is at least `BELIEF_FIELD.contourFrom` (0.75 of the peak; see `topTierOf`). The contour (`belief-contour`, `data-nodeids`) outlines the union of those nodes' M6.3 district cells. It keeps each cell edge no other in-tier cell shares (tolerance 1e-6), so no polygon union is needed. It is drawn as a `surfaceSunken` casing plus a dashed `incidentRed` line ("6 4") inside the overlay, so it is under the edges and takes no clicks. The cells need the plate's bounds, so `mapgeometry.ts` gained `plateBoundsOf(map, padding)`, which `MapRenderer`'s viewBox now reads too. `BeliefOverlay` takes `bounds?`, and `BeliefOverlayPanel` passes the plate's. The contour recomputes the cells separately from the blocks (see Inbox). Under a uniform prior every node is at the peak, so on turn 1 the contour is the whole plate's edge.
  - **The pins.** They sit in a layer `map-report-pins` after `map-exit-gates` and before `map-incidents`, with inline `pointer-events: none`. The pinned river -> overlay -> edges -> nodes order is untouched. There is one `map-report-pin` per node with a report no older than `REPORT_PIN.maxAge` (5 turns). The pin carries `data-nodeid`, `data-kind`, `data-source`, `data-age`, `data-staleness` and `data-count`. The freshest report at a node decides the pin, and at equal age a sighting beats a clearance. Age and staleness are `feedrow.ts`'s `ageOf`/`stalenessOf` against `FEED_ROW_THEME`, so a pin and its feed row agree. Aging and stale pins fade through `--mh-fade-aging` (new, 0.8) and `--mh-fade-stale`. Drawing is capped at `LIMITS.maxReportPins` (24), freshest first, and the rest are counted on the layer's `data-withheld` (the feed's truncate-and-declare). A pin reads only `HunterReport` fields, so a prank is pinned exactly like a true sighting (rule 4). Each pin is 18 units tall, with its tip on the pip's top edge (`lift` 6.5, which is half of `pip.size`, and a test ties the two), so the district glyph stays visible. A sighting has a `text` outline with a red dot; a no-sighting has a muted outline with a bar.
  - **The legend** is a `<ul aria-label="Map legend">` (`belief-legend`, items `belief-legend-item` with `data-item` ramp | contour | sighting | no_sighting | age). It is a strip under the map inside `.mh-frame__map`, whose rows are now `minmax(0, 1fr) auto`, so it covers no node. `DispatchScreen` mounts it; `ReplayScreen` does not. Every swatch draws from the map's own tables. Checked in Chromium with a throwaway spec (since deleted): no overflow at 1280x800 or 1920x1080.
  - **For M6.9:** the replay's `BeliefOverlayPanel` gets the field and contour automatically. The debrief has no legend; mount `BeliefLegend` there if wanted. **For M6.10:** pins, contour and legend have no motion, so report-arrival motion is M6.10's to add. The pins are unlabelled SVG, like the heat, so the heatmap's text equivalent should cover them too.
  - Results: 1369 tests (was 1323) in 97 files (was 93); Biome checked 224 files; coverage 99.6% statements / 96.69% branches (the coverage scope is `core`, unchanged); `test:e2e` 3/3 passed.

- [x] **M6.7 Procedural avatars and the dossier**
  deps: M6.1
  `apps/web/src/avatar.ts` is pure logic turning a seed plus an `AvatarKind` into layered shape **data** - silhouette, headwear, mask band, eye band, palette indices - and `avatar.tsx` renders it. Used by a new criminal dossier card, by report sources in the feed, and by the debrief.
  **This task carries a hidden-information decision.** The criminal's profile is derived from the seed, so seeding a revealing avatar from the seed would leak profile-correlated appearance through exactly the channel `HunterView` closes. The dossier therefore renders a **seed-independent redacted silhouette** before the reveal - Payday's mask over an unknown face - and the seeded portrait only on the debrief and replay screens, where `RevealFrame` has already told the player everything.
  AC: the same seed and kind always produce the same avatar; different seeds visibly differ; **the pre-reveal criminal dossier is identical across seeds**, asserted directly; no import of a name `biome.json` denies to `apps/web`.
  Notes:
  - **Files: `avatar.ts` (logic), `avatarportrait.tsx` (renderer), `dossier.tsx` (card), `dossierpanel.tsx` (connected card).** The renderer is not `avatar.tsx`: beside `avatar.ts`, an extensionless `./avatar` resolves to the `.ts` file under `moduleResolution: bundler` and in Vite, so a same-named `.tsx` could not be imported.
  - **API for M6.8/M6.9.** `avatarOf(seed: number, kind: AvatarKind): Avatar` with `AVATAR_KINDS = ["criminal", "witness", "cctv", "tip", "patrol"]` (the four `ReportSource`s plus the criminal); `REDACTED_AVATAR: Avatar`. `<AvatarPortrait avatar label size theme? />` draws one; sizes are `AVATAR_THEME.portraitSize` (96) and `AVATAR_THEME.thumbnailSize` (32, for feed rows). `<CriminalDossier subject theme? />` takes a `DossierSubject` (`{ kind: "redacted" } | { kind: "revealed"; avatar }`) built by `dossierSubjectOf(seed, frames)`; `<CriminalDossierPanel />` reads `hunt.seed` and `hunt.frames` off the store and renders nothing without a hunt.
  - **Test ids added:** `avatar` (the `<svg>`, with `data-subject`), `avatar-layer` (each `<path>`, with `data-part` and `data-ramp`), `criminal-dossier` (with `data-subject` = `redacted`/`revealed`), `criminal-dossier-status`.
  - **The reveal switches on `hunt.frames !== null`**, i.e. on the store having built `RevealFrame`s, which it only does once a hunt has settled (`framesFor`). The redacted `DossierSubject` carries no seed, so nothing seed-dependent can reach the card before then. `dossierpanel.test.tsx` asserts byte-identical markup for two real running hunts on seeds 1 and 2, and that it stays so across a turn; `dossier.test.tsx` asserts the same over arbitrary seed pairs as data.
  - **The dossier is not mounted anywhere.** The task names no mount point; M6.8 (intel column) or M6.9 (debrief) places `CriminalDossierPanel`, which already switches on its own and so can sit in both.
  - **The hash is in-file, not `core`'s `Rng`**: `createRng` belongs to the composition root, and threading an injected logic object to every face drawn is a lot of plumbing for a cosmetic draw that never has to agree with the simulation. A splitmix32 finaliser over the seed and an FNV-1a salt of the kind; the kind salt means the same seed gives a witness and a tip different faces.
  - **For M6.8's feed avatars:** seed a source avatar from something public and per-report (the report id's hash, say) with the source as the kind. Never draw `avatarOf(hunt.seed, "criminal")` anywhere before the reveal.
  - **Hue discipline:** the criminal's mask is the one saturated tone (`mark` ramp = `PALETTE.incidentRed`), and a test keeps it off every other kind. Clothing and headwear reuse the surface/edge ramp. Four desaturated skin tones were added to `PALETTE` (`skinPale`/`skinWarm`/`skinTan`/`skinDeep`), so they are published as CSS variables too, which is harmless. `avatarportrait.test.tsx` asserts every `AVATAR_THEME` ramp is exactly `AVATAR_TONE_COUNTS` long.

The tally is `dl.mh-tally`; each row adds `data-sign` (`bonus`, `penalty` or `nil`). Every existing id and attribute is kept. One text change: a component row used to read "4 (-60)" in one `dd`. The measured value is now a span in the `dt` and the points sit alone in the `dd`. No test read that text.

Each step is a `replay-transport` button with `data-step`, disabled when its target is the turn already shown. The glyphs are `TRANSPORT_ICON_THEME` in `theme.ts`. The range input and `replay-scrubber-turn` stay. "of N" sits in a sibling span, so the turn span's text is unchanged, and the label is now visually hidden.

Two bounds sit in `limits.ts`: `maxClipboardTextLength` is 2 x `maxReplayStringLength` (16384 characters), checked before the write, and `maxClipboardWriteMilliseconds` is 3000, raced against the write. Every failure comes back as a result, never a throw: `too_long`, `unavailable`, `rejected` (whether the writer rejects or throws) or `timed_out`. `ShareLinkCopy` renders the button (`share-link-copy`) and an `<output>` (`share-link-copy-status`, with `data-result`).
