- [x] **M6.10 Motion, accessibility, and the verification pass**
  deps: M6.9
  Apply the named motion tokens - turn advance, report arrival, meter movement, checkpoint fire, outcome sting - each gated behind `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`. Motion is confirmation, never decoration.
  Then the sweep: a contrast audit against the final palette, **a text equivalent for the heatmap** (the top suspected districts as a list - M5.3b's Inbox item, where `aria-hidden` is rejected by `noAriaHiddenOnFocusable` and a text summary is the honest fix), focus order through the new frame, overflow re-verified at both viewports, and `CREDITS.md` updated to record that the overhaul added no third-party assets.
  AC: every animation is disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`; no layout overflow at 1280x800 and 1920x1080; the heatmap has a non-visual equivalent; `bun run verify` and `bun run test:e2e` pass with zero warnings.
  Notes:
  - **The guard runs the other way from this task's wording, as M6.1 already set it up.** Motion is opted in inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` rather than switched off under `reduce`, so a rule nobody guarded cannot move. `tools/theme/reducedmotion.test.ts` checks this. It cuts every guarded block out of `index.css` and asserts that no `animation`, `transition` or `@keyframes` is left. It asserts that all five motion tokens are used inside the guard. It also scans every non-test `.ts`/`.tsx` in `apps/web/src` for inline animation or transition styles, `<animate`, `.animate(` and `requestAnimationFrame`. Mutation-checked in both directions.
  - **Motion tokens.** `MOTION` gained `turnAdvance` and `checkpointFire` (base), `reportArrival`, `meterMovement` and `outcomeSting` (slow), and `arrivalOffset` (0.5rem). Each one replays by remounting the element, not with script:
    - the rail clock's `<dd>` and each meter's value are keyed by their displayed text;
    - feed rows already carried `data-new`;
    - a report pin is keyed by the turn its node last heard a report (`lastHeardByNodeOf` in `reportpins.ts`), and it gains an additive `data-new`. A pin's age is never 0, because reports arrive after they were seen, so age could not drive the motion.
  - **What each motion animates.**
    - The checkpoint animates an inner `<g class="mh-map-checkpoint">`, because a CSS transform would override the outer `transform` attribute.
    - **"Checkpoint fire" is the barrier going live, not a hit.** The hunter is never told a checkpoint was hit (architecture rule 4 and the Inbox entry on it), so there is no hit to confirm.
    - The outcome sting is the banner. The tally rows follow it with a delay of `outcomeSting` and `backwards` fill.
  - **Contrast audit.** It is `contrast.ts` plus `contrast.test.ts`, WCAG relative luminance: 4.5 for text and 3 for the focus ring and field edge. The test ties itself to `index.css`, so any `color: var(--mh-color-*)` in an unaudited token fails. It found three problems, all now fixed:
    - `textDim` measured 3.13 on overlay and moved from `#5e7288` to `#7a8fa5`, which gives 4.65-6.02 across the surfaces;
    - the stale feed row's `opacity: var(--mh-fade-stale)` dropped its text below 4.5 and was removed, so staleness now reads from the row's tone and label, and the stale pin still fades;
    - the form field's border was under 3 and now uses `text-dim`.

    `PALETTE.accent` is unchanged.
  - **Heatmap text equivalent.** `heatmapsummary.ts` computes it with the same `beliefHeatOf`, `topTierOf` and `reportPinsOf` the map draws with, so the list cannot disagree with the picture. `HeatmapSummaryList` renders it as a visually hidden `section[aria-label="Map summary"]` (`heatmap-summary`) holding two lists:
    - the most suspected districts, strongest first, as whole percentages with a "top tier" flag (`heatmap-suspect`);
    - the pinned reports, worded as the feed words them (`heatmap-report`).

    Each list is bounded by `LIMITS.maxHeatmapSummaryEntries` (5), and the overflow is declared as "N more not listed" (`heatmap-withheld`), with at-limit and one-over tests. It is mounted before the map on the dispatch board and in the replay.
  - **Focus order.** The map is 110 tab stops on seed 1, ahead of the orders, so both screens got a skip link as their first tab stop. `skiplink.tsx` renders it as a button, not an anchor, because a fragment would rewrite the `?replay=` URL. It focuses `frame-command` on the board and the scrubber in the replay, both `tabIndex=-1`. `dispatchscreen.test.tsx` pins the tab order: no positive tabindex anywhere, the skip link before the map, the regions in reading order, and End Turn after the actions. It was confirmed once in real Chromium.
  - **Checked with throwaway Playwright specs, since deleted, following the M5.7/M6.2/M6.9 precedent.** They are not in the e2e suite, which AGENTS.md keeps to critical flows. They checked:
    - that `scrollWidth`/`scrollHeight` equal the client sizes on the briefing, the board and the debrief at 1280x800 and 1920x1080;
    - that `document.getAnimations()` is empty under emulated `reduce`;
    - that all six keyframes run under `no-preference`.
  - **Numbers.** No existing assertion was changed. Totals:
    - 1484 tests (was 1407) in 105 files (was 101);
    - Biome checks 244 files (was 235);
    - coverage is unchanged at 99.6% statements and 96.69% branches;
    - e2e passes 3 of 3, unedited;
    - the bundle is 336.12 kB (108.11 kB gzipped) and the CSS 21.44 kB (3.72 kB gzipped).

    No new dependencies; `core` and `sim` are untouched.
  - **M6 is complete.**

- [ ] PixiJS renderer behind `MapRenderer`
- [ ] Force, and the captured-alive score component it makes real (the `alive` flag on `GameOutcome.captured` plus `score.capturedAliveBonus`)
- [ ] Actions billed after they are chosen (overtime, standing upkeep on a containment, federal help), and the bankruptcy end condition they would make reachable again
- [ ] Sound (freesound.org, with credits)

---

## Decisions and open questions

Each entry blocks a named task. Decide before that task starts, not during it. Resolved entries stay here with their resolution, because the tasks downstream were written before the answer existed.

**Resolved**

- **MVP meter set.** Pressure in, fatigue out. `HunterState` is `{ actionPoints, budget, trust, pressure, containments }` (M1.2). M3.8a's consequences phase is what raises pressure; it is display-only in the MVP and is not a score component, because it tracks the clock and M3.10 already scores turns taken. DESIGN.md now lists fatigue under "Out of scope for MVP".
- **Post-game reveal type.** `RevealFrame = { turn, view, criminalNodeId }`, declared in M1.2 beside `HunterView`. M3.9 uses it; `WorldState` may exist inside `core` as an intermediate and never crosses into `web`.
- **How the UI holds a game between turns.** M5.2 said "a store holding `WorldState` privately", which architecture rule 4 forbids and `biome.json` blocks by name. Resolution: `SealedWorld`, an opaque branded alias of `WorldState` with no readable members, declared in M3.1b. The store keeps data and hands it back to `core`; it never reads it and never becomes logic. Sealing is a type-level guarantee, not encryption - the bytes survive `JSON.stringify` because replays need them to - and that limit is written into AGENTS.md rule 4.
- **Where the belief distribution lives.** DESIGN.md says "the hunter view includes a belief distribution over nodes", spread "each turn". The plan had it as M4.0, a module folding a *sequence* of `HunterView`s after the fact, and `HunterView` had no such member - so the two documents disagreed about whether the heatmap is state or a derivation, and **nothing in the plan accumulated the history the derivation needed**: M5.2's store holds the sealed world and the action log, and no task gave it a view log or a bound on one. Resolution: **DESIGN.md is right and the plan was wrong.** `Belief` is plain serializable data on `WorldState`, M3.8a's consequences phase advances it once per turn, and `toHunterView` projects it. The task is **M3.6b**, moved ahead of M3.7 so M3.8a can call it.
  The deciding argument is M4.1, not DESIGN.md. Its AC is "bots only use `HunterView`", and a `heatmap-chaser` needs the distribution. With belief outside the world, the bot must either accumulate its own history - logic holding data, which engineering principle 1 forbids outright - or the runner threads a second state channel alongside `WorldState`, which M3.8c's determinism test and M3.9's replay would then both have to cover. Putting it in the world costs one field and keeps one determinism envelope. It also removes the need for a `maxViewHistory` bound, because there is no history: the distribution is a fixed-size vector over nodes.
  The cost is real and worth stating: `core` now computes the hunter's inference with the criminal's true position in scope, so hidden-information safety is a **test** (M3.6b's last AC), not a type. Blocks M3.6b, M3.8a, M4.1, M5.3b.

- **Config versus setup.** `GameConfig` names the criminal profile, which DESIGN.md hides, so `web` could not build one. M3.1a splits `GameSetup` (player-visible, goes in the replay string) from the resolved `GameConfig` (stays in `WorldState`), and derives the profile from the seed. A shared replay link therefore does not spoil the hunt it replays.

- **`GameEvent` leaks which report is a prank, and `HunterView` needs an event projection.** `events.ts` gives `eyewitness` and `prank_call` a `reportId`, and `HunterView.events` is the unredacted `GameEvent[]`. A `prank_call` event therefore names exactly which report is a prank - the one thing `HIDDEN_REPORT_FIELDS` and `HunterReport` exist to hide. Neither `view.test.ts`'s shape checks nor M3.2's stated AC ("every report in the view has no `truth`/`accuracy`") would catch it, so it would have shipped. Resolution: declare `HunterEvent` in `view.ts` beside `HunterReport`, collapsing both variants into one `report_arrived { turn, reportId }`; `HunterView.events` becomes `readonly HunterEvent[]`. The feed still learns that a report landed, which is what M5.4 needs, and stops learning what kind it was. DESIGN.md's "Reports" section now states the rule. The hidden-variant list is **data**, the way `HIDDEN_REPORT_FIELDS` is, so the projection and its type cannot drift. Blocks M3.2; M3.7 must not add an event that names a report's nature.

- **Where the start hour comes from.** Derived from the seed, and **not** a member of `GameSetup`. DESIGN.md says a hunt "starts at a random time of day"; M3.1a's first draft listed start hour in the player-visible setup, which would make it a player choice and put it in every replay string. Deriving it costs nothing, keeps the string shorter, and leaves one fewer field for `web`, `sim` and the replay codec to agree on. `GameConfig.startHour` stays - that is the resolved form. Blocks M3.1a.

- **Whether a capture was alive.** An earlier review gave `GameOutcome.captured` an `alive: boolean` for **M3.8b** to set. **Superseded at the pre-M2.2 review, for the same reason bankruptcy was cut.** The MVP action subset is roadblock, canvass, CCTV and briefing, none of which use force, and capture is both sides standing on one node - so the flag is `true` by construction and `capturedAliveBonus` is not a score component but a constant added to every win. Resolution: `GameOutcome.captured` gains no flag, `score.capturedAliveBonus` is removed from `balance.ts` (done at this review), and DESIGN.md's win line says the component arrives with the actions that can vary it. M7 names the door. Blocks M3.8b, M3.10.

- **How much of M5 is Playwright's.** AGENTS.md is explicit: "`web` gets Playwright tests only for critical flows (start game, take a turn, reach an end screen)." Five M5 ACs contradicted it by naming Playwright for node selection, score-component rendering, overlay hit-testing and replay scrubbing. Resolution: **Playwright owns exactly M5.5's turn flow and M5.6a's finish**; M5.3a, M5.3b, M5.4 and M5.6b's scrubber assert under the jsdom environment M5.2 installs. The ACs below are reworded. This is also the cheaper split - a jsdom assertion on the mapping from probability to a rendered attribute is a unit test, and AGENTS.md separately forbids snapshot-testing the SVG.

- **Which travel mode `minEscapeTurns` is measured in.** **Foot.** DESIGN.md's rule ("shortest start-to-nearest-exit path >= `MIN_ESCAPE_TURNS`") never named a mode, and `Traversal` requires one (M1.4a). The choice decides whether M2 works at all: `balance.edges.road.costByMode` is `car: 1`, `foot: 2`, and a centre start on the default 8x6 grid is at most 7 grid steps from any border cell, so by car the nearest of three border exits is 3-5 turns, below the threshold of 6, and *every* generated map would fail M2.2 until M2.3 exhausted `LIMITS.maxMapGenerationAttempts` and threw. On foot the same map is 6-10 and passes. Foot is also the criminal's guaranteed mode in the MVP - stealing a vehicle is M7 - so it is the honest floor on how long escape takes. Blocks M2.2.
- **What "a chokepoint that matters" means.** A `bridge` or `tunnel` edge whose removal strictly increases the shortest start-to-nearest-exit cost. DESIGN.md's "at least one chokepoint (bridge or tunnel) exists that matters" admitted at least three readings (a bridge merely existing, an edge on every shortest path, a cut edge); "removal raises the cost" is cheap to compute with M1.4a's `shortestPathToAny` and is the property the design actually cares about - a chokepoint the criminal can route around costs nothing to hold.
  **The edge-kind restriction is part of the rule, not decoration.** An earlier draft of this entry dropped it, which makes the rule near-vacuous: almost every map has *some* edge on a unique shortest path, so the validator would pass everything. Restricting it to bridges and tunnels is what ties the rule to the chokepoint M2.1b builds. DESIGN.md has been reworded to state both halves.
  **Consequence, which also settles the riverless-map question below.** No generator emits `tunnel`, and M2.1b can refuse a course (`river_not_bridgeable`), so a riverless city satisfies no reading of this rule and is **invalid - regenerate, never accept**. M2.1b measured refusals at 3.5% of courses, so this costs roughly one extra attempt per 30 maps against `LIMITS.maxMapGenerationAttempts` of 64. The one case it does not absorb is a grid under 4 cells in **both** dimensions, which `MIN_BANK = 2` means can *never* carry a river: such a config refuses every attempt at the river stage, and **M2.3 must fail naming that refusal** rather than looping quietly. (This sentence said "under 4 cells across" until the pre-M2.3 review measured it: a 3x6 grid carries a river fine and is valid 23% of the time, because the river takes the axis that fits. Only 3x3 and smaller are impossible, and they never reach a validator rule.) `BALANCE.map.defaults` is 8x6, so no MVP map is affected. Blocks M2.2.
- **Footpath placement.** M2.1a emits a named fraction of surviving road edges as `footpath` instead. M1.3's note calls footpath non-blockability load-bearing ("leaving one edge kind open on foot is what stops a single well-placed block from ending the hunt"), but no task in M2 created one: M2.1a made roads, M2.1b converted some to bridges, M2.1c added no edges, so every MVP map would have been fully blockable. The fraction is a `balance.map` knob. Blocks M2.1a.

- **What capture is in the MVP.** DESIGN.md's win is both sides standing on one node, and no MVP action puts a hunter on a node, so `captured` was a `GameOutcome` variant no rule could reach - found at M3.8b-2, confirmed at M3.8c from the rules side and at M4.1 from the hunter's, and tracked as an open Inbox entry that blocked M4.3 outright, because no value in `balance.ts` can move a win rate that is structurally zero. **Decided by the maintainer at M4.2a: a criminal who walks into a roadblock it did not know about is either taken or slips past.**
  Chosen over the two alternatives on the table. Adding a hunter-placing action (patrol, stakeout) would be truer to the co-location rule but moves an item out of the M7 backlog into the MVP, which is a scope change; narrowing M4.3 to targets the MVP can vary would leave the game with no win at all. The interception rule is the smallest of the three, it is already what DESIGN.md's resolution phase describes ("roadblock hit, drone spot, slip past"), and it makes the one MVP action that measurably moves the game the one that can end it - which is also the answer to "playing is currently punished", since intel now has a move attached to it.
  **Co-location is not replaced, it is postponed**: it stays the general rule and becomes reachable with the first action that places a hunter unit. DESIGN.md's "End conditions" and "Hunter actions" both say so now. The task is **M3.11**, placed at the end of M3 because it is an MVP turn-loop rule, and M4.3 gains it as a dep.

- **Score model.** Decided at M1.3, as planned. Capture scores a base of 1200; turns taken, budget spent and casualties subtract; trust remaining and a live capture add. The invariant M3.10 and M4.3 must preserve is that the worst possible capture outscores the best possible loss, which `balance.test.ts` asserts from the constants themselves.

- **How `core`'s game functions are wired.** M3.1a's `createGame(setup, seed)` had nowhere for `balance` to enter, which M1.3 requires ("logic takes `balance: Balance` as an argument and does not import `BALANCE`"), and it spent the `create*` prefix M1.2 reserved for injectable factories. Resolution: every stateless game module follows the `createRng` template - `createXLogic(deps)` returning an object of pure functions, wired once per app at the composition root (`apps/web/src/main.tsx`, `packages/sim/src/main.ts`). So M3.1a is `createGameLogic(deps).create(setup, seed)`, M3.8a is `createTurnLogic(deps).step(...)`, M3.10 is `createScoringLogic({ balance }).score(...)`. `toHunterView` and the `make*` constructors stay bare functions: they take no deps and read no balance, which is exactly the line between the two prefixes. M5.2's store receives a wired `GameLogic`; it never calls `create*` itself. Blocks M3.1a, M3.8a, M3.10.

- **How `sim` reaches inside a sealed world.** M3.1b sealed every game function behind `SealedWorld` while telling `sim` to use `WorldState`. A brand makes the two non-assignable and `seal` was to stay core-internal, so `sim` would have held a world it could not step - and the alternative, exporting `step` over both types, is two APIs for one function. Resolution: **one signature, and `unseal` is exported.** Game functions take and return `SealedWorld` only; `seal` stays internal; `unseal(world) => WorldState` is exported and denied to `apps/web` by name in `biome.json` beside `WorldState` and `GameConfig`, with its own fixture in `tools/biome/architecture-rules.test.ts`. That is consistent with what AGENTS.md rule 4 already says sealing is - a type-level guarantee, not encryption - and gives the one workspace with no hidden-information concern a named, lint-enforced door instead of a parallel API. Most of what M4.2 aggregates (outcome, turns taken, loss reason) is visible in `HunterView` anyway; `unseal` is for the rest. Blocks M3.1b; M4.2 is the consumer.

- **Bankruptcy is not an MVP end condition.** DESIGN.md listed "budget < 0" as a loss, but M3.3 rejects an action the hunter cannot afford, so the balance floors at 0 and the condition is unreachable - M3.8c's AC ("each end condition has a test that reaches it") could not have been met. Reading it as "cannot afford the cheapest action" does not rescue it either: `balance.actions.trueBriefing.budgetCost` is 0, so something is always affordable. Resolution: **cut it**, and cut it in the code at this review rather than leaving M3.8c to discover it - DESIGN.md's loss list, `GameOutcome`'s `bankrupt` variant, `score.outcomeBase.bankrupt` and `endConditions.bankruptBelow` are all gone. Budget stays a real constraint, enforced at planning, and running dry costs the hunter their tools rather than the case. Reintroducing the loss needs an action billed *after* it is chosen (overtime, standing upkeep on a containment, federal help); M7 now names that. Blocks M3.3, M3.8c, M3.10.

- **Where slow tests live.** M2.3's property sweep over generate-until-valid, M3.6's statistical margins, M3.8c's 200-game determinism run and M4.3's batch all land in `bun run test`, which every task's definition of done runs, and none of the four knew about the others. Decided now so they are written against one convention instead of each inventing one: a slow test is named `*.slow.test.ts` and belongs to a fifth Vitest project; `bun run test` and CI keep running everything, and a new `bun run test:fast` excludes that project for the inner loop. The project and the script are created by **M2.3**, the first task that has a slow test to put in one - adding empty config now would be dead weight, and the `vitest.config.ts` shape is already settled (M0.3). The trigger is measured, not felt: when `bun run verify` passes 60 seconds, the slowest test moves. Guides M2.3, M3.6, M3.8c, M4.3.

**Open**

- None. Everything raised in review has a decision above; new questions go here with the task they block.

## Inbox

Agents add discovered out-of-scope work here.

- ~~Pre-M2.2 plan review raised ten findings; the top three were fixed.~~ **All ten are resolved.** Three landed as decisions above (how `core`'s game functions are wired, how `sim` reaches inside a sealed world, bankruptcy cut). The rest landed at the same review: captured-alive cut from the MVP, M3.1 and M3.4 each split in two, M5.7 given a bounded deliverable, M3.5's missing dep on M3.3 and M5.2's on M3.2 added, M5.3a's AC given the river back, the slow-test convention decided, and AGENTS.md's coverage row, Zustand wording and repository layout block brought in line with what the repo actually contains. Nothing from that review is outstanding.

- The devcontainer now installs Bun via `ghcr.io/devcontainers-extra/features/bun:1`, but this has only been verified by tag resolution, not by an actual container rebuild. Confirm on the next rebuild; the Bun in the current container was curl-installed to `~/.bun/bin`.
- M0.5 added `bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium` to the devcontainer's `postCreateCommand`, but it has never run: the browser in the current container was installed by hand, and `install-deps` needed `sudo`. Confirm on the same rebuild that checks the Bun feature above. (CI's browser step landed in M0.6.)
- Dependabot auto-merge only actually waits for CI if branch protection on `main` marks the CI checks as required. Needs configuring in repo settings; not expressible in a file.
- `.gitignore` still carries Chrome-extension entries (`*.crx`, `key.pem`) from the bootstrap template.
- ~~AGENTS.md's "Repository layout" block omits `tools/`, `.github/`, `.devcontainer/` and `LICENSE`, all of which exist.~~ **Resolved** at the M2.2 review. `tools/`, `.github/`, `.devcontainer/`, `vitest.config.ts`, `tsconfig.tools.json` and `playwright.config.ts` had already been added to the block by an earlier task without the item being struck; `LICENSE` was the only one still missing and is now listed.
- ~~AGENTS.md section 3 promises "maximum nesting depth of 2", but no Biome rule enforces it and `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` is not the same constraint. Either write a GritQL plugin for it or soften the wording.~~ **Resolved** at the pre-M2.3 review by softening: the rule now says in AGENTS.md that it is review-enforced and names `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` as the nearest automated proxy it is not. A GritQL plugin was considered and rejected for now - depth is cheap to see in review, and a plugin that miscounts callbacks would cost more than it caught. Reconsider if a task ever lands a function that passes complexity 10 at depth 4.
- ~~`tools/` in the working tree also contains an unrelated, untracked `fetch_agent_tools.sh` from local tooling.~~ **Resolved** at the M2.2 review: it is tracked at `tools/fetch_agent_tools.sh`, so the decision was made by committing it.
- AGENTS.md's command table should say that `bun test` (Bun's own runner) is not `bun run test` (Vitest). The two disagree on config, environment and assertion library, and the former silently half-runs the suite.
- `bun run test:fast` prints an empty coverage summary (`100% ( 0/0 )`) while `bun run test` reports the real figures. Pre-existing since M2.3 created the `slow` project, and confirmed at M2.4 to predate it rather than follow from it; the v8 provider appears to attribute nothing when the project filter is negated. Harmless to the inner loop, but it means the fast run cannot be trusted for coverage.
- Coverage is generated for `core` but has no thresholds, so it can rot silently. Consider `coverage.thresholds` once `core` has real modules (after M1.2), tuned to whatever M1 actually reaches rather than an aspirational number.
- **Four tasks are marked "split before starting" rather than split now** (pre-M2 review, confirmed in the pre-M2.1c review): ~~**M3.8b** (AP spend, then simultaneous resolution)~~ - **split before M3.8b-1 into M3.8b-1/M3.8b-2**, and M3.8c's dep narrowed to M3.8b-2 - ~~**M3.7** (event machinery, then the remaining events)~~ - **split at M3.7a into M3.7a/M3.7b**, and M3.8a's dep narrowed to M3.7a - ~~**M4.2** (batch runner, then the CLI surface)~~ - **split before M4.2a into M4.2a/M4.2b/M4.2c**, three rather than two because the Inbox separately made the `GameOutcome` deadline variant M4.2's blocker and it is a `core` change while both halves asked for are `sim` - ~~**M5.5**~~ - **split before M5.5a into M5.5a/M5.5b**, with the screen assembly given to M5.5a because selection is UI state owned above the map, and M5.6a's dep narrowed to M5.5b - and ~~**M5.6b** (two checkboxes)~~ - **split before M5.6b-1 into M5.6b-1/M5.6b-2**, with the scrubber given to M5.6b-1 and the share link plus its URL boundary to M5.6b-2, and M5.7's dep narrowed to M5.6b-2. Deliberately deferred: M3.8 was split twice because the first split happened before its dependencies existed, and each of these is four or more milestones out. The flag is in each task body, so whoever picks one up cannot miss it.
- ~~**M2.4's AC** ("`sim` can write `map-<seed>.svg`") implies argv parsing, but M4.2 owns the CLI and AGENTS.md section 5 makes CLI arguments a bounded boundary - narrow M2.4 to the SVG builder plus the bounded write helper, tested directly, and let M4.2 wire the flag.~~ **Resolved at M2.4, as proposed.** `packages/sim/src/main.ts` is still a no-op; **M4.2 wires `createMapSvgLogic`, `createArtifactWriter` and `createFileSink` there** and adds the argv bound.
- ~~The definition of done says `bun run verify` passes "with zero warnings", but it has printed two `useLiteralKeys` infos on every run since M0.4, so every task technically fails its own DoD.~~ **Resolved** in the pre-M2.1c review, together with the item below: `useLiteralKeys` is now `off` for `**/*.test.ts` in `biome.json`. The DoD stands as written and `bun run lint` prints nothing.
- ~~`LIMITS.maxMapGenerationAttempts` is still the untuned 64 from M0.2. M2.3 should tune it rather than inherit the placeholder.~~ **Resolved** at M2.3: tuned to **128** on a 3000-seed measurement (mean 5.98 attempts, max 57). 64 held over every seed measured, but left only 1.1x over the observed tail, which is a 0.8% spurious-failure chance per 1000-game M4.2 batch.
- **M2.1c places exits by foot distance from the start and never by which bank of the river they are on, which is what makes 78% of generated maps fail the chokepoint rule.** Measured at M2.2 over 500 seeds: in 76% of cities, removing *every* bridge leaves the shortest escape cost unchanged, because the nearest exit is on the start's own side of the water. The validator is right and the rule is as decided; the generator is what makes valid maps scarce. Biasing at least one exit to the far bank - the way M2.1c already biases for distance - would raise the yield a lot and make the river matter to the hunt rather than to the drawing. Out of scope for M2.2, and M2.3 works either way at 16%.
- `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` (max 10) will likely trip on M2.1b's river cut and M2.2's multi-rule validator. The M1.4b precedent is to split the function, not raise the ceiling and not disable per file.
- ~~`bun run lint` exits 0 but prints two `lint/complexity/useLiteralKeys` infos on `tools/biome/architecture-rules.test.ts`. Biome wants `counted.changed`; `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature` in `tsconfig.base.json` wants `counted["changed"]`.~~ **Resolved:** `useLiteralKeys` is off for `**/*.test.ts`. The tsc rule is type-aware and load-bearing, Biome's is style, so style yielded, scoped to the files where the two can disagree.
- ~~M2.1b's `MIN_BANK = 2` means a grid under 4 cells across gets no river at all, and M2.2 needs to agree on whether a riverless city is valid or a regeneration.~~ **Resolved** in the pre-M2.1c review, by the chokepoint decision above: a riverless city has no bridge and no tunnel, so it fails the chokepoint rule and is regenerated. A grid under 4 cells in **both** dimensions therefore has no valid map at all, and M2.3 must fail naming M2.1b's `river_not_bridgeable` refusal rather than looping to its cap. (Corrected at the pre-M2.3 review from "under 4 cells across", and from "the violation" - the refusal comes from the generator stage, so the validator never runs.)
- **Cross-cutting audit pass (2026-09-19), run outside any task at the maintainer's request:** re-checked the seven architecture rules against what actually enforces them, swept stale comments, and cut function length and complexity. Changes: `balance.map.escapeMode` now holds the travel mode every generated distance is measured in, replacing four copies of a private `"foot"` constant in `validator.ts`, `districts.ts`, `topology.ts` and `river.ts` (`game.ts`'s `START_MODE` stays separate on purpose - see its comment); `topology.ts`'s two edge builders became one `GRID_AXES` table plus `axisEdges`, with horizontal-before-vertical called out as load-bearing for determinism; `districts.ts` grew `claimAround` and `labelNodes` to bring `growRegions` to nesting depth 2 and shorten `label`; `river.ts` grew a `Banks` type and a `notBridgeable` constructor, removing the `crosses` closure that a record was about to capture; `game.ts` grew `startingWorld`. Rule 5 is now enforced in both remaining directions - `packages/sim` may not import `@manhunter/web` or any UI package, `apps/web` may not import `@manhunter/sim` - and `apps/web`'s restricted-import list gained the hidden-state constructors (`makeWorldState`, `makeCriminalState`, `makeReport`, `EMPTY_CRIMINAL_KNOWLEDGE`), each with a fixture in `tools/biome/architecture-rules.test.ts`. Highest cognitive complexity in the repo is now 9 (`graph.ts`'s `search`), against a ceiling of 10.
- **`Balance` is `typeof BALANCE` over an `as const` table, so every scalar in it is a literal type, and M4.3's sweep cannot vary one** (found at M3.4a, where a `trustMin === trustMax` test would not compile). `balance.districts` escapes this because it is a separately annotated constant, which is why a test can build a district with a different witness density but not a hunter with a different trust range. M4.3 needs either a widened `MutableBalance` view or explicit annotations on the sub-tables it sweeps; picking one is that task's, and the choice decides whether guards like a divide-by-zero check on the trust range become reachable (and therefore required) or stay impossible.

- ~~**`CriminalState` has no position history, so `balance.actions.pullCctv.lookbackTurns` cannot be honoured** (found at M3.4a). CCTV is meant to be footage *of the past*; today the pull reads the criminal's node at the turn it is taken, because that is the only node the state holds. What it needs is a bounded trail of recent positions in `WorldState`, capped by a named constant in `limits.ts` at least as long as the largest `lookbackTurns` a balance can set, appended by whatever moves the criminal. **Owned by M3.8b**, which is the task that designs the movement state; M3.4a's `PULL_CCTV.delay` and its tests are the only things that change when it lands.~~ **Resolved at M3.8b-2, as specified.** `CriminalState.trail` is `readonly NodeId[]`, newest first, one entry per turn rather than one per move - a criminal that hid or was turned back still spent a turn somewhere - appended in the single place that resolves the criminal's action. `LIMITS.maxCriminalTrail = 8`, four times the shipped `lookbackTurns` of 2, and `balance.test.ts` now asserts `lookbackTurns <= LIMITS.maxCriminalTrail` so M4.3's sweep cannot ask for footage that was never kept. `PULL_CCTV.delay` and `actions.test.ts` were the only other things touched, as this entry said they would be; footage of a node the criminal has left carries `UNKNOWN_TRAVEL_MODE`, because a trail records where somebody was and not how they were travelling.

- ~~**A canvass report can never be announced by the event table as the two tasks are currently specced** (found at M3.8a). `CANVASS_DELAY` is 0 and the events phase runs before planning, so a canvass report lands after the only turn on which M3.7a's `receivedAtTurn === world.clock.turn` filter would match it.~~ **Resolved at M3.7b by accepting it, with the reasoning and the regression test recorded in that task's note.** DESIGN.md files `eyewitness` and `prank_call` under Information events, which are the city volunteering something; a canvass answer is intel the hunter asked for and already holds, so the phase position selects the right set and no code changed. Neither of the other two options (move the announcement after planning, give canvass a delay) was taken, so `actions.ts` and M3.8b's planning phase are unaffected.

- **Two architecture rules are still review-only, with no test that would catch a regression.** Rule 3 (state is plain serializable data: no classes, Maps, Sets or functions in state) has a fast-check round-trip in `limits.test.ts` but nothing that walks an actual `WorldState`; the honest place for that is M3.8c, which is already the task that owes the byte-identical-final-state test. Rule 6 (data-driven content: a new action or event is a table entry, not a branch in the turn loop) has nothing enforcing it and probably cannot have, but M3.7 and M3.8b are where it would first be broken. Neither is worth a task on its own; both are worth naming in the acceptance criteria of the task that lands the state they guard. **Done for rule 3 at M3.8c:** `world.test.ts` walks a real played world recursively, and a planted `Map`, `Set`, `RegExp`, function and `undefined` prove the walk can fail. **Done for rule 6 on the event side:** M3.7a's acceptance criteria name it, and M3.7b's ask the next task to report it if adding an event needs anything but a new entry. ~~M3.8b still owes the same for actions~~ - **done at M3.8b-1**, where the planning phase reads no `action.kind` and two tests say so, one of them a `Record<HunterActionKind, HunterAction>` that stops compiling when a kind is added. ~~M3.8c still owes rule 3's walk over a real `WorldState`.~~ **Both halves are now discharged; only rule 6's "a new action or event is a table entry" remains review-only, and probably has to.**

- ~~**The MVP has no reachable capture, so the win condition cannot be tested or balanced**~~ **Answered by the maintainer at M4.2a, and the answer is the third option this entry did not list: neither a restatement of co-location nor a new hunter-placing action, but the roadblock itself.** A criminal who walks into a checkpoint it did not know about is taken or slips past, which is what DESIGN.md's resolution phase already described. The decision and why it was preferred to the other two are recorded under "What capture is in the MVP" above; the work is **M3.11**, and DESIGN.md is reworded. This entry stays open in substance until M3.11 lands, and it is M4.3's dep rather than its blocker now. The original finding follows. (found at M3.8b-2). DESIGN.md's win is both sides standing on one node, but no shipped action puts a hunter unit on a node: a roadblock targets an edge, and canvass, CCTV and briefing target a district or nothing at all. Patrol, plainclothes, stakeout, exit checkpoint, K9 and drone are all in the M7 backlog. The co-location rule therefore has one side missing, and M3.8b-2 deliberately wrote no capture branch rather than one no test could reach (its note, and M3.8b-1's precedent on `hunt_over`). **Owned by M3.8c**, whose AC is "each end condition has a test that reaches it" and which cannot meet it for capture as things stand: either the rule is stated over something the MVP can produce, or the MVP needs one action that places the hunter, which is a scope change and belongs in this Inbox before it belongs in a task. **Blocks M4.3**, whose win-rate targets mean nothing while no bot can win. **Confirmed at M3.8c**, which implemented the other three conditions and left `captured` as the one `GameOutcome` variant with no rule; the table it lives in is built so that landing it is one entry and no branch. **Confirmed again at M4.1 from the hunter's side**: `heatmap-chaser` spends its whole action point allowance every turn locating the criminal and ends 200 of 200 hunts identically to a hunter who does nothing (mean 4.65 turns against 4.66). Knowing where the criminal is has no move attached to it.

- ~~**The MVP hunt is over in about five turns, and the hunter cannot lengthen it** (measured at M3.8c). Over seeds 1-200 with a hunter who does nothing, 200 of 200 hunts end `escaped` at a mean of 4.66 turns, and none reach the 24-turn deadline. The amateur walks to the nearest exit and the only MVP action that can stand in its way is a roadblock, which costs 50 of a 1000 budget and 3 of 70 trust for 4 turns on one edge of a 48-node map. So a "hunt" in the shipped game is four or five decisions long, which is not what DESIGN.md describes, and it is the same missing piece as the capture entry above: nothing places the hunter. **Blocks M4.3** together with that entry - a difficulty sweep over a game whose outcome is fixed before the player's third turn has nothing to tune. Worth measuring against a bot that spends its budget (M4.1) before deciding whether the answer is balance, a new action, or exits that are further away. **Measured at M4.1 over the same seeds 1-200.** Of the three scripted bots, only `greedy-roadblock` lengthens the hunt: mean 17.23 turns against the do-nothing baseline's 4.66, 110 of 200 escapes, 897 of a 1000 budget spent. `heatmap-chaser` spends 306 and changes nothing at all (200 of 200 escaped, mean 4.65), because intel has nothing to feed. So the answer is not balance alone: the one MVP action that can stand in the criminal's way already works, and what a hunter who buys information still cannot do is act on it. **That last sentence is what the M4.2a capture decision acts on**: M3.11 attaches a move to knowing where the criminal is going, by making the roadblock able to end the hunt. Still open in substance - whether it is enough to make a hunt last is a measurement **M4.3** owes, not a claim this entry can make in advance.~~ **Closed at M4.3.** With `slipPastChance` at 0.7 a worked hunt runs a mean 8.56 turns against an unworked 4.65, and 46 of 400 run the full 24-turn deadline. The cause was the one named here - there was nothing the hunter could do to the criminal's turn - and M3.11's checkpoint is what answered it. Regression-tested by `runs a worked hunt well past the five turns an unworked one takes`.

- ~~**`GameOutcome` has no "the deadline ran out" variant**, so a hunt that survives to `config.maxTurns` stays `in_progress` for ever (found at M3.8c, deliberately not invented there: the task's list was capture, escape, trust collapse and casualties, and DESIGN.md's loss list does not name the clock either). It is unreachable today because every hunt ends far sooner, which is why M3.8c left it alone. It stops being unreachable the moment the entry above is answered, and two tasks need it before then: **M4.2** aggregates outcomes by kind and would report a timed-out hunt as still running, and **M5.6a**'s end screen has no copy to show for one. `turnsRemaining` already floors at 0 (M3.2's note), so the UI half is ready. Landing it is a `GameOutcome` variant, a `balance.score.outcomeBase` row, and one entry in `END_CONDITIONS` reading `world.clock.turn >= world.config.maxTurns`. **No longer unreachable, and it did not need the entry above to be answered** (measured at M4.1): `greedy-roadblock` leaves 75 of 200 hunts still `in_progress` at the 24-turn deadline, and `random` leaves 2. M4.2 aggregates by outcome kind and will report 37% of a greedy batch as still running, so this is now that task's blocker rather than a latent one.~~ **Resolved at M4.2a.** `timed_out` is declared last in `GameOutcome` and checked last in `END_CONDITIONS`, `outcomeBase.timed_out` is 150 (a stalemate, tuned by M4.3), and a hunt that reaches its own `config.maxTurns` now ends rather than running for ever. M5.6a's end screen has a sixth kind to write copy for; the reasoning for where the variant sits is in that task's note.

- **Two lines of the "How `core`'s game functions are wired" and "Score model" decisions are stale** (found at M3.10, deliberately not edited there - the decisions themselves are the maintainer's). The wiring decision still names `createScoringLogic({ balance }).score(...)`, which M3.6b departed from for `step`, M3.8a followed and M3.10 has now followed too: `balance` is an argument on every call, never captured by a factory, so three of the three shipped units contradict the text. The score decision still says "trust remaining and a live capture add", but captured-alive was cut at the pre-M2.2 review by the decision two entries above it and `capturedAliveBonus` is gone from `balance.ts`. Both are one-sentence corrections; nothing in the code depends on them.

- **Every losing hunt that ends in trust collapse scores exactly `minimumScore`, so the score cannot rank them** (found at M3.10). Only `captured` has a non-zero `outcomeBase`, and trust collapse means `trust` is 0 by definition, so a collapsed hunt has no bonus at all and the three penalties floor it at the minimum however well it went beforehand. Casualty losses are nearly the same. **M4.3 is the consumer**: a difficulty sweep that reads mean score gets a pile of identical zeros from the loss side and is tuning against capture rate alone, which is also the Inbox entry above about no reachable capture. Fixing it is a balance question, not a scoring one - a small base for each loss kind, or a floor low enough to let losses differ - and it wants measuring against a bot that plays (M4.1) before anyone picks. **Measured at M4.1**: over seeds 1-200 the mean score is 55.0 for a hunter who does nothing, 13.9 for `heatmap-chaser`, 2.6 for `random` and exactly 0.0 for `greedy-roadblock` - every one of whose 200 hunts floors at the minimum. The penalties are the whole signal on the loss side, so the score today ranks bots by how little they spent. **Sharpened at M4.3, still open, and now wider than trust collapse.** Every one of `greedy_roadblock`'s 159 non-captures in 400 hunts scores exactly `minimumScore` - 96 escapes, 46 stalemates and 17 casualty losses, one distinct value between the three. **Not fixable from `outcomeBase`**: a stalemate always pays the full `maxTurns * turnPenalty` of 360 against a row `balance.test.ts`'s capture invariant caps at 289, which is why M4.3 left `outcomeBase.timed_out` at 150 - no admissible value moves a single total. Lowering the floor does rank them (at `minimumScore = -2000`: 71 distinct escape scores, 16 casualty, 8 stalemate) but **inverts a value**, stalemates averaging -437 against escapes' -290, so containing the criminal for 24 turns would score worse than letting them walk out on turn 5. The floor and the turn penalty have to move together; that is a scoring-model decision and M4.3 deliberately did not take it.

- ~~Nothing keeps `core`'s `LIMITS.maxReplayStringLength` and `apps/web/src/limits.ts`'s copy of it in step (found at M3.9, where both were set to 8192 deliberately).~~ **Resolved at M5.6b-2.** `core` now exports `LIMITS`; `apps/web/src/limits.ts` reads `CORE_LIMITS.maxReplayStringLength` directly rather than carrying a copy, so the two cannot drift apart.

- ~~**A replay may record more turns than its own `setup.maxTurns`, and today that is correct** (found at M3.9). `replayRefusalIn` bounds the recorded turn list by `LIMITS.maxGameTurns` and not by the deadline, because `step` has no deadline rule - a hunt genuinely can run past `maxTurns`, which is the Inbox entry above about `GameOutcome` having no timed-out variant. **When that variant lands, the same task should add one line to `replayRefusalIn`**: more recorded turns than `setup.maxTurns` is then a replay of a hunt that could not have happened, and refusing it at the boundary is cheaper than playing 240 turns to discover the last 200 were `hunt_over`.~~ **Resolved at M4.2a.** `replayRefusalIn`'s third guard is now `replay.actions.length > replay.setup.maxTurns`, and the `LIMITS.maxGameTurns` bound it replaced was removed rather than kept: `deadline_too_long` runs first and already caps `setup.maxTurns` at the global limit, so the old guard could never fire after it. `playback.ts` keeps its own `maxGameTurns` guard, which bounds a different thing. `REPLAY_VERSION` went to 2 in the same task; the reasoning is in its note.

- **Whether the hunter is ever told that a roadblock was hit** (found at M3.8b-2, considered and not added). It would be the strongest negative-evidence signal in the game and the natural input for M3.6b's heatmap, and it is also precisely the leak architecture rule 4 exists to stop: "your checkpoint was hit" names the edge the criminal is standing on. DESIGN.md's report list does not include it and neither does its event list, so M3.8b-2 added nothing rather than guess. Decide it as a design question before any task implements it. If it lands it should be a `Report` with a source and a reliability, like everything else the hunter learns, rather than a `GameEvent` the view forwards intact.

- ~~**A hunter who does nothing outscores every bot that plays** (measured at M4.1, seeds 1-200 on the default grid). Mean score is 55.0 passive, 13.9 for `heatmap-chaser`, 2.6 for `random`, 0.0 for `greedy-roadblock`. Every hunt ends the same way whatever the hunter does, so the only thing the score can see is the budget and trust spent getting there, and the strictly dominant strategy is to spend nothing. That is a sharper statement of the two entries above than either makes alone: it is not that losses rank badly, it is that **playing is currently punished**, and a difficulty sweep (M4.3) run against mean score today would tune the game towards inaction. Not fixable from the scoring side - M3.10's components are each defensible - so it belongs with whichever task answers the capture entry. **That is M3.11**, which gives spending a way to pay for itself: a roadblock that can end the hunt is worth its 50 budget and 3 trust in a way one that can only delay is not. **The measurement above is already stale on two counts and must be redone at M4.3** - M4.2a's `timed_out` moves the 75 greedy hunts that were scoring 0 as `in_progress` onto a base of 150 plus surviving trust, and M3.11 moves the stream, so every seed plays a different hunt.~~ **Closed at M4.3, as stated.** `greedy_roadblock` now averages 695.2 against doing nothing's 51.8 - 13.4 times - and `random` 87.3, both over the idle baseline, pinned by `pays a hunter who works the case a multiple of one who sits on it`. A residual remains and belongs to the bot rather than to the balance: `heatmap_chaser` still scores below doing nothing, which is its own entry below.

- ~~Nothing keeps `apps/web`'s `LIMITS.maxRecordedTurns` and `core`'s `LIMITS.maxGameTurns` in step (found at M5.2, where both are 240).~~ **The duplication is resolved at M5.6b-2**: `apps/web/src/limits.ts` reads `CORE_LIMITS.maxGameTurns` directly for `maxRecordedTurns`. **Still open**: whether the store keeps recording actions past a hunt's own deadline before `step` reports `timed_out` was not addressed by this fix (M5.6b-2's scope was the constant, not the store's timing), and the stale comments this entry named in `apps/web/src/limits.ts`/`gamestore.test.ts` were not revisited.

- **AGENTS.md's command table names a `--profile` flag that cannot exist** (found at M4.2, before M4.2a). It reads `bun run sim -- --games 1000 --profile amateur --seed 1`, but `GameSetup` carries a `difficulty` and the criminal profile is derived from the seed and hidden - the config/setup split decided at M3.1a, and DESIGN.md's "the player never picks a profile". The table predates that split. M4.2c's flag is `--difficulty`; **correcting the line in AGENTS.md is the maintainer's**, since that file is the instruction set rather than a working document. Today the two would behave identically anyway: every `balance.criminal.pools` row names `amateur` alone until M7.

- **Nothing distinguishes a checkpoint the criminal slipped past** (found at M3.11, out of its scope). The resolution phase now draws on every collision, but a criminal who gets through leaves the hunter exactly what a criminal who never came leaves: a block still standing. A `checkpoint_missed` report would be the natural home for it and would give M3.6b's heatmap its strongest positive evidence in the game - the criminal was on that edge, this turn. It is the same design question as the roadblock-was-hit entry above and should be settled with it, as a `Report` with a source and a reliability rather than a `GameEvent` the view forwards intact; the difference is that a slip-past is a near miss the hunter arguably would hear about, where a capture ends the hunt anyway.

- ~~**`LIMITS.maxOutputFileBytes` is justified by a number that turned out to be wrong** (found at M4.2b). Its comment in `packages/sim/src/limits.ts` says the 16 MiB bound is "really sized for M4.2's batch report over up to `maxGamesPerRun` games". M4.2b measured that report at about 1.6 KB whatever the batch size, so the justification is now wrong even though the number is still safe. Whoever owns the next artefact boundary (M4.2c) should rewrite the comment around the real largest artefact, which is M2.4's map SVG at 14.5 KB.~~ **Closed at M4.2c**, which rewrote the comment around the real largest artefact - M2.4's map SVG, measured at 14,796 bytes on a real run. The bound itself did not move.

- **`.gitignore` does not cover `.vitest/`** (found at M4.2b, hit again at M4.2c, and it breaks `bun run verify`). Running Vitest with `--reporter json` writes `.vitest/json/output.json`, which Biome then checks and rejects for formatting, so `bun run verify` fails on a file nobody wrote. It was deleted by hand here. `.vitest/` belongs beside `coverage/` in `.gitignore`.

- **`--out DIR` does not create the directory it writes into** (found at M4.2c, deliberately not decided there). A missing one surfaces as `did not write <path>: ENOENT ...` and a non-zero exit, which is honest but means a script has to `mkdir -p` first. Creating it is a new I/O decision - recursive or not, and what bounds a path - and belongs to whoever next owns the artefact boundary rather than to a CLI task.

- **`vitest.config.ts` reports no coverage at all for `packages/sim` and `apps/web`** (found at M4.2c). `coverage.include` is `["packages/core/src/**/*.ts"]`, and a `--coverage.include` override on the command line does not take when a project filter is in play, so any project-filtered run quietly reports "Unknown%" - measuring `sim` at M4.2c needed a throwaway config at the repo root merged over the base one. Every task since M0 has been asked to report coverage on the files it touched, so two of the three workspaces have been meeting that bar by hand. Either widen the include to every workspace or document the override. Re-confirmed at M5.5a, where it now matters more: `apps/web/src` has grown from 5 files to 20 and every web component to date is unmeasured. Worth deciding before M5.7 adds more.

- **`heatmap_chaser` captures nothing at any setting of the balance** (measured at M4.3, and the evidence points at the bot). Across a 400-hunt sweep it took 0 hunts at every slip chance from 0.5 to 0.85, ran a mean 4.64 turns against the do-nothing baseline's 4.65, and scored a mean 14.2 against doing nothing's 51.8 - so buying intel is currently strictly worse than sitting still, and it is the only one of the three bots the capture rule does not move at all. It spends its allowance locating the criminal and never queues the roadblock that is the only way a hunt can be won. M4.1's `packages/sim/src/bots/**` was out of scope for M4.3, so this is a bots entry rather than a fix - and until it is settled, `heatmap_chaser` measures nothing a balance target can use.

- ~~**The test suite had no declared time bound, and CI is about four times slower than a developer machine** (found by the maintainer on the first CI run of the M3.11-M4.3 branch).~~ **Fixed outside any task, at the maintainer's request.** `game.test.ts` and `generate.slow.test.ts` both timed out on CI at Vitest's undeclared 5000ms default while passing locally - neither hangs and neither is pathological; neither had ever been told how long it had. `turn.slow.test.ts` had, in its own `DETERMINISM_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000`, and it is the one heavy test that survived. The fix reuses that number as `TEST_TIMEOUT_MS` on **every** project in `vitest.config.ts`, set per project because `projects` do not inherit root `test` options - a root-level `testTimeout` would read as bounded and enforce nothing. Guarded by a new `tools/vitest/suite-bounds.test.ts`, written first and confirmed failing on all five projects before the config moved, which fails if a project is added without a bound. **A wider rule was drafted and rejected**: requiring a named `numRuns` on every `test.prop` flagged some thirty cheap property tests in `rng`, `time`, `view` and `world` that were never implicated, and rewriting them would have been churn without a cause.

- ~~The map `<svg>` has a computed `viewBox` but no width, height or pan/zoom (found at M5.3a). Whichever task lays out the dispatch screen sizes its container; M5.7 should decide whether a large city needs zoom.~~ **Decided at M5.7: no zoom for MVP.** The computed `viewBox` already sizes the container correctly, and the "no layout overflow" AC passed against it unmodified at both target viewports. New evidence from a future, larger city would motivate a new task, not reopen this one.

- ~~**No focus ring is drawn on a keyboard-focused node or edge** (found at M5.3a). The map is reachable by Tab but not visibly so. **Still open after M5.7**, which looked at it and declined: it needs a `:focus-visible` CSS selector, which has no one-time DOM-property equivalent the way the dark background did, and both real fixes (an injected `<style>` element at the composition root, or interaction-state logic in `maprenderer.tsx`) read as past a visual pass's "reads tokens instead of literals" scope into new behaviour. Worth a small follow-up task.~~ **Resolved at M6.5**: each node and edge carries a hidden `mh-map-focus` outline that `.mh-map-target:focus-visible` reveals.

- **`DEFAULT_MAP_THEME` (`apps/web`) and `DEFAULT_SVG_THEME` (`packages/sim/src/svg.ts`) are two copies of one palette that can drift** (found at M5.3a). **Resolved as far as possible at M5.7**: `apps/web`'s copy is now `theme.ts`'s `MAP_THEME`, the single source for `web`. `packages/sim/src/svg.ts`'s `DEFAULT_SVG_THEME` is untouched - architecture rule 5 forbids sharing between `web` and `sim` in either direction, and `core` holds no presentation strings (rule 1), so there is no clean fix left within the architecture as it stands. If the two palettes are ever meant to be pixel-identical, the only further option is relaxing an architecture rule, which is a maintainer decision, not a task.

- **The composition-root wiring block is duplicated across three web test files** (found at M5.4): `mappanel.test.tsx`, `reportfeedpanel.test.tsx` and `meterspanel.test.tsx` each rebuild the `createGameLogic`/`createTurnLogic` graph, because a connected-panel test needs a real `SealedWorld` and `seal` is core-internal. M5.5 will make it four. Extract a shared test fixture (e.g. `apps/web/src/wiring.testfixture.ts`, outside the Vitest `include` glob) when it does.

- ~~**`HunterView.events` is rendered by nothing** (found at M5.4). The `report_arrived` projection exists so the feed could learn a report landed, but M5.4 derives "new" from `receivedAtTurn` instead, so the world events (`civilian_hurt`, nightfall, weather) have no home on the dispatch screen at all. Decide whether M5.5's screen or a second event strip surfaces them.~~ **Resolved at M6.5 for `civilian_hurt`**, the one event that names a node: it is drawn as a located marker in `map-incidents`. `nightfall` and `rush_hour` still have no surface, which is tracked as its own item below.

- **Native `<meter>` styling is vendor-prefixed** (`::-webkit-meter-bar`, `::-moz-meter-bar`) with no standard pseudo-element (found at M5.4). **Closed at M5.7, both options exhausted; superseded at M6.8, which styles the native `<meter>` from the stylesheet with `appearance: none` and the vendor pseudo-elements.** `accentColor: PALETTE.accent` computes correctly (verified against a real Chromium render) but Chromium still paints its own green/yellow/red heuristic over the value bar regardless - a genuine Chromium limitation, not a CSS mistake. The Inbox's own fallback, a `role="meter"` styled `<div>`, is rejected outright by Biome's `useSemanticElements` ("Replace with `<meter>`"), and no `biome-ignore` precedent exists in this repo for a cosmetic case. Landed on the native `<meter>` with `accentColor` set; nothing further to do unless someone revisits `useSemanticElements`'s applicability here, a Biome-config decision above a task's pay grade.

- **`LIMITS.maxReportsInFeed` (500) has no stated relation to what a hunt can produce** (found at M5.4): a 24-turn MVP hunt produces well under a tenth of it. It is a defensive bound only, tied to nothing in `core`. **Revisited at M5.6b-2**, which settled the sibling `maxRecordedTurns`/`maxQueuedActions`/`maxReplayStringLength` entries by importing a `core` constant, but found no `core.LIMITS` field this one pairs with - it is `core`-agnostic by its own description above, not merely undeclared. Stays open as an intentionally independent literal unless someone proposes a real relation to tie it to.

- ~~**The heatmap has no accessible equivalent** (found at M5.3b). The heat blobs are unlabelled SVG shapes with no role and no text, so a screen reader user gets the node markers but not the hunter's belief. Biome's `noAriaHiddenOnFocusable` rejects `aria-hidden` on the `<g>`, so the honest fix is a text summary (the top-N suspected nodes in the report column, or belief folded into each node marker's `aria-label`) rather than an attribute. Plausibly M5.7 or a new accessibility task.~~ **Closed at M6.10** by `heatmapsummary.ts`: a bounded, visually hidden list of the top suspected districts and the pinned reports, mounted before the map.

- **`positionIndex` is written twice** (found at M5.3b): `maprenderer.tsx` has one and `beliefoverlay.tsx` has an identical `positionsOf`. Six lines, duplicated deliberately rather than editing M5.3a's file mid-task. Worth a shared `nodes.ts` helper the first time a third overlay needs it, which M5.6b's criminal path will.

- **Nothing enforces that `wiring.testfixture.ts` stays outside the Vitest include glob** (found at M5.5a). It is excluded only because the glob is `*.test.ts(x)` and the file is named `.testfixture.ts`; a rename would silently turn it into a zero-test suite. Vitest fails on a file with no tests, so it would be caught, but confusingly. Could be pinned by an explicit `exclude` entry. Note that `gamestore.test.ts` and `storecontext.test.tsx` deliberately still wire their own deps - the first uses a hand-written fake `TurnLogic`, which is the point of that test - so nobody should "finish" the refactor by converting them.

- ~~The new-hunt form is unstyled and has no keyboard-submit path (found at M5.5a): no `<form onSubmit>`, and Start is a `type="button"`.~~ **Resolved at M5.7.** Real `<form onSubmit>`, `type="submit"` on the Start button, no new logic and no layout change.

- **`LIMITS.maxSeedInputLength` and `core`'s RNG seed range agree only by comment** (found at M5.5a). **Revisited at M5.6b-2** alongside the entries it settled: unlike those, the RNG's seed range is an implicit 32-bit bound, not a named `core.LIMITS` constant, so there was nothing to import. Stays open until either the RNG exports a real constant (e.g. `MAX_SEED`) to agree with, or this is accepted as an intentionally independent literal.

- ~~`apps/web`'s `LIMITS.maxQueuedActions` and `core`'s agree only by comment (found at M5.5b, both 32).~~ **Resolved at M5.6b-2.** `apps/web/src/limits.ts` reads `CORE_LIMITS.maxQueuedActions` directly, alongside `maxReplayStringLength` and `maxRecordedTurns`.

- **Nothing tells the dispatch screen the hunt is over except a refusal** (found at M5.5b). Playing a hunt to its end leaves `end-turn` live, and pressing it produces a `hunt_over` refusal that the player reads as an error rather than as the game finishing. The right fix is the end screen swapping in on `view.outcome`, so **owned by M5.6a**; M5.5b deliberately left the refusal surface as the only signal rather than half-building an end state.

- ~~**The end screen has no way back to a new hunt** (found at M5.6a, deliberately not built there - the AC is "outcome and score breakdown", nothing more). `GameStoreState` has no reset/restart dispatch and `state.hunt` never returns to `null` once a hunt starts, so once the board swaps to `EndScreenPanel` the only way to play again today is to reload the page. Worth a `start`-shaped action (or reusing `start`, which already refuses/replaces cleanly) plus a button on the end screen; owned by whichever of M5.6b or M5.7 wants to close the loop, since neither's AC currently asks for it either.~~ **Resolved** at M6.9: `restart` dispatch plus a New hunt button on the debrief.

- **The replay screen shows the hunt's final belief heatmap for every scrubbed turn, not the belief as it stood on that turn** (found at M5.6b-1, deliberate, not a bug). Each `RevealFrame.view` carries a full per-turn `HunterView` including that turn's own `belief`, so a richer replay could scrub the heatmap too, not just the criminal's marker - `ReplayScreen` would swap `BeliefOverlayPanel` (which reads the live store view) for a `BeliefOverlay` fed `frames[turn].view.belief` directly. Not done here because M5.3b's own note pinned the composition to `overlay={<><BeliefOverlayPanel /><CriminalPath ... /></>}` specifically, and re-deciding that composition was out of scope. Worth a look at M5.7 or a future polish task.
- ~~**No restart/new-hunt affordance was added to the replay screen either** (found at M5.6b-1, the same gap M5.6a's Inbox already named for the end screen). Still owned by whichever of M5.6b-2 or M5.7 closes that loop.~~ **Resolved** at M6.9: the replay is part of the debrief, which carries the restart button.

- **A share link for an in-progress hunt resumes play rather than showing a read-only replay** (found at M5.6b-2, deliberate, not a bug). `loadShared` reuses the exact `Hunt` shape a played-out hunt gets, and nothing distinguishes "loaded from a link" from "held in memory" once `state.hunt` is populated, so a link shared mid-hunt hands the loader a normal playable `in_progress` hunt with `End Turn` live, rather than a frozen replay. Worth a line in DESIGN.md's "After-action replay" section if the maintainer wants this behavior documented rather than left as an emergent property of reusing `Hunt`.
- ~~**No copy-to-clipboard affordance on the share link** (found at M5.6b-2). `ShareLink` renders the URL as a clickable `<a>` with its full text visible, which satisfies "round-trips through the URL," but `navigator.clipboard.writeText` was left out as UI polish for M5.7's visual pass rather than a new I/O boundary this task needed to open.~~ **Resolved** at M6.9: `clipboard.ts` (bounded, failure-tolerant, injected) and the `share-link-copy` button.

- ~~**No restart/new-hunt affordance exists anywhere in the UI** (found at M5.6a for the end screen, M5.6b-1 for the replay screen, both deferred to "M5.6b-2 or M5.7"; M5.6b-2 did not take it; M5.7's own scope statement excludes it - a new component plus a `GameStoreState` reset dispatch plus a layout decision). This is the last standing gap before M5/the MVP is fully closed out. It is small (one store action reusing `start`'s existing refuse/replace semantics, plus one button on each of `EndScreenPanel` and `ReplayScreen`) but is a real UI decision - button placement, whether it clears the share-link panel too - that a visual-pass task correctly should not absorb. **Recommend a new task** (an M5.8 or an M7 backlog line) to close it.~~ **Resolved** at M6.9: `restart` dispatch, with a button on the debrief and on the briefing.

- ~~**`maprenderer.tsx`'s private `boundsOf` and `districtcells.ts`'s `paddedBoundsOf` compute the same padded extent**~~ Resolved at M6.4: the `viewBox`, the plate and the cells all read `paddedBoundsOf`, and `boundsOf` is deleted.
- ~~**The debrief's replay map is still an unsized `<svg>`** (found at M6.2). Inside `ScreenFrame` it fills the column width and paints taller than the viewport (about 1248x873 at 1280x800), so the debrief scrolls inside its column to reach the scrubber and share link. No page overflow, so M6.2's AC holds, but M6.9's debrief layout should give it a sized container the way `.mh-frame__map` does for the board.~~ **Resolved** at M6.9: `.mh-replay__map` sizes it (925x512 at 1280x800, 1488x813 at 1920x1080).
- **A revealed dossier cannot name the criminal's profile** (found at M6.7). `RevealFrame` carries only `turn`, `view` and `criminalNodeId`, so the debrief can show the seeded portrait but not whether it was an amateur or a professional. Showing it needs `core` to expose the profile post-game (a `RevealFrame`-side field or a playback result member), which M6's "`core` is untouched" constraint rules out. Decide whether the debrief wants it, and in which milestone.
- **SVG `id`s in `MapRenderer` are document-global** (found at M6.4). `road-glow` and `map-block-<type>` are fixed strings, so two maps mounted on one page share them. Today that is harmless, because any two mounted maps use the same theme and the definitions are identical. A second map with a different theme would silently take the first map's hatches. If the debrief and the board ever render together with different themes, derive the ids from `useId()`.
- **The river is not dimmed at night** (found at M6.4, deliberate). The wash has to sit before `map-river` because of the pinned order, so the water stays at day brightness. If playtesting finds that jarring, the channel could take its own night tone from `DAYLIGHT_WASH` without moving any layer.
- **A dimmed target is still accepted when the armed action cannot really use it** (found at M6.5). With a roadblock armed, a footpath is dimmed, but `DispatchScreen.select` narrows by `targetKindOf` alone, so clicking the footpath still selects it. `core` then refuses the dispatch with `edge_not_blockable`. M6.5 kept `select` as it was, as its constraints required. If `select` should also consult `edgeSelectable`, that is a behaviour change to the screen's narrowing invariant and should be its own task.
- **An already-blocked edge is not dimmed for a roadblock** (found at M6.5). `core` refuses a second roadblock on it (`edge_already_blocked`), but `selectableOf` reads only `balance.edges[kind].blockable`, not the standing containments. Dimming those edges too would need the containments passed alongside.
- **`nightfall` and `rush_hour` events still have no surface** (found at M6.5, splitting the rest of M5.4's item). They name no node, so they do not belong on the map. The wash already shows night, but nothing announces rush hour. A feed row or a header-rail chip would be the natural place.
- **The belief contour recomputes the district cells** (found at M6.6). `BeliefOverlay` calls `districtCellsOf` on the same nodes and the same `plateBoundsOf` bounds that `MapRenderer`'s blocks already used, so each render clips the Voronoi cells twice. It is cheap at 48 nodes. If profiling ever shows it, compute the cells once, above both.
- **Under the uniform prior the contour outlines the whole plate** (found at M6.6, deliberate). On turn 1 every node ties for the peak, so every node is in the top tier. If playtesting finds a full-plate outline noisy, the contour could be suppressed while the tier covers every node.
- **Restart leaves a shared link's `?replay=` in the address bar** (found at M6.9). `restart` clears the store, but the URL the page was opened from is untouched, so reloading after a restart loads the shared hunt again. Clearing it means `history.replaceState`, a new browser boundary to inject at `main.tsx`; left out because M6.9's AC asks only that the share-link panel clear.
- **The debrief's legend has no key for the criminal's path** (found at M6.9). `BeliefLegend` is mounted under the replay map and keys the heat, the contour and the pins, but not the red path and marker `CriminalPathPanel` draws. Adding it means a `CRIMINAL_PATH_THEME` swatch in the legend, shown only where the path is.
- **The map's nodes and roads are one tab stop each** (found at M6.10). That is 110 on seed 1. M6.10's skip links route around them, but a roving tabindex (one stop, arrow keys inside) would be the listbox pattern done properly. That changes the pinned `tabindex="0"` per node in the map tests, so it is a deliberate test change for its own task.
- **Checkpoint-fire motion can only confirm a barrier going live** (found at M6.10). A hit or a slip-past would be the more useful thing to confirm, but that is the undecided roadblock-was-hit question above, and architecture rule 4 governs it. If that entry lands as a report, it gets report-arrival motion for free.
- **A pin whose count grows in a turn replays its arrival even if its kind did not change** (found at M6.10, deliberate). The pin is keyed by the turn its node last heard a report, so a second report at a node already pinned animates the pin again. That is correct as confirmation that something arrived there. It is listed only in case playtesting finds it noisy.
